Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa

Homage to the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Suttantapiṭaka

The Sutta Piṭaka

Aṅguttaranikāye

In the Aṅguttara Nikāya

Saṃgāyanassa pucchā vissajjanā

Questions and Answers of the Council

Pucchā – paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle [M 1.0001] āvuso dhammasaṃgāhakā mahākassapādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā paṭhamaṃ vinayapiṭakaṃ saṃgāyitvā suttantapiṭake ca dīghamajjhimasaṃyuttasaṅkhāte tayo nikāye saṃgāyitvā tadanantaraṃ kiṃ nāma pāvacanaṃ saṃgāyiṃsu.

Question: Venerable, during the first great council, the Dhamma compilers, the venerable elders, the ancient council-reciters, headed by Mahākassapa, having first recited the Vinaya Piṭaka, then in the Sutta Piṭaka recited the three Nikāyas comprising the Dīgha, Majjhima, and Saṃyutta. After that, what teaching did they recite?

Vissajjanā – paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle bhante dhammasaṃgāhakā mahākassapādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā paṭhamaṃ vinayaṃ saṃgāyitvā suttantapiṭake ca dīghamajjhimasaṃyuttasaṅkhāte tayo nikāye saṃgāyitvā tadanantaraṃ navahi ca suttasahassehi pañcahi ca suttasattehi sattapaññāsāya ca suttehi paṭimaṇḍitaṃ vīsatibhāṇavārasataparimāṇaṃ aṅguttaranikāyaṃ nāma pāvacanaṃ saṃgāyiṃsu.

Answer: Venerable sir, during the first great council, the Dhamma compilers, the venerable elders, the ancient council-reciters, headed by Mahākassapa, having first recited the Vinaya, then in the Sutta Piṭaka recited the three Nikāyas comprising the Dīgha, Majjhima, and Saṃyutta. After that, they recited the teaching called the Aṅguttara Nikāya, adorned with nine thousand five hundred and fifty-seven suttas, comprising two thousand recitation-sections.

Pucchā – aṅguttaranikāyepi [M 1.0002] āvuso ekakanipāto dukanipāto tikanipātotiādinā nipātapakaraṇaparicchedavasena ekādasavidhā. Tattha kataraṃ nipātapakaraṇaṃ saṃgāyiṃsu.

Question: Venerable, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya there are eleven kinds of nipāta-section divisions, such as the Ekaka Nipāta, Duka Nipāta, Tika Nipāta, and so on. Which nipāta-section did they recite?

Vissajjanā – aṅguttaranikāye bhante ekādasasu nipātapakaraṇaparicchedesu ekakanipātaṃ paṭhamaṃ saṃgāyiṃsu.

Answer: Venerable, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, among the eleven nipāta-section divisions, they first recited the Ekaka Nipāta.

1. Rūpādivagga

1. The Rūpādi Vagga

Pucchā – ekakanipātepi [M 1.0003] āvuso rūpādivaggo nīvaraṇappahānavaggo akammaniyavaggotiādinā vaggabhedavasena bahuvidhā. Tattha kataraṃ vaggaṃ paṭhamaṃ saṃgāyiṃsu.

Question: Venerable, in the Ekaka Nipāta there are various vagga divisions, such as the Rūpādi Vagga, the Nīvaraṇappahāna Vagga, the Akammaṇiya Vagga, and so on. Which vagga did they recite first?

Vissajjanā – ekakanipāte bhante rūpādivaggo nīvaraṇappahānavaggotiādinā vīsatiyā vaggesu rūpādivaggaṃ paṭhamaṃ saṃgāyiṃsu.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the Ekaka Nipāta, among the twenty vaggas, such as the Rūpādi Vagga and the Nīvaraṇappahāna Vagga, they first recited the Rūpādi Vagga.

Pucchā – sādhu sādhu āvuso, mayampi dāni āvuso tatoyeva paṭṭhāya saṃgāhanatthāya saṃgītipubbaṅgamāni dhammapucchanavissajjanakiccāni āvahituṃ samārabhāma. Tenāvuso bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte rūpādivagge purimāni pañca suttāni kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitāni.

Question: Good, good, Venerable. We too, Venerable, shall now undertake the task of questioning and answering on the Dhamma, proceeding from that point for the purpose of compilation, with recitation as the prelude. Therefore, Venerable, where, concerning whom, and how were the first five suttas in the Rūpādi Vagga of the Ekaka Nipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya spoken by the Blessed One—who knows and sees, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 1.0004] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha rūpādigarukānaṃ pañcannaṃ purisānaṃ ajjhāsayavasena “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekarūpampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ purisassa cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave itthirūpaṃ, itthirūpaṃ bhikkhave purisassa cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, concerning several monks, having regard to the dispositions of five men devoted to forms and the like, the Blessed One spoke thus: 'Monks, I do not perceive even one other form that so captivates a man's mind as does a woman's form. A woman's form, monks, captivates a man's mind.'

Nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekarūpampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ purisassa cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave itthirūpaṃ, itthirūpaṃ bhikkhave purisassa cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati.

Monks, I do not perceive even one other form that so captivates a man's mind as does a woman's form. A woman's form, monks, captivates a man's mind.

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0005] āvuso pacchimāni pañca suttāni bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitāni.

Question: Venerable, in that same place, where, concerning whom, and how were the last five suttas spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tasmiṃyeva bhante sāvatthiyaṃ sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha rūpādigarukānaṃ pañcannaṃ itthīnaṃ ajjhāsayavasena “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekarūpampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ itthiyā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave purisarūpaṃ, purisarūpaṃ bhikkhave itthiyā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, concerning several monks, having regard to the dispositions of five women devoted to forms and the like, the Blessed One spoke thus: 'Monks, I do not perceive even one other form that so captivates a woman's mind as does a man's form. A man's form, monks, captivates a woman's mind.'

Nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekarūpampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ itthiyā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave purisarūpaṃ, purisarūpaṃ bhikkhave itthiyā cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhati.

Monks, I do not perceive even one other form that so captivates a woman's mind as does a man's form. A man's form, monks, captivates a woman's mind.

Nītatthavasena, neyyatthavasena

In terms of definitive meaning, in terms of provisional meaning.

Pucchā – imasmiṃ [M 1.0006] āvuso rūpādivagge bhagavatā nītatthavasena kiṃ kathitaṃ, neyyatthavasena pana kiṃ ñāpitaṃ.

Question: Venerable, in this Rūpādi Vagga, what was spoken by the Blessed One as having definitive meaning, and what was made known as having provisional meaning?

Vissajjanā – imasmiṃ bhante rūpādivagge bhagavatā nītatthavasena vaṭṭaṃ kathitaṃ. Neyyatthavasena pana vivaṭṭampi ñāpitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, in this Rūpādi Vagga, the Blessed One spoke of the round as having definitive meaning. But as having provisional meaning, he made known the unbinding as well.

2. Nīvaraṇappahānavagga

2. The Nīvaraṇappahāna Vagga

Pucchā – dutiyo [M 1.0007] pana āvuso nīvaraṇappahānavaggo bhagavatā kattha kathañca bhāsito.

Question: But friend, where and how was the second Nīvaraṇappahāna Vagga spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yena anuppanno vā kāmacchando uppajjati, uppanno vā kāmacchando bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave subhanimittaṃ, subhanimittaṃ bhikkhave ayoniso manasikaroto anuppannoceva kāmacchando uppajjati, uppanno ca kāmacchando bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya saṃvattatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsito.

Answer: Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, the Blessed One spoke thus: "Monks, I do not see even one other thing whereby unarisen sensual lust arises, or arisen sensual lust increases and expands, as this, monks: the sign of beauty. Monks, for one who attends unwisely to the sign of beauty, unarisen sensual lust arises, and arisen sensual lust increases and expands."

Pucchā – imasmiṃ [M 1.0008] pana āvuso dutiye nīvaraṇappahānavagge bhagavatā kiṃ kathitaṃ.

Question: But friend, what was spoken by the Blessed One in this second Nīvaraṇappahāna Vagga?

Vissajjanā – imasmiṃ pana bhante dutiye nīvaraṇappahānavagge bhagavatā vaṭṭampi vivaṭṭampi kathitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, in this second Nīvaraṇappahāna Vagga, the Blessed One has spoken about both the round and its cessation.

3. Akammaniyava

3. The Akammaniya Vagga

Pucchā – tenāvuso…pe… [M 1.0009] sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte tatiye akammaniyavagge kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Question: Friend, ... what kind of Dhamma teachings were taught by the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One in the third Akammaniya Vagga of the Ekaka Nipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya?

Vissajjanā – tatiye bhante akammaniyavagge “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ abhāvitaṃ akammaniyaṃ hoti, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave cittaṃ, cittaṃ bhikkhave abhāvitaṃ akammaniyaṃ hoti. Nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ bhāvitaṃ kammaniyaṃ hoti, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave cittaṃ. Cittaṃ bhikkhave bhāvitaṃ kammaniyaṃ hotī”ti evamādikā bhagavatā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the third Akammaniya Vagga, Dhamma discourses such as these were taught by the Blessed One: "Monks, I do not see even one other thing that, when undeveloped, is so unserviceable as the mind. Monks, an undeveloped mind is unserviceable. Monks, I do not see even one other thing that, when developed, is so serviceable as the mind. Monks, a developed mind is serviceable."

4. Adantavagga

4. The Adanta Vagga

Pucchā – catutthe [M 1.0010] panāvuso adantavagge bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Question: Friend, in the fourth Adanta Vagga, what kind of Dhamma teachings were taught by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – catutthe bhante adantavagge “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ adantaṃ mahato anatthāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave cittaṃ, cittaṃ bhikkhave adantaṃ mahato anatthāya saṃvattati. Nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ dantaṃ mahato atthāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave cittaṃ, cittaṃ bhikkhave dantaṃ mahato atthāya saṃvattatī”ti evamādikā bhagavatā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the fourth chapter, on the Untamed: "Monks, I do not see even one other thing that, when untamed, leads to such great harm as the mind. Monks, an untamed mind leads to great harm. Monks, I do not see even one other thing that, when tamed, leads to such great benefit as the mind. Monks, a tamed mind leads to great benefit." Thus were such Dhamma discourses delivered by the Blessed One.

5. Paṇihitaacchavagga

5. The Chapter on the Misdirected

Pucchā – pañcame [M 1.0011] āvuso paṇihitaacchavagge bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Question: In the fifth chapter, friend, on the Misdirected, what kind of Dhamma discourses were delivered by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – pañcame bhante paṇihitaacchavagge “seyyathāpi bhikkhave sālisūkaṃ vā yavasūkaṃ vā micchāpaṇihitaṃ hatthena vā pādena vā akkantaṃ hatthaṃ vā pādaṃ vā bhecchati, lohitaṃ vā uppādessatīti netaṃ ṭhāna vijjati. Taṃ kissahetu, micchāpaṇihitattā bhikkhave sūkassa. Evameva kho bhikkhave so vata bhikkhu micchāpaṇihitena cittena avijjaṃ [M 1.0012] bhecchati, vijjaṃ uppādessati, nibbānaṃ sacchikarissatīti netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjati, taṃ kissa hetu, micchāpaṇihitattā bhikkhave cittassā”ti evamādikā bhagavatā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the fifth chapter, on the Misdirected: "Just as, monks, it is not possible that a wrongly directed rice awn or barley awn, when pressed upon by hand or foot, would pierce the hand or foot and draw blood. For what reason? Because the awn is wrongly directed, monks. Even so, monks, it is not possible that a monk with a wrongly directed mind would penetrate ignorance, give rise to knowledge, and realize Nibbāna. For what reason? Because the mind is wrongly directed, monks." Thus were such Dhamma discourses delivered by the Blessed One.

6. Accharāsaṅghātavagga

6. The Chapter on the Snapping of a Finger

Pucchā – accharāsaṅghātavagge [M 1.0013] āvuso paṭhama dutiyasuttāni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: In the chapter on the Snapping of a Finger, friend, how were the first and second suttas spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – accharāsaṅghātavagge bhante bhagavatā “pabhassaramidaṃ bhikkhave cittaṃ, tañca kho āgantukehi upakkilesehi upakkiliṭṭhaṃ, taṃ assutavā puthujjano yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, tasmā ‘assutavato puthujjanassa cittabhāvanā natthī’ti vadāmi. Pabhassaramidaṃ bhikkhave cittaṃ, tañca kho āgantukehi upakkilesehi vippamuttaṃ, taṃ subhavā ariyasāvako yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Tasmā ‘sutapato [M 1.0014] ariyasāvakassa cittabhāvanā atthī’ti vadāmī”ti. Evaṃ kho bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: In the chapter on the Snapping of a Finger, venerable sir, the Blessed One said: "Monks, this mind is luminous, but it is defiled by adventitious defilements. The uninstructed ordinary person does not understand this as it really is; therefore I say that for the uninstructed ordinary person there is no development of mind. Monks, this mind is luminous, and it is freed from adventitious defilements. The instructed noble disciple understands this as it really is; therefore I say that for the instructed noble disciple there is development of mind." Thus indeed were they spoken by the Blessed One.

Tatiyasutta

The Third Discourse

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0015] āvuso tatiyasuttādīni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: Friend, how were the suttas beginning with the Third spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante tatiyasuttādīni pañcasuttāni “accharāsaṅghātamattampi ce bhikkhave bhikkhu mettācittaṃ āsevati, bhāveti, manasikaroti. Ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave bhikkhu arittajjhāno viharati satthusāsanakaro ovādapatikaro amoghaṃ raṭṭhapiṇḍaṃ bhuñjati, ko pana vādo ye naṃ bahulīkaronti. Yekeci bhikkhave dhammā akusalā akusalabhāgiyā akusalapakkhikā, sabbete manopubbaṅgamā, mano tesaṃ dhammānaṃ paṭhamaṃ uppajjati, anvadeva akusalādhammā, yekeci bhikkhave dhammā kusalā kusalabhāgiyā kusalapakkhikā, sabbete manopubbaṅgamā, mano tesaṃ dhammānaṃ paṭhamaṃ uppajjati, anvadeva kusalā dhammā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, in that same place, the Blessed One spoke the five suttas beginning with the Third Sutta: "Monks, if a monk cultivates, develops, and attends to a mind of loving-kindness even for the duration of a finger-snap, he is called a monk who dwells not deficient in jhāna, who follows the Teacher’s instruction, who responds to advice, and who does not eat the country’s alms in vain. What then of those who make it their regular practice? Monks, whatever unwholesome states there are, pertaining to the unwholesome, siding with the unwholesome—all these have mind as their forerunner; mind arises first for them, and the unwholesome states follow after. Monks, whatever wholesome states there are, pertaining to the wholesome, siding with the wholesome—all these have mind as their forerunner; mind arises first for them, and the wholesome states follow after." Thus indeed were they spoken by the Blessed One.

Aṭṭhamasutta

The Eighth Discourse

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0017] āvuso aṭṭhama navama dasamasuttāni ca vīriyārambhādivagge dasasuttāni ca kalyāṇamittādivagge paṭhamasuttañca bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: Friend, how were the Eighth, Ninth, and Tenth Discourses in that same place, and the ten discourses in the chapter on the beginning of effort, and the First Discourse in the chapter on good friends spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante aṭṭhama navama dasamasuttāni ca vīriyārambhādivagge dasasuttāni ca kalyāṇamittādivagge paṭhamasuttañca “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yena anuppannā vā akusalā dhammā uppajjanti, uppannā vā kusalā dhammā parihāyanti, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave pamādo, pamattassa bhikkhave anuppannā ceva akusalā dhammā uppajjanti, uppannā ca kusalā dhammā parihāyantī”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, in that same place the Blessed One spoke the Eighth, Ninth, and Tenth Discourses, and the ten discourses in the chapter on the beginning of effort, and the First Discourse in the chapter on good friends, beginning thus: 'Monks, I do not see even one other thing whereby unarisen unwholesome states arise and arisen wholesome states decline, as does heedlessness. For the heedless, monks, unarisen unwholesome states arise and arisen wholesome states decline.' Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Dutiyasutta

The Second Discourse

Pucchā – kalyāṇamittādivagge [M 1.0019] āvuso dutiya tatiyasuttāni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: Friend, how were the Second and Third Discourses in the chapter on good friends spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – kalyāṇamittādivagge bhante dutiya tatiyasuttāni “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yena anuppannā vā akusalā dhammā uppajjanti, uppannā vā kusalā dhammā parihāyanti, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave anuyogo akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ ananuyogo kusalānaṃ dhammāna”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the chapter on good friends, the Second and Third Discourses were spoken by the Blessed One, beginning thus: 'Monks, I do not perceive even one other thing whereby unarisen unwholesome states arise and arisen wholesome states decline, as does this: application to unwholesome states and non-application to wholesome states.' Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Chaṭṭhasutta

The Sixth Discourse

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0020] āvuso chaṭṭhasuttādīni ca pamādādivagge paṭhamasuttañca bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: Friend, in that same place, how were the Sixth Discourse and the discourses beginning with it, and the First Discourse in the chapter on heedlessness, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante chaṭṭhasuttādīni ca pamādādivagge paṭhamasuttañca “appamattikā esā bhikkhave parihāni yadidaṃ ñātiparihāni. Etaṃ patikiṭṭhaṃ bhikkhave parihānīnaṃ yadidaṃ paññāparihānīti. Appamattikā esā bhikkhave vuddhi yadidaṃ ñātivuddhi. Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave vuddhīnaṃ yadidaṃ paññāvuddhi[M 1.0021]. Tasmātiha bhikkhave evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ ‘paññāvuddhiyā vaddhissāmā’ti. Evañhi vo bhikkhave sikkhitabba”nti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, in that same place, the Sixth Discourse and the discourses beginning with it, and the First Discourse in the chapter on heedlessness, were spoken by the Blessed One, beginning thus: 'Monks, slight is this decline, that is, the decline of relatives. This is the most blameworthy of declines, monks, that is, the decline of wisdom. Slight is this growth, monks, that is, the growth of relatives. This is the foremost of growths, monks, that is, the growth of wisdom. Therefore, monks, you should train yourselves thus: "We will grow in wisdom." Thus, monks, should you train yourselves.' Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Pamādādivagga

The Chapter on Heedlessness

Pucchā – tenāvuso…pe… [M 1.0022] sammāsambuddhena pamādādivagge dutiyasuttādīni kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: Then, friend... how were the Second Discourse and the discourses beginning with it in the chapter on heedlessness spoken by the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yo evaṃ mahato anatthāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave pamādo. Pamādo bhikkhave mahato anatthāya saṃvattati. Nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi, yo evaṃ mahato atthāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave appamādo, appamādo bhikkhave mahato anatthāya saṃvattatīti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: 'Monks, I do not perceive even one other thing that leads to such great harm as heedlessness. Heedlessness, monks, leads to great harm. Monks, I do not perceive even one other thing that leads to such great benefit as heedfulness. Heedfulness, monks, leads to great benefit.' Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Dutiya pamādādivagga

The Second Chapter on Heedlessness

Pucchā – dutiye [M 1.0024] panāvuso pamādādivagge paṭhamasuttādīni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question: Friend, how were the First Discourse and the discourses beginning with it in the second chapter on heedlessness spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – dutiye bhante pamādādivagge ajjhattikaṃ bhikkhave aṅganti karitvā nāññaṃ ekaṅgampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ mahato anatthāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave pamādo. Pamādo bhikkhave mahato anatthāya saṃvattati. Ajjhattikaṃ bhikkhave aṅganti karitvā nāññaṃ ekaṅgampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ mahato atthāya saṃvattati, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave appamādo. Appamādo bhikkhave mahato atthāya saṃvattatīti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the second chapter on heedlessness, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: 'Monks, considering internal factors, I do not perceive even one other factor that leads to such great harm as heedlessness. Heedlessness, monks, leads to great harm. Monks, considering internal factors, I do not perceive even one other factor that leads to such great benefit as heedfulness. Heedfulness, monks, leads to great benefit.' Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Dasasutta

The Tenth Discourse

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0026] āvuso tettiṃsamādīnica dasasuttāni adhammavagge ca dasasuttāni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

The Question: Friend, in that same place, how were the ten discourses beginning with the Thirty-three, and the ten discourses in the chapter on non-Dhamma, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante tettiṃsamādīni dasasuttāni ca adhammavagge dasasuttāni ca ye te bhikkhave bhikkhū adhammaṃ “dhammo”ti dīpenti, te bhikkhave bhikkhū bahujanaahitāya paṭipannā bahujanaasukhāya bahuno janassa anatthāya ahitāya dukkhāya devamanussānaṃ. Bahuñca te bhikkhave bhikkhū apuññaṃ pasavanti, te cimaṃ saddhammaṃ antaradhāpentīti evamādinā ca, ye te bhikkhave bhikkhū adhammaṃ “adhammo”ti dīpenti, te bhikkhave bhikkhū bahujanahitāya paṭipannā bahujanasukhāya bahujano janassa atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Bahuñca te bhikkhave bhikkhū puññaṃ pasavanti, te cimaṃ saddhammaṃ ṭhapentīti evamādinā ca bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

The Response: Venerable sir, in that same place, the ten discourses beginning with the Thirty-three, and the ten discourses in the chapter on non-Dhamma, were spoken by the Blessed One, beginning thus: 'Monks, those monks who proclaim non-Dhamma as "Dhamma," they, monks, are practicing for the harm of many, for the unhappiness of many, for the detriment, harm, and suffering of many people, both devas and humans. And those monks, monks, generate much demerit; they cause this true Dhamma to disappear.' And beginning thus: 'But those monks, monks, who proclaim non-Dhamma as "non-Dhamma," they, monks, are practicing for the welfare of many, for the happiness of many, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of many people, both devas and humans. And those monks, monks, generate much merit; they establish this true Dhamma.' Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Ekapuggalavagga

The Chapter on the One Person

Pucchā – ekapuggalavagge [M 1.0029] āvuso bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

The Question: Friend, in the Chapter on the One Person, what sort of Dhamma teaching was delivered by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – ekapuggalavagge bhante bhagavatā ekapuggalo bhikkhave loke uppajjamāno uppajjati bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katamo ekapuggalo, tathāgato arahaṃ sammāsambuddho, ayaṃ kho bhikkhave ekapuggalo loke uppajjamāno uppajjati bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānanti evamādikā bhagavatā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

The Reply: Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the One Person, the Blessed One delivered the following Dhamma teaching: 'Monks, when one person arises in the world, he arises for the welfare of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of devas and humans. Who is that one person? The Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. This, monks, is that one person who arises in the world for the welfare of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of devas and humans.' Thus were the Dhamma teachings delivered by the Blessed One.

Etadaggavagga

The Chapter on the Foremost

Aññāsikoṇḍañña vatthu

The Story of Aññāsikoṇḍañña

Pucchā – etadavagge [M 1.0030] āvuso āgatesu ekacattālīsāya theresu āyasmā aññāsikoṇḍaññatthero kathaṃ bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Question: Friend, in the Chapter on the Foremost, among the forty-one elders who came, how was the Venerable Elder Aññāsikoṇḍañña established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?

Vissajjanā – etadaggavagge bhante āgatesu ekacattālīsāya mahātheresu “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ rattaññūnaṃ yadidaṃ aññāsikoṇḍañño”ti, evaṃ kho bhante āyasmā aññāsikoṇḍaññatthero bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the Foremost, among the forty-one great elders who had come, the Blessed One declared: 'Monks, among my monk disciples who are of long standing, Aññāsikoṇḍañña is the foremost.' Thus, venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Aññāsikoṇḍañña was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

Aggasāvakavatthu

The Story of the Chief Disciples

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0036] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte etadaggavagge āgatesu ekacattālīsāya mahātheresu āyasmā ca sāriputtatthero dhammasenāpati paṭhamo aggasāvako āyasmā ca mahāmoggallānatthero dutiyo aggasāvako kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Question: Friend, how were the Venerable Elder Sāriputta, the General of the Dhamma, the first chief disciple, and the Venerable Elder Mahāmoggallāna, the second chief disciple, established in the foremost position by that Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Book of Ones, the Chapter on the Foremost, among the forty-one great elders who had come?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante sāriputtatthero dhammasenāpati “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ mahāpaññānaṃ yadidaṃ sāriputto”ti. Āyasmā pana mahāmoggallānatthero “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ iddhimantānaṃ yadidaṃ mahāmoggallāno”ti. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Sāriputta, the General of the Dhamma, was established thus: 'Monks, among my monk disciples who are of great wisdom, Sāriputta is the foremost.' And the Venerable Elder Mahāmoggallāna thus: 'Monks, among my monk disciples who possess psychic powers, Mahāmoggallāna is the foremost.' In this way, venerable sir, they were established in the foremost position by the Blessed One.

Idaṃ [M 1.0040] te āsanaṃ vīra, paññattaṃ tavanucchaviṃ;

Mama cittaṃ pasādento, nisīda pupphamāsane.

This seat, O hero, is prepared for you, fitting for you; gladdening my heart, be seated on this flowered seat.

Ye [M 1.0044] vata loke arahanto vā arahattamaggaṃ vā samāpannā, ayaṃ tesaṃ bhikkhu aññataro.

Truly, of those in the world who are Arahants or have attained the path to Arahantship—this monk is one of them.

Yaṃnūnāhaṃ imaṃ bhikkhuṃ upasaṅkamitvā pañhaṃ puccheyyaṃ.

What if I were to approach this monk and ask him a question?

Ye dhammā hetuppabhavā,

Tesaṃ hetuṃ tathāgato;

Āha tesañca yo nirodho,

Evaṃvādī mahāsamaṇo –

Of those phenomena that arise from a cause, the Tathāgata has told their cause, and also their cessation. Thus teaches the Great Recluse.

Labheyyāma [M 1.0046] mayaṃ bhante bhagavato santike pabbajjaṃ, labheyyāma upasampadaṃ.

Venerable sir, may we receive the going forth in the presence of the Blessed One; may we receive the full ordination.

Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0047] bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ mahāpaññānaṃ yadidaṃ sāriputto.

Monks, among my monk disciples who are of great wisdom, Sāriputta is the foremost.

Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ iddhimantānaṃ yadidaṃ mahāmoggallāno.

Monks, among my monk disciples who possess psychic powers, Mahāmoggallāna is the foremost.

Mahākassapavatthu

The Story of Mahākassapa

Pucchā – āyasmā [M 1.0048] pana āvuso mahākassapatthero paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle pāmokkhasaṅghanāyakabhūto bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Question: Now how, friend, was the Venerable Elder Mahākassapa, who was the chief leader of the Saṅgha at the time of the First Great Council, established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante mahākassapatthero paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle saṅghapāmokkhabhūto “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ dhutavādānaṃ yadidaṃ mahākassapo”ti. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Mahākassapa, who was the leader of the Saṅgha at the time of the First Great Council, was established in the foremost position by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, among my monk disciples who advocate ascetic practices, Mahākassapa is the foremost.'

Anuruddhattheravatthu

The Story of the Elder Anuruddha

Pucchā – tenāvuso…pe… [M 1.0051] sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte etadaggavagge āgatesu ekacattālīsāya mahātheresu āyasmā anuruddhatthero kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Question: Friend, how was the Venerable Elder Anuruddha, among the forty-one great elders who came, established in the foremost position by that Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Book of Ones, the Chapter on the Foremost?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā [M 1.0052] bhante anuruddhatthero “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ dibbacakkhukānaṃ yadidaṃ anuruddho”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Anuruddha was established in the foremost position by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, among my monk disciples who possess the divine eye, Anuruddha is the foremost.'

Sīvalivatthu

The Story of Sīvali

Pucchā – āyasmā [M 1.0054] pana āvuso sīvalitthero bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Question: But how, friend, was the Venerable Elder Sīvali established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante sīvalitthero “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ lābhīnaṃ yadidaṃ sīvalī”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Sīvali was established in the foremost position by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, among my monk disciples who are recipients of offerings, Sīvali is the foremost.'

Sammāsambuddho [M 1.0056] vata so bhagavā, yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya dhammaṃ deseti.

Truly, that Blessed One is a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, who teaches the Dhamma for the abandoning of such suffering.

Suppaṭipanno vata tassa bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho, yo imassa evarūpassa dukkhassa pahānāya paṭipanno.

Well-practiced indeed is the Saṅgha of that Blessed One's disciples, which has practiced for the abandoning of such suffering.

Susukhaṃ vata nibbānaṃ, yatthidaṃ evarūpaṃ dukkhaṃ na saṃvijjati –

Truly blissful is Nibbāna, where no such suffering is found.

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0058] bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ lābhīnaṃ yadidaṃ sīvali” hu –

Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are recipients of gains is Sīvali.

Ānandātheravatthu

The Story of Elder Ānanda

Pucchā – āyasmā pana āvuso ānandatthero dhammabhaṇḍāgāriko paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle dhammavissajjakatherabhūto kathaṃ bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Question: But how, friend, was the venerable Ānanda, the treasurer of the Dhamma, who was the elder who expounded the Dhamma at the time of the First Great Council, established by the Blessed One in this foremost position?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā [M 1.0059] bhante ānandatthero dhammabhaṇḍāgāriko paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle dhammavissajjakabhūto “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ bahussutānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando. Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ satimantānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando. Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ gatimantānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando. Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ dhitimantānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando. Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ upaṭṭhākānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā pañcasu etadaggaṭṭhānesu ṭhapito.

Answer: Venerable sir, the venerable Ānanda, the treasurer of the Dhamma and the one who expounded the Dhamma at the time of the First Great Council, was established by the Blessed One in five foremost positions thus: “Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are learned is Ānanda. Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are mindful is Ānanda. Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are of good memory is Ānanda. Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are resolute is Ānanda. Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are attendants is Ānanda.” Thus, venerable sir, was he established by the Blessed One in five foremost positions.

Satasahassena [M 1.0061] me kītaṃ,

Satasahassena māpitaṃ;

Sobhanaṃ nāma uyyānaṃ;

Paṭiggaṇha mahāmuni –

“Bought by me for a hundred thousand, built for a hundred thousand; the park named Sobhana—accept it, O Great Sage.”

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0064] bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ bahussutānaṃ. Satimantānaṃ. Gatimantānaṃ. Dhitimantānaṃ. Upaṭṭhākānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando” –

“Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are learned, mindful, of good memory, resolute, and attendants, is Ānanda.”

Upālivatthu

The Story of Upāli

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0065] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte etadaggavagge āgatesu ekacattālīsāya mahātheresu āyasmā upālitthero paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle vinayavissajjakabhūto kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Question: Friend, among the forty-one great elders mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga of the Ekakanipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, how was the venerable Upāli, who was the expounder of the Vinaya at the time of the First Great Council, established in the foremost position by that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante upālitthero paṭhamamahāsaṃgītikāle vinayavissajjakabhūto “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ vinayadharānaṃ yadidaṃ upālī”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Answer: Venerable sir, the venerable Upāli, who was the expounder of the Vinaya at the time of the First Great Council, was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: “Foremost, monks, among my bhikkhu disciples who are upholders of the Vinaya is Upāli.” Thus, venerable sir, was he established by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

Vinayo vāsayo mayhaṃ,

Vinayo ṭhānacaṅkamaṃ;

Kappemi vinaye vāsaṃ,

Vinayo mama gocaro.

“The Vinaya is my dwelling, the Vinaya is my place for standing and walking; I make my abode in the Vinaya, the Vinaya is my pasture.”

Mahāpajāpatigotamītherīvatthu

The Story of the Elder Nun Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī

Pucchā – etadaggavagge [M 1.0068] āvuso āgatāsu terasasu therīsu mahāpajāpati gotamītherī kathaṃ bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: Friend, among the thirteen elder nuns mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga, how was the elder nun Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī established in the foremost position by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – etadaggavagge [M 1.0069] bhante āgatāsu terasasu therīsu mahāpajāpatigotamītherī “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ rattaññūnaṃ yadidaṃ mahāpajāpati gotamī”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, among the thirteen elder nuns mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga, the elder nun Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: “Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and are of long standing is Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī.” Thus, venerable sir, was she established by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0073] bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ rattaññūnaṃ yadidaṃ mahāpajāpatigotamī” –

“Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and are of long standing is Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī.”

Pucchā – khemātherī pana āvuso aggasāvikā bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: But, friend, how was the elder nun Khemā, the chief female disciple, established in the foremost position by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – khemātherī bhante paṭhamā aggasāvikā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ mahāpaññānaṃ yadidaṃ khemā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, the elder nun Khemā, the first chief female disciple, was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: “Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and are of great wisdom is Khemā.” Thus, venerable sir, was she established by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

“Ye rāgarattānupatanti sotaṃ,

Sayaṃkataṃ makkaṭova jālaṃ,

Etampi chetvāna vajanti dhīrā;

Anapekkhino sabbadukkhaṃ pahāya” –

“Those dyed with passion fall into the stream, like a spider into its self-spun web; the wise, having cut this, go forth, free from longing, abandoning all suffering.”

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0077] bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ mahāpaññānaṃ yadidaṃ khemā” –

“Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and are of great wisdom is Khemā.”

Pucchā – uppalavaṇṇā pana āvuso therī dutiyaaggasāvikā bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: But, friend, how was the elder nun Uppalavaṇṇā, the second chief female disciple, established in the foremost position by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – uppalavaṇṇā bhante therī dutiyaaggasāvikā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ iddhimantīnaṃ yadidaṃ uppalavaṇṇā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, the elder nun Uppalavaṇṇā, the second chief female disciple, was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: “Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and possess psychic powers is Uppalavaṇṇā.” Thus, venerable sir, was she established by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

“Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ iddhimantīnaṃ yadidaṃ uppalavaṇṇā” –

“Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and possess psychic powers is Uppalavaṇṇā.”

Paṭācārātherībhikkhunīmavatthu

The Story of the Elder Nun Paṭācārā

Pucchā – tenāvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte etadaggavagge āgatāsu terasasu therīsu paṭācārānāma therī kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: Friend, among the thirteen elder nuns mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga of the Ekakanipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, how was the elder nun named Paṭācārā established in the foremost position by that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – etadaggavagge bhante āgatāsu terasasu therikāsu paṭācārā therī “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ vinayadharānaṃ yadidaṃ paṭācārā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, among the thirteen elder nuns mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga, the elder nun Paṭācārā was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: “Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and are upholders of the Vinaya is Paṭācārā.” Thus, venerable sir, was she established by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

“Ubho puttā kālaṅkatā,

Panthe mayhaṃ patī mato;

Mātāpitā ca bhātā ca,

Ekacitakasmiṃ ḍayhare” –

“Both my sons are dead, on the path my husband died; my mother, father, and brother were burned on a single pyre.”

“Catūsu samuddesu jalaṃ parittakaṃ,

Tato bahuṃ assujalaṃ anappakaṃ;

Dukkhena phuṭṭhassa narassa socanā,

Kiṃ kāraṇā amma tuvaṃ pamajjasi” –

“The water in the four oceans is but a trifle; far more abundant are the tears of a person touched by suffering and grieving. For what reason, mother, are you heedless?”

“Na [M 1.0083] santi puttā tāṇāya,

Na pitā nāpi bandhavā;

Antakenādhipannassa,

Natthi ñātīsu tāṇatā;

Etamatthavasaṃ ñatvā, paṇḍito sīlasaṃvuto;

Nibbānagamanaṃ maggaṃ, khippameva visodhaye” –

“Sons are no protection, nor father, nor relatives; for one overcome by the Ender, there is no protection among kinsmen. Understanding this matter, the wise, restrained by virtue, should swiftly clear the path leading to Nibbāna.”

“Yo [M 1.0084] ca vassasataṃ jīve,

Apassaṃ udayabbayaṃ;

Ekāhaṃ jīvitaṃ seyyo,

Passato udayabbayaṃ” –

“Though one might live a hundred years not seeing arising and passing away, better is a single day of life for one who sees arising and passing away.”

“Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ vinayadharānaṃ yadidaṃ paṭācārā” –

“Foremost, monks, among my female disciples who are nuns and are upholders of the Vinaya is Paṭācārā.”

Dhammadinnātherībhikkhunīmavatthu

The Story of the Elder Nun Dhammadinnā.

Pucchā – dhammadinnā [M 1.0085] pana āvuso therī bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: "But how, friend, was the Elder Nun Dhammadinnā established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?"

Vissajjanā – dhammadinnā bhante therī “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ dhammakathikānaṃ yadidaṃ dhammadinnā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, the Elder Nun Dhammadinnā was established by the Blessed One thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my nun disciples who are expounders of the Dhamma is Dhammadinnā.' Thus, Venerable Sir, she was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position."

“Yassa [M 1.0088] pure ca pacchā ca,

Majjheca natthi kiñcanaṃ;

Akiñcanaṃ anādānaṃ,

Tamahaṃ brūmi brāhmaṇaṃ” –

"For whom there is nothing before, after, or in between; who is without possessions and without clinging: him I call a Brahmin."

“Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ dhammakathikānaṃ yadidaṃ dhammadinnā” –

"Foremost, O monks, among my nun disciples who are expounders of the Dhamma is Dhammadinnā."

Yasodharātherībhikkhunīmavatthu

The Story of the Elder Nun Yasodharā.

Pucchā – bhaddakaccānānāma āvuso yasodharātherī bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: "Friend, how was the Elder Nun Yasodharā, named Bhaddakaccānā, established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?"

Vissajjanā – bhaddakaccānā [M 1.0089] bhante yasodharātherī bhagavatā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ mahābhiññapattānaṃ yadidaṃ bhaddakaccānā”ti. Evaṃ kho bhante etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, the Elder Nun Yasodharā, Bhaddakaccānā, was established by the Blessed One thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my nun disciples who have attained great supernormal knowledge is Bhaddakaccānā.' Thus, Venerable Sir, she was established in the foremost position."

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0090] bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ mahābhiññapattānaṃ yadidaṃ bhaddakaccānā” –

"Foremost, O monks, among my nun disciples who have attained great supernormal knowledge is Bhaddakaccānā."

Tapussabhallikavatthu

The Story of Tapussa and Bhallika.

Pucchā – aṅguttaranikāye [M 1.0091] āvuso etadaggavagge āgatesu ekādasasu upāsakesu tapussabhallikānāma vāṇijā bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: "Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Etadagga Vagga, among the eleven lay disciples mentioned, how were the merchants named Tapussa and Bhallika established in the foremost position by the Blessed One?"

Vissajjanā – tapussabhallikā bhante vāṇijā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsakānaṃ paṭhamaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchantānaṃ yadidaṃ tapussabhallikā vāṇijā”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, the merchants Tapussa and Bhallika were established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my lay disciples who first went for refuge are the merchants Tapussa and Bhallika.' Thus, Venerable Sir, they were established by the Blessed One in the foremost position."

“Ete [M 1.0093] bhante mayaṃ bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma dhammañca, upāsake no bhagavā dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupete saraṇaṃ gate” hu –

"Venerable Sir, we go to the Blessed One for refuge, and to the Dhamma. May the Blessed One remember us as lay followers who, from this day forth, for as long as life lasts, have gone for refuge."

“Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsakānaṃ paṭhamaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchantānaṃ yadidaṃ tapussabhallikā vāṇijā” –

"Foremost, O monks, among my lay disciples who were the first to go for refuge are Tapussa and Bhallika, the merchants."

Anāthapiṇḍakavatthu

The Story of Anāthapiṇḍika.

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0094] bhagavatā arahatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte etadaggavagge āgatesu ekādasasu upāsakesu sudatto gahapati anāthapiṇḍiko kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Question: "Friend, how was the householder Sudatta, Anāthapiṇḍika, established in the foremost position among the eleven lay disciples mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga of the Book of the Ones in the Aṅguttara Nikāya by that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?"

Vissajjanā – etadaggavagge bhante āgatesu ekādasasu upāsakesu sudatto gahapati anāthapiṇḍiko “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsakānaṃ dāyakānaṃ yadidaṃ sudatto gahapati anāthapiṇḍiko”ti. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, among the eleven lay disciples mentioned in the Etadagga Vagga, the householder Sudatta, Anāthapiṇḍika, was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my lay disciples who are donors is the householder Sudatta, Anāthapiṇḍika.' Thus, Venerable Sir, he was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position."

Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0097] bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsakānaṃ dāyakānaṃ yadidaṃ sudatto gahapati anāthapiṇḍiko–

"Foremost, O monks, among my lay disciples who are donors is Sudatta the householder, Anāthapiṇḍika."

Sūrambaṭṭhaupāsakāvatthu

The Story of the Lay Follower Sūrambaṭṭha.

Pucchā – sūrambaṭṭho nāma āvuso upāsako bhagavatā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Question: "Friend, how was the lay follower named Sūrambaṭṭha established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?"

Vissajjanā – sūrambaṭṭho upāsako “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsakānaṃ aveccappasannānaṃ yadidaṃ sūrambaṭṭho”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapito.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, the lay follower Sūrambaṭṭha was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my lay disciples who have unwavering confidence is Sūrambaṭṭha.' Thus, Venerable Sir, he was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position."

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0100] bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsakānaṃ aveccappasannānaṃ yadidaṃ sūrambaṭṭho” hu –

"Foremost, O monks, among my lay disciples who have unwavering confidence is Sūrambaṭṭha."

Sujātā upāsikāvatthu

The Story of the Laywoman Sujātā.

Pucchā – etadaggavagge [M 1.0101] āvuso āgatāsu dasasu upāsikāsu sujātānāma upāsikā seniyadhītā kathaṃ bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: "Friend, in the Etadagga Vagga, among the ten laywomen mentioned, how was the laywoman named Sujātā, the daughter of Seniya, established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?"

Vissajjanā – sujātā bhante seniyadhītā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ upāsikānaṃ paṭhamaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchantīnaṃ yadidaṃ sujātā seniyadhītā”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, Sujātā, the daughter of Seniya, was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my female lay disciples who were the first to go for refuge is Sujātā, the daughter of Seniya.' Thus, Venerable Sir, she was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position."

“Etā [M 1.0104] mayaṃ bhante bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca, upāsikāyo no bhagavā dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupetā saraṇaṃ gatā” hu –

"Venerable Sir, we go to the Blessed One for refuge, to the Dhamma, and to the Saṅgha of monks. May the Blessed One remember us as laywomen who, from this day forth, for as long as life lasts, have gone for refuge."

“Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ upāsikānaṃ paṭhamaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchantīnaṃ yadidaṃ sujātā seniyadhītā” –

"Foremost, O monks, among my female lay disciples who were the first to go for refuge is Sujātā, the daughter of Seniya."

Visākhā upāsikāvatthu

The Story of the Laywoman Visākhā.

Pucchā – visākhā [M 1.0105] pana āvuso upāsikā kathaṃ bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Question: "But, friend, how was the laywoman Visākhā established by the Blessed One in the foremost position?"

Vissajjanā – visākhā bhante upāsikā migāramātā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ upāsikānaṃ dāyikānaṃ yadidaṃ visākhā migāramātā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

Reply: "Venerable Sir, the laywoman Visākhā, Migāra's mother, was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position thus: 'Foremost, O monks, among my female lay disciples who are donors is Visākhā, Migāra's mother.' Thus, Venerable Sir, she was established by the Blessed One in the foremost position."

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0107] bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ upāsikānaṃ dāyikānaṃ yadidaṃ visākhā migāramātā” hu –

“Monks, foremost among my female lay disciples who are donors is Visākhā Migāramātā.”

Khujjuttarā upāsikāvatthu

The Story of the Female Lay Follower Khujjuttarā

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0108] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye ekakanipāte etadaggavagge āgatāsu dasasu upāsikāsu khujjuttarānāma upāsikā kathaṃ etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

The Question: Friend, how was the female lay follower named Khujjuttarā placed in the foremost position by the Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, among the ten female lay followers mentioned in the Etadaggavagga of the Ekakanipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya?

Vissajjanā – etadaggavagge bhante āgatāsu dasasu upāsikāsu khujjuttarā upāsikā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ upāsikānaṃ bahussutānaṃ yadidaṃ khujjuttarā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

The Reply: Venerable sir, among the ten female lay followers mentioned in the Etadaggavagga, the female lay follower Khujjuttarā was placed by the Blessed One in the foremost position with the declaration: “Monks, foremost among my female lay disciples who are learned is Khujjuttarā.” Thus, venerable sir, she was placed by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0111] bhikkhave mama sāvakānaṃ upāsikānaṃ bahussutānaṃ yadidaṃ khujjuttarā” hu –

“Monks, foremost among my female lay disciples who are learned is Khujjuttarā.”

Kāḷīupāsikāvatthu

The Story of the Female Lay Follower Kāḷī

Pucchā – kāḷīnāma āvuso upāsikā kuraragharikā kathaṃ bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

The Question: Friend, how was the female lay follower named Kāḷī of Kuraragharika placed in the foremost position by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – kāḷī bhante upāsikā kuraragharikā “etadaggaṃ bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ anussavappasannānaṃ yadidaṃ kāḷī upāsikā kuraragharikā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā etadaggaṭṭhāne ṭhapitā.

The Reply: Venerable sir, the female lay follower Kāḷī of Kuraragharika was placed by the Blessed One in the foremost position with the declaration: “Monks, foremost among my female lay disciples who are confident through hearsay is the female lay follower Kāḷī of Kuraragharika.” Thus, venerable sir, she was placed by the Blessed One in the foremost position.

“Etadaggaṃ [M 1.0112] bhikkhave mama sāvikānaṃ upāsikānaṃ anussavappasannānaṃ yadidaṃ kāḷī upāsikā kuraragharikā” –

“Monks, foremost among my female lay disciples who are confident through hearsay is the female lay follower Kāḷī of Kuraragharika.”

Aṭṭhānapāḷi

The Text on Impossibilities

Pucchā – sakalāpi [M 1.0113] āvuso aṭṭhānapāḷi bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitā.

The Question: Friend, how was the entire Text on Impossibilities spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – aṭṭhānametaṃ bhikkhave anavakāso, yaṃ diṭṭhisampanno puggalo kañcisaṅkhāraṃ niccato upagaccheyya, netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjati. Ṭhānañca kho etaṃ bhikkhave vijjati, yaṃ puthujjano kañcisaṅkhāraṃ niccato upagaccheyya, ṭhānametaṃ vijjatīti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā sakalāpi aṭṭhānapāḷi bhāsitā.

The Reply: “Monks, this is impossible, there is no chance, that a person accomplished in view would regard any conditioned formation as permanent; that possibility does not exist. And this is possible, monks, that an ordinary person would regard some conditioned formation as permanent; that possibility exists.” In this way and others, venerable sir, the entire Text on Impossibilities was spoken by the Blessed One.

Ekadhammapāḷi paṭhamavagga

The Text on a Single Thing: The First Chapter

Pucchā – ekadhammapāḷiyaṃ [M 1.0114] āvuso paṭhamavagge kīdisī dhammadesanā bhagavatā desitā.

The Question: Friend, in the first chapter of the Text on a Single Thing, what kind of Dhamma teaching was taught by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – ekadhammapāḷiyaṃ bhante paṭhamavagge “ekadhammo bhikkhave bhāvito bahulīkato ekantanibbidāya virāgāya nirodhāya upasamāya abhiññāya sambodhāya nibbānāya saṃvattati. Katamo ekadhammo, buddhānussati, ayaṃ kho bhikkhave ekadhammo bhāvito bahulīkato ekantanibbidāya virāgāya nirodhāya upasamāya abhiññāya sambodhāya nibbānāya saṃvattatī”ti evamādikā bhagavatā dhammadesanā desitā.

The Reply: Venerable sir, in the first chapter of the Text on a Single Thing, the Blessed One taught Dhamma teachings such as this: “Monks, one thing, when developed and cultivated, leads solely to disenchantment, to dispassion, to cessation, to peace, to direct knowledge, to full enlightenment, to Nibbāna. What is that one thing? Recollection of the Buddha. Monks, this one thing, when developed and cultivated, leads solely to disenchantment, to dispassion, to cessation, to peace, to direct knowledge, to full enlightenment, to Nibbāna.”

Pucchā – dutiyavagge [M 1.0115] pana āvuso bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

The Question: But friend, what kind of Dhamma teachings were taught by the Blessed One in the second chapter?

Vissajjanā – dutiyavagge [M 1.0116] bhante bhagavatā “nāhaṃ bhikkhave aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yena anuppannā vā akusalā dhammā uppajjanti, uppannā vā akusalā dhammā bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya saṃvattanti, yathayidaṃ bhikkhave micchādiṭṭhi. Micchādiṭṭhikassa bhikkhave anuppannā ceva akusalā dhammā uppajjanti, uppannā ca akusalā dhammā bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya saṃvattantī”ti. Evamādikā bhagavatā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

The Reply: In the second chapter, venerable sir, the Blessed One taught Dhamma teachings such as this: “Monks, I do not see even one other thing by which unarisen unwholesome states arise, or by which arisen unwholesome states lead to increase and abundance, as this, monks: wrong view. For one with wrong view, monks, unarisen unwholesome states indeed arise, and arisen unwholesome states lead to increase and abundance.”

Tatiyavagga

The Third Chapter

Pucchā – tatiyavagge [M 1.0117] pana āvuso pañcamādīni aṭṭha suttāni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

The Question: In the third chapter, friend, how were the eight suttas, beginning with the fifth, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tatiyavagge bhante pañcamādīni aṭṭhasuttāni “durakkhāte bhikkhave dhammavinaye yo ca samādapeti, yañca samādapeti, yo ca samādapito tathattāya paṭipajjati. Sabbete bahuṃ apuññaṃ pasavanti. Taṃkissahetu, durakkhābhattā bhikkhave dhammassa. Svākkhāte bhikkhave dhammavinaye yo ca samādapeti, yañca samādapeti, yo ca samādapito, tathattāya paṭipajjati, sabbete bahuṃ puññaṃ pasavanti. Taṃkissahetu svākkhātattā bhikkhave dhammassā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

The Reply: In the third chapter, venerable sir, the eight suttas beginning with the fifth were spoken by the Blessed One in this way and others: “Monks, in a Dhamma and Discipline poorly proclaimed, whoever encourages, whoever is encouraged, and whoever, being encouraged, practices accordingly—all of them generate much demerit. Why is that? Because, monks, the Dhamma is poorly proclaimed. Monks, in a Dhamma and Discipline well-proclaimed, whoever encourages, whoever is encouraged, and whoever, being encouraged, practices accordingly—all of them generate much merit. Why is that? Because, monks, the Dhamma is well-proclaimed.”

Apara accharāsaṅghātavagga

Another Chapter on the Snap of the Fingers

Pucchā – aparaaccharāsaṅghātavagge [M 1.0120] pana āvuso bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

The Question: But, friend, in the other Chapter on the Snap of the Fingers, what kind of Dhamma teachings were taught by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – aparaaccharāsaṅghātavagge bhante “accharāsaṅghātamattampi ce bhikkhave bhikkhu paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ bhāveti, ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave bhikkhu arittajjhāno viharati satthusāsanakaro ovādapatikaro amoghaṃ raṭṭhapiṇḍaṃ bhuñjati, ko pana vādo ye naṃ bahulīkarontī”ti evamādikā bhante bhagavatā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

The Reply: Venerable sir, in the other Chapter on the Snap of the Fingers, the Blessed One taught Dhamma teachings such as this: “Bhikkhus, if a bhikkhu develops the first jhāna even for the duration of a finger snap, this, bhikkhus, is called a bhikkhu who dwells not devoid of jhāna, who fulfills the Teacher’s instructions, who is responsive to advice, who eats the country’s alms-food not in vain. What need is there to speak of those who cultivate it frequently?”

Kāyagatāsativagga

The Chapter on Mindfulness Directed to the Body

Pucchā – sakalopi [M 1.0122] āvuso kāyagatāsativaggo bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsito.

The Question: Friend, how was the entire Chapter on Mindfulness Directed to the Body spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – yassa kassaci bhikkhave mahāsamuddo cetasā phuṭo, antogadhā tassa kunnadiyo yākāci samuddaṅgamā, evameva bhikkhave yassa kassaci kāyagatāsati bhāvitā bahulīkatā, antogadhā tassa kusalā dhammā yekeci vijjābhāgiyā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā sakalopi kāyagatāsativaggo bhāsito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, in this way and others, the Blessed One spoke the entire Chapter on Mindfulness Directed to the Body: “Bhikkhus, for whomever the great ocean is encompassed by the mind, whatever rivers flow into the ocean are included therein. In the same way, bhikkhus, for whomever mindfulness directed to the body is developed and cultivated, whatever skillful qualities are conducive to true knowledge are included therein.”

Amatavagga

The Chapter on the Deathless

Pucchā – sakalopi [M 1.0123] āvuso amatavaggo bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsato.

The Question: Friend, how was the entire Chapter on the Deathless spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – amataṃ te bhikkhave na paribhuñjanti, ye kāyagatāsatiṃ na paribhuñjanti. Amataṃ te bhikkhave paribhuñjanti, ye kāyagatāsatiṃ paribhuñjanti. Amataṃ te bhikkhave aparibhuttaṃ, yesaṃ kāyagatāsati aparibhuttā. Amataṃ te bhikkhave paribhuttaṃ, yesaṃ kāyagatāsati paribhuttāti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā sakalopi amatavaggo bhāsito.

The Reply: Venerable sir, in this way and others, the Blessed One spoke the entire Chapter on the Deathless: “Bhikkhus, those who do not partake of mindfulness directed to the body do not partake of the Deathless. Bhikkhus, those who partake of mindfulness directed to the body partake of the Deathless. The Deathless is not partaken of by those for whom mindfulness directed to the body is not partaken of. The Deathless is partaken of by those for whom mindfulness directed to the body is partaken of.”

Kammakaraṇavagga, vajjasutta

The Chapter on the Performance of Kamma; the Sutta on Faults

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0125] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye dukanipāte kammakaraṇavagge paṭhamaṃ vajjasuttaṃ kattha kassa kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, by the Blessed One, the Perfectly Enlightened One—in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of the Twos, in the Chapter on Deeds—where, for whom, and how was the first Discourse on Faults spoken?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ “dvemāni bhikkhave vajjāni. Katamāni dve diṭṭhadhammikañca vajjaṃ samparāyikañca vajja”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, the Blessed One spoke thus to a number of bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, there are these two faults. What are the two? A fault pertaining to the present life and a fault pertaining to the life to come.”

Tasmātiha [M 1.0127] bhikkhave evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ “diṭṭhadhammikassa vajjassa bhāyissāma, samparāyikassa vajjassa bhāyissāma, vajjabhīruno bhavissāma vajjabhayadassāvino”ti. Evañhi kho bhikkhave sikkhitabbaṃ.

“Therefore, bhikkhus, you should train yourselves thus: ‘We will fear a fault pertaining to the present life; we will fear a fault pertaining to the life to come; we will be fearful of faults; we will see the danger in faults.’ Thus, bhikkhus, should you train yourselves.”

Padhānasutta

The Discourse on Exertion

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0128] āvuso dutiyaṃ padhānasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kassa kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, where, to whom, and how was the second Discourse on Exertion spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃyeva bhante sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ “dvemāni bhikkhave padhānāni durabhisambhavāni lokasmiṃ. Katamāni dve, yañca gihīnaṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasataṃ cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānuppādanatthaṃ padhānaṃ, yañca agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitānaṃ sabbūpadhipaṭinissaggattaṃ padhānaṃ. Imāni kho bhikkhave dve padhānāni durabhisambhavāni lokasmi”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī itself, the Blessed One spoke thus to a number of bhikkhus: “Bhikkhus, these two exertions are difficult to accomplish in the world. What two? The exertion of householders living at home to produce robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicinal requisites; and the exertion of those gone forth from home to homelessness to relinquish all attachments. Bhikkhus, these two exertions are difficult to accomplish in the world.”

Tapanīyasutta

The Discourse on Tormenting

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0130] āvuso tapanīyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, how was the Discourse on Tormenting spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vissajjanā – dveme bhikkhave dhammā tapanīyā. Katame dve, idha bhikkhave ekaccassa kāyaduccaritaṃ kataṃ hoti, akataṃ hoti kāya sucaritaṃ, vacīduccaritaṃ kataṃ hoti, akataṃ hoti vacīsucaritaṃ, manoduccaritaṃ kataṃ hoti, akataṃ hoti manosucaritaṃ. So “kāyaduccaritaṃ me kata”nti tappati, “akataṃ me kāyasucarita”nti tappati. Vacīduccaritaṃ (pa) “manoduccaritaṃ me kata”nti tappati, “akataṃ me manosucarita”nti tappati. Ime kho bhikkhave dve dhammā tapanīyāti. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: “Bhikkhus, these two things are tormenting. What two? Here, bhikkhus, a certain person has performed bodily misconduct and has not performed bodily good conduct; has performed verbal misconduct and has not performed verbal good conduct; has performed mental misconduct and has not performed mental good conduct. He is tormented, thinking, ‘I have performed bodily misconduct,’ and he is tormented, thinking, ‘I have not performed bodily good conduct.’ He is tormented, thinking, ‘I have performed verbal misconduct…’ He is tormented, thinking, ‘I have performed mental misconduct,’ and he is tormented, thinking, ‘I have not performed mental good conduct.’ These, bhikkhus, are the two tormenting things.” Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Atapanīyasutta

The Discourse on Non-Tormenting

Pucchā – catutthaṃ [M 1.0131] pana āvuso atapanīyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: But, friend, how was the fourth, the Discourse on Non-Tormenting, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – catutthaṃ bhante atapanīyasuttaṃ “dveme bhikkhave dhammā atapanīyā. Katame dve, idha bhikkhave ekaccassa kāyasucaritaṃ kataṃ hoti, akataṃ hoti kāyaduccaritaṃ. Vacīsucaritaṃ kataṃ hoti, akataṃ hoti vacīduccaritaṃ. Manosucaritaṃ kataṃ hoti, akataṃ hoti manoduccaritaṃ. So ‘kāyasucaritaṃ me kata’nti na tappati, ‘akataṃ me kāyaduccarita’nti natappati. ‘Vacīsucaritaṃ…pe… manosucaritaṃ me kata’nti na tappati. ‘Akataṃ me manoduccarita’nti na tappati. Ime kho bhikkhave dve dhammā atapanīyā”ti. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, the fourth is the Discourse on Non-Tormenting: “Bhikkhus, there are these two things that do not torment. What two? Here, bhikkhus, a certain person has performed bodily good conduct and has not performed bodily misconduct; has performed verbal good conduct and has not performed verbal misconduct; has performed mental good conduct and has not performed mental misconduct. He is not tormented, thinking, ‘I have performed bodily good conduct,’ and he is not tormented, thinking, ‘I have not performed bodily misconduct.’ He is not tormented, thinking, ‘I have performed verbal good conduct…’ He is not tormented, thinking, ‘I have performed mental good conduct,’ and he is not tormented, thinking, ‘I have not performed mental misconduct.’ These, bhikkhus, are the two things that do not torment.” Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Upaññātasutta

The Discourse on What Was Declared

Pucchā – pañcamaṃ [M 1.0132] pana āvuso upaññātasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: But, friend, how was the fifth Discourse on What Was Declared spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – pañcamaṃ bhante upaññātasuttaṃ “dvinnāhaṃ bhikkhave dhammānaṃ upaññāsiṃ. Yā ca asantuṭṭhitā kusalesu dhammesu, yā ca appaṭivānitā padhānasmiṃ. Appaṭivāni sudāhaṃ bhikkhave padahāmi ‘kāmaṃ-taco ca nhāru ca aṭṭhi ca avasissatu upasussatu sarīre maṃsalohitaṃ, yaṃ [M 1.0133] taṃ purisathāmena purisavīriyena purisaparakkamena pattabbaṃ, na taṃ apāpuṇitvā vīriyassa saṇṭhānaṃ bhavissatī”ti. Evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, the fifth Discourse on What Was Declared was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Bhikkhus, I have declared two things: discontent with wholesome states and non-retreat in striving. Bhikkhus, I strive without retreating, thinking: “Let only skin, sinews, and bones remain; let the flesh and blood dry up in my body, but I will not stop striving until I achieve what can be achieved by human strength, human energy, and human effort.”’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Cariyasutta

The Discourse on Conduct

Pucchā – navamaṃ [M 1.0134] pana āvuso cariyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: But, friend, how was the ninth Discourse on Conduct spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – navamaṃ bhante cariyasuttaṃ “dveme bhikkhave dhammā sukkā lokaṃ pālenti. Katame dve, hirīca ottappañca. Ime kho bhikkhave dve sukkā dhammā lokaṃ na pāleyyuṃ, nayidha paññāyetha ‘mātā’tivā ‘mātucchā’tivā ‘mātulānī’tivā ‘ācariyabhariyā’tivā ‘garūnaṃ dārā’ti”vāti, evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, the ninth Discourse on Conduct was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Bhikkhus, these two bright principles protect the world. Which two? Conscience and moral dread. If, bhikkhus, these two bright principles did not protect the world, there would be no recognition here of “mother,” “maternal aunt,” “maternal uncle’s wife,” “teacher’s wife,” or “wives of respected ones.”’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Adhikaraṇavagga, sattamasutta

The Chapter on Issues, the Seventh Sutta

Pucchā – adhikaraṇavagge [M 1.0136] pana āvuso sattamasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: But, friend, regarding the seventh sutta in the Chapter on Issues, where was it spoken by the Blessed One, concerning whom, about what matter, and how?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante jāṇussoṇiṃ brāhmaṇaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Jāṇussoṇi bhante brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “ko nu kho bho gotama hetu ko paccayo, yena midhekacce sattā kāyassa bhedā paraṃmaraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjantī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “katattā ca brāhmaṇa akatattā ca evamidhekacce sattā kāyassa bhedā paraṃmaraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjantī”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, it was spoken at Sāvatthī concerning the brahmin Jāṇussoṇi. Venerable sir, the brahmin Jāṇussoṇi said this to the Blessed One: ‘What, Master Gotama, is the cause, what is the condition, whereby some beings here, on the dissolution of the body, after death, are reborn in a state of deprivation, in an unhappy destination, in perdition, in hell?’ On that occasion, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: ‘Because of what has been done, brahmin, and because of what has not been done, some beings here, on the dissolution of the body, after death, are reborn in a state of deprivation, in an unhappy destination, in perdition, in hell.’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Ko [M 1.0137] nu kho bho gotama hetu ko paccayo, yena midhekacce sattā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjanti.

“What, Master Gotama, is the cause, what is the condition, whereby some beings here, on the dissolution of the body, after death, are reborn in a state of deprivation, in an unhappy destination, in perdition, in hell?”

“Katattāca brāhmaṇa akatattāca evamidhekacce sattā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjanti” –

“Because of what has been done, brahmin, and because of what has not been done, some beings here, on the dissolution of the body, after death, are reborn in a state of deprivation, in an unhappy destination, in perdition, in hell.”

Ko [M 1.0138] pana bho gotama hetu ko paccayo, yena midhekacce sattā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjanti –

“But what, Master Gotama, is the cause, what is the condition, whereby some beings here, on the dissolution of the body, after death, are reborn in a good destination, a heavenly world?”

Katattā ca brāhmaṇa akatattā ca evamidhekacce sattā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjanti.

“Because of what has been done, brahmin, and because of what has not been done, some beings here, on the dissolution of the body, after death, are reborn in a good destination, a heavenly world.”

Na kho ahaṃ imassa bhoto gotamassa saṃkhittena bhāsitassa vitthārena atthaṃ avibhattassa vitthārena atthaṃ –

“I do not understand the detailed meaning of this brief statement made by the venerable Gotama, which has not been analyzed in detail.”

Sādhu me bhavaṃ gotamo tathā dhammaṃ desetu.

“It would be good, Master Gotama, if you would teach me the Dhamma in such a way.”

Aṭṭhamasutta

The Eighth Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0140] āvuso aṭṭhamasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kassa kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, in that same place, where, to whom, and how was the eighth sutta spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmato ānandassa “ekaṃsenāhaṃ ānanda akaraṇīyaṃ vadāmi kāyaduccaritaṃ manoduccarita”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, it was spoken at Sāvatthī to the Venerable Ānanda thus: ‘Ānanda, I declare categorically that bodily misconduct and mental misconduct are not to be done.’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

“Ekaṃsenāhaṃ [M 1.0141] ānanda karaṇīyaṃ vadāmi kāyasucaritaṃ vacīsucaritaṃ manosucaritaṃ” –

“Ānanda, I state unequivocally that good bodily conduct, good verbal conduct, and good mental conduct are to be done.”

Navamasutta

The Ninth Sutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0142] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye dukanipāte adhikaraṇavagge navamasuttaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – “But, friend, was the ninth sutta in the Adhikaraṇavagga of the Dukanipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya spoken by the Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?”

Vissajjanā – akusalaṃ [M 1.0143] bhikkhave pajahatha, sakkā bhikkhave akusalaṃ pajahituṃ. Nocedaṃ bhikkhave sakkā abhavissa akusalaṃ pajahituṃ, nāhaṃ evaṃ vadeyyaṃ “akusalaṃ bhikkhave pajahathā”ti. Yasmā ca kho bhikkhave sakkā akusalaṃ pajahituṃ, tasmāhaṃ evaṃ vadāmi “akusalaṃ bhikkhave pajahathā”ti. Akusalañca hidaṃ bhikkhave pahīnaṃ ahitāya dukkhāya saṃvatteyya, nāhaṃ evaṃvadeyyaṃ “akusalaṃ bhikkhave pajahathā”ti. Yasmā ca kho bhikkhave akusalaṃ pahīnaṃ hitāya sukhāya saṃvattati, tasmāhaṃ evaṃ vadāmi “akusalaṃ bhikkhave pajahathā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Monks, abandon what is unwholesome. It is possible, monks, to abandon what is unwholesome. If it were not possible, monks, to abandon what is unwholesome, I would not say, “Monks, abandon what is unwholesome.” But because it is possible, monks, to abandon what is unwholesome, therefore I say, “Monks, abandon what is unwholesome.” And if abandoning what is unwholesome led to harm and suffering, I would not say, “Monks, abandon what is unwholesome.” But because abandoning what is unwholesome leads to benefit and happiness, therefore I say, “Monks, abandon what is unwholesome.”’ Thus, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Dasamasutta, ekādasamasutta

The Tenth Sutta and the Eleventh Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0145] āvuso dasamaekādasamasuttāni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

The Question – “Friend, how were the tenth and eleventh suttas spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?”

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante dasamaekādasamasuttāni “dveme bhikkhave dhammā saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattanti. Katame dve, dunnikkhittañca padabyañjanaṃ attho ca dunnīto, dunnikkhittassa bhikkhave padabyañjanassa atthopi dunnayo hoti. Ime kho bhikkhave dve dhammā saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattantī”ti ca. “Dveme bhikkhave dhammā saddhammassa ṭhitiyā asammosāya anantaradhānāya saṃvattanti. Katame dve, sunikkhittañca padabyañjanaṃ attho ca sunīto, sunikkhittassa bhikkhave padabyañjanassa atthopi sunayo hoti. Ime kho bhikkhave dve dhammā saddhammassa ṭhitiyā asammosāya anantaradhānāya saṃvattantī”ti ca. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, in that same place, the tenth and eleventh suttas were spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Monks, these two things lead to the confusion and disappearance of the True Dhamma. Which two? Badly arranged words and phrases, and meaning badly construed. When the words and phrases are badly arranged, monks, the meaning too is badly understood. These two things, monks, lead to the confusion and disappearance of the True Dhamma.’ And: ‘Monks, these two things lead to the endurance, non-confusion, and non-disappearance of the True Dhamma. Which two? Well-arranged words and phrases, and meaning well construed. When the words and phrases are well arranged, monks, the meaning too is well understood. These two things, monks, lead to the endurance, non-confusion, and non-disappearance of the True Dhamma.’ Thus, venerable sir, were they spoken by the Blessed One.”

Bālavagga

The Chapter on Fools

Dutiyasutta

The Second Sutta

Pucchā – bālavagge [M 1.0147] pana āvuso dutiyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – “But, friend, how was the second sutta in the Chapter on Fools spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vissajjanā – bālavagge bhante dutiyaṃ suttaṃ “dveme bhikkhave tathāgataṃ abbhācikkhanti. Katame dve, duṭṭho vā dosantaro saddho vā duggahitena. Ime kho bhikkhave dve tathāgataṃ abbhācikkhantī”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, the second sutta in the Chapter on Fools was spoken thus: ‘Monks, these two malign the Tathāgata. Which two? One who is malicious and inwardly hostile, and one who is faithful but has grasped wrongly. These two, monks, malign the Tathāgata.’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.”

Tatiyasutta

The Third Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0148] āvuso tatiyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – “Friend, how was the third sutta spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?”

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante tatiyasuttaṃ bhagavatā “dveme bhikkhave tathāgataṃ abbhācikkhanti. Katame dve, yo ca abhāsitaṃ alapitaṃ tathāgatena ‘bhāsitaṃ lapitaṃ tathāgatenā’ti dīpeti. Yo ca bhāsitaṃ lapitaṃ tathāgatena ‘abhāsitaṃ alapitaṃ tathāgatenāti’ [M 1.0149] dīpeti. Ime kho bhikkhave dve tathāgataṃ abbhācikkhanti. Dveme bhikkhave tathāgataṃ nābbhācikkhanti. Katame dve, yo ca abhāsitaṃ alapitaṃ tathāgatena ‘abhāsitaṃ alapitaṃ tathāgatenā’ti dīpeti. Yo ca bhāsitaṃ lapitaṃ tathāgatena ‘bhāsitaṃ lapitaṃ tathāgatenā’ti dīpeti. Ime kho bhikkhave dve tathāgataṃ nābbhācikkhantī”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, in that same place, the third sutta was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Monks, these two malign the Tathāgata. Which two? One who declares what was not spoken or uttered by the Tathāgata as “spoken and uttered by the Tathāgata”; and one who declares what was spoken and uttered by the Tathāgata as “not spoken or uttered by the Tathāgata.” These two, monks, malign the Tathāgata. Monks, these two do not malign the Tathāgata. Which two? One who declares what was not spoken or uttered by the Tathāgata as “not spoken or uttered by the Tathāgata”; and one who declares what was spoken and uttered by the Tathāgata as “spoken and uttered by the Tathāgata.” These two, monks, do not malign the Tathāgata.’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.”

Catutthavagga, pañcamasutta

The Fourth Sutta and the Fifth Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0150] āvuso catutthapañcamasuttāni bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

The Question – “Friend, how were the fourth and fifth suttas spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?”

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante catutthapañcamasuttāni “dveme bhikkhave tathāgataṃ abbhācikkhanti. Katame dve, yo ca neyyatthaṃ suttantaṃ ‘nītattho suttanto’ti [M 1.0151] dīpeti, yo ca nītatthaṃ suttantaṃ ‘neyyattho suttanto’ti dīpetī”ti, evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, in that same place, the fourth and fifth suttas were spoken by the Blessed One thus, and so on: ‘Monks, these two malign the Tathāgata. Which two? One who declares a discourse of interpretable meaning to be of definitive meaning; and one who declares a discourse of definitive meaning to be of interpretable meaning.’”

Samacittavagga, paṭhamasutta

The Chapter on the Even Mind

Pucchā – samacittavagge [M 1.0153] āvuso paṭhamasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – “Friend, how was the first sutta in the Chapter on the Even Mind spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vissajjanā – samacittavagge bhante paṭhamasuttaṃ “asappurisabhūmiñca vo bhikkhave desessāmi sappurisabhūmiñca, taṃ suṇātha sādhukaṃ manasikarotha, bhāsissāmī”ti, evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, the first sutta in the Chapter on the Even Mind was spoken by the Blessed One thus, and so on: ‘Monks, I will teach you the ground of the ignoble person and the ground of the noble person. Listen to it, attend closely, I will speak.’”

Dutiyasutta

The Second Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0156] āvuso dutiyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – “Friend, how was the second sutta spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?”

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante dutiyasuttaṃ “dvinnāhaṃ bhikkhave na suppatikāra vadāmi. Katamesaṃ dvinnaṃ, mātu ca pitu cā”ti, evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – “Venerable sir, in that same place, the second sutta was spoken by the Blessed One thus, and so on: ‘Monks, I declare that two are not easy to repay. Which two? Mother and father.’”

Samacittasutta

The Sutta on the Even Mind

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0157] āvuso pañcamaṃ samacittasuttaṃ kattha kassa kena kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – “Friend, where, to whom, by whom, and in what manner was the fifth sutta, the Sutta on the Even Mind, spoken in that same place?”

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ “ajjhattasaṃyojanañca āvuso puggalaṃ desessāmi bahiddhāsaṃyojanañca, taṃsuṇātha

The Reply – “Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, to many bhikkhus: ‘Friends, I will teach you the person with internal fetters and the person with external fetters. Listen to it.

Sādhukaṃ [M 1.0158] manasikarothā”ti, evamādinā bhante āyasmatā sāriputtattherena dhammasenāpatinā bhāsitaṃ.
