Attend closely.’ Thus, and so on, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Venerable Sāriputta, the General of the Dhamma.”

Ovāda

Exhortation

Pucchā – kathañca [M 1.0161] āvuso tattha bhagavā ovādaṃ adāsi.

The Question – “And how, friend, did the Blessed One give his exhortation there?”

Vissajjanā – tattha bhante bhagavā “tasmātiha sāriputta evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ ‘santindriyā bhavissāma santamānasā’ti. Evañhi vo sāriputta sikkhitabba”nti evamādinā ovādamadāsi.

The Reply – “There, venerable sir, the Blessed One gave his exhortation thus, and so on: ‘Therefore, Sāriputta, you should train thus: “We will have our senses calmed and our minds calmed.” Thus, Sāriputta, should you train.’”

“Eso bhante āyasmā sāriputto pubbārāme migāramātupāsā de bhikkhūnaṃ ajjhattasaṃyojanañca puggalaṃ deseti bahiddhā saṃyojanañca” –

"Venerable sir, the Venerable Sāriputta is teaching the bhikkhus in the Eastern Park, in Migāra's Mother's Mansion, about the person with internal fetters and the person with external fetters."

Sādhu [M 1.0162] bhante bhagavā yena āyasmā sāriputto tenupasaṅkamatu anukampaṃ upādāya.

"It is good, venerable sir, that the Blessed One should approach the Venerable Sāriputta out of compassion."

Tā kho pana sāriputta devatā dasapi hutvā vīsampi hutvā tiṃsampi hutvā cattālīsampi hutvā paññāsampi hutvā saṭṭhipi hutvā āraggakoṭinitudanamattepi tiṭṭhanti, na ca aññamaññaṃ byābādhenti–

"Then, Sāriputta, those deities—whether ten, twenty, thirty, forty, fifty, or sixty of them—can stand on a space the size of a needle-point's prick, and they do not crowd one another."

Tasmātiha [M 1.0163] sāriputta evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ santindriyā bhavissāma santamānasā.

"Therefore, Sāriputta, you should train thus: 'We will have our faculties calmed and our minds calmed.'"

Parisavagga

The Chapter on Assemblies

Uttānasutta

The Uttāna Sutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0165] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye dukanipāte parisavagge paṭhamaṃ uttānasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, how was the first Uttāna Sutta spoken by the Blessed One… the Perfectly Enlightened One, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Book of the Twos, in the Chapter on Assemblies?

Vissajjanā – paṭhamaṃ bhante uttānasuttaṃ “dvemā sikkhave parisā, katamā dve, uttānā ca parisā gambhīrā ca parisā, katamā ca bhikkhave uttānā parisā, idha bhikkhave yassaṃ parisāyaṃ bhikkhū uddhatā honti unnaḷā capalā mukharā vikiṇṇavācā muṭṭhassatī asampajānā asamāhitā vibbhantacittā pākatindriyā. Ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave uttānā parisā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, the first Uttāna Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: "Bhikkhus, there are these two assemblies. Which two? The shallow assembly and the profound assembly. And what, bhikkhus, is the shallow assembly? Here, bhikkhus, in whatever assembly the bhikkhus are agitated, insolent, fickle, garrulous, of scattered speech, unmindful, not clearly comprehending, unconcentrated, with wandering minds, and with unrestrained faculties. This, bhikkhus, is called the shallow assembly."

Soparisattaka

Soparisattaka

Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave imāsaṃ dvinnaṃ parisānaṃ yadidaṃ gambhīrā parisā.

"Bhikkhus, of these two assemblies, this is the foremost, that is, the profound assembly."

Anaggavatīsutta

The Anaggavatī Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0167] āvuso tatiyaṃ anaggavatīsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, how was the third Anaggavatī Sutta spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante tatiyaṃ anaggavatīsuttaṃ “dvemā bhikkhave parisā. Katamā dve, anaggavatī ca parisā aggavatī ca parisā. Katamā ca bhikkhave anaggavatī parisā, idha bhikkhave yassaṃ parisāyaṃ therā bhikkhū bāhulikā honti sāthalikā, okkamane pubbaṅgamā, paviveke nikkhittadhurā, na vīriyaṃ ārabhanti appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikabhassa sacchikiriyāyā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, in that same place the third Anaggavatī Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: "Bhikkhus, there are these two assemblies. Which two? The assembly without a chief and the assembly with a chief. And what, bhikkhus, is the assembly without a chief? Here, bhikkhus, in whatever assembly the elder bhikkhus are given to abundance, lax, leaders in backsliding, who have shirked the duty of seclusion, and do not arouse energy for the attainment of the unattained, for the achievement of the unachieved, for the realization of the unrealized."

Okkācitavinītasutta

The Okkācitavinīta Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0170] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ okkācitavinītasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, how was the sixth Okkācitavinīta Sutta spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante chaṭṭhaṃ okkācitavinītasuttaṃ “dvemā bhikkhave parisā. Katamā dve, okkācitavinītā parisā no paṭipucchāvinītā, paṭipucchā vinītā parisā no okkācitavinītā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, in that same place the sixth Okkācitavinīta Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: "Bhikkhus, there are these two assemblies. Which two? An assembly trained by acquiescence, not trained by questioning; and an assembly trained by questioning, not trained by acquiescence."

Puggalavagga

The Chapter on Individuals

Asantasannivāsasutta

The Asantasannivāsa Sutta

Pucchā – puggalavagge [M 1.0173] āvuso ekādasamaṃ asantasannivāsasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, in the Chapter on Individuals, how was the eleventh Asantasannivāsa Sutta spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – puggalavagge bhante ekādasamaṃ asantasannivāsasuttaṃ “asantasannivāsañca vo bhikkhave desessāmi santasannivāsañca, taṃ suṇātha, sādhukaṃ manasikarotha bhāsissāmī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Individuals, the eleventh Asantasannivāsa Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: "Bhikkhus, I will teach you co-residence with the unrighteous and co-residence with the righteous. Listen to that and attend closely, I will speak."

Sukhavagga

The Chapter on Happiness

Pucchā – sukhavagge [M 1.0176] pana āvuso bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Question – But in the Chapter on Happiness, friend, what kind of Dhamma teachings were taught by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sukhavagge bhante bhagavatā “dvemāni bhikkhave sukhāni. Katamāni dve, gihisukhañca pabbajitasukhañca, imāni kho bhikkhave dve sukhāni. Etadaggaṃ bhikkhave imesaṃ dvinnaṃ sukhānaṃ yadidaṃ pabbajitasukha”nti evamādikā dhammadesanāyo desitā.

Response – In the Chapter on Happiness, venerable sir, Dhamma teachings such as the following were taught by the Blessed One: "Bhikkhus, there are these two kinds of happiness. Which two? The happiness of a householder and the happiness of one gone forth. These, bhikkhus, are the two kinds of happiness. Of these two kinds of happiness, this is the foremost, that is, the happiness of one gone forth."

Āyācanavagga

The Chapter on Aspiration

Pucchā – āyācanavagge [M 1.0178] pana āvuso bhagavatā paṭhamādīni cattāri suttāni kathaṃ bhāsitāni.

Question – But in the Chapter on Aspiration, friend, how were the first four suttas spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – āyācanavagge bhante paṭhamādīni cattāri suttāni “saddho bhikkhave bhikkhu evaṃ sammā āyācamāno āyāceyya tādiso homi, yādisā sāriputtamoggallānā”ti, evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitāni.

Response – In the Chapter on Aspiration, venerable sir, the first four suttas were spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: "Bhikkhus, a faithful bhikkhu, aspiring rightly, should aspire thus: 'May I be like Sāriputta and Moggallāna!'"

Avaṇṇārahasutta

The Avaṇṇāraha Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0180] āvuso pañcamaṃ avaṇṇārahasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – There, friend, how was the fifth Avaṇṇāraha Sutta spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante pañcamaṃ avaṇṇārahasuttaṃ “dvīhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato bālo abyatto asappuriso etaṃ upahataṃ attānaṃ pariharati, sāvajjo ca hoti sānuvajjo ca viññūnaṃ, bahuñca apuññaṃ pasavatī”ti, evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – There, venerable sir, the fifth Avaṇṇāraha Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Bhikkhus, endowed with two qualities, a foolish, incompetent, and bad person carries himself in a ruined and corrupt way; he is blameworthy and censured by the wise, and he generates much demerit.”

Appasādanīyasutta

The Appasādanīya Sutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0182] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye dukanipāte āyācanavagge chaṭṭhaṃ appasādanīyasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, how was the sixth Discourse on What is Uninspiring spoken by the Blessed One... the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One... in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Book of Twos, in the Chapter on Requesting?

Vissajjanā – chaṭṭhaṃ bhante appasādanīyasuttaṃ “dvīhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato bālo abyatto asappuriso etaṃ upahataṃ attānaṃ pariharati, sāvajjo ca hoti sānuvajjo ca viññūnaṃ, bahuñca apuññaṃ pasavati. Katamehi dvīhi, ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃseti, ananuvicca apariyo gāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsetī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, the sixth Discourse on What is Uninspiring was spoken thus: “Monks, a fool, an incompetent person, an ignoble person, endowed with two qualities, maintains himself in a way that is afflicted, is blameworthy and censured by the wise, and generates much demerit. What two? Without investigating and without examining, he displays confidence in what is uninspiring; and without investigating and without examining, he displays lack of confidence in what is inspiring.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Mātāpitusutta

The Discourse on Mother and Father

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0184] āvuso sattamaṃ mātāpitusuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: There, friend, how was the seventh Discourse on Mother and Father spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante sattamaṃ mātāpitusuttaṃ “dvīsu bhikkhave micchā paṭipajjamāno bālo abyatto asappuriso etaṃ upahataṃ attānaṃ pariharati, sāvajjo ca hohi sānuvajjo ca viññūnaṃ, bahuñca apuññaṃ pasavati. Katamesu dvīsu, mātari ca pitari cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: There, venerable sir, the seventh Discourse on Mother and Father was spoken thus: “Monks, a fool, an incompetent person, an ignoble person, behaving wrongly towards two, maintains himself in a way that is afflicted, is blameworthy and censured by the wise, and generates much demerit. Towards what two? Towards his mother and father.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Tathāgatasutta

The Discourse on the Tathāgata

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0185] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ tathāgatasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: There, friend, how was the eighth Discourse on the Tathāgata spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva [M 1.0186] bhante aṭṭhamaṃ tathāgatasuttaṃ “dvīsu bhikkhave micchāpaṭipajjamāno bālo abyatto asappuriso etaṃ upahataṃ attānaṃ pariharati, sāvajjo ca hoti sānuvajjo ca viññūnaṃ, bahuñca apuññaṃ pasavati. Katamesu dvīsu, tathāgate ca tathāgatasāvake cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: There, venerable sir, the eighth Discourse on the Tathāgata was spoken thus: “Monks, a fool, an incompetent person, an ignoble person, behaving wrongly towards two, maintains himself in a way that is afflicted, is blameworthy and censured by the wise, and generates much demerit. Towards what two? Towards the Tathāgata and the Tathāgata’s disciple.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Tikanipāta

The Book of Threes

Bālavagga, bhayasutta

The Chapter on Fools, the Discourse on Fear

Pucchā – tikanipāte [M 1.0189] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ bhayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kassa kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: But in the Book of Threes, friend, where, to whom, and how was the first Discourse on Fear spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulānaṃ “yāni kānici bhikkhave bhayāni uppajjanti, sabbāni tāni bālato uppajjanti no paṇḍitato. Ye keci uppaddavā uppajjanti, sabbete bālato uppajjanti no paṇḍitato. Yekeci upasaggā uppajjanti, sabbete bālato uppajjanti no paṇḍitato”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, to many: “Monks, whatever fears arise, all those arise from the fool, not from the wise one. Whatever misfortunes arise, all those arise from the fool, not from the wise one. Whatever dangers arise, all those arise from the fool, not from the wise one.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Cintīsutta

The Discourse on Thinking

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0190] āvuso tatiyaṃ cintīsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: There, friend, how was the third Discourse on Thinking spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante cintīsuttaṃ “tīṇimāni bhikkhave bālassa bālalakkhaṇāni [M 1.0191] bālanimittāni bālāpadānāni. Katamāni tīṇi, idha bhikkhave bālo duccintitacintī ca hoti dubbhāsitabhāsī ca dukkaṭakammakārī cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: There, venerable sir, the Discourse on Thinking was spoken thus: “Monks, these are the three characteristics of a fool, the signs of a fool, the marks of a fool. What three? Here, monks, a fool is one who thinks ill-thought thoughts, speaks ill-spoken speech, and does ill-done deeds.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Accayasutta

The Discourse on Transgression

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0193] āvuso catutthaṃ accayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: There, friend, how was the fourth Discourse on Transgression spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante catutthaṃ accayasuttaṃ “tīhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato bālo veditabbo. Katamehi tīhi, accayaṃ accayato disvā yathādhammaṃ nappaṭikaroti, parassa kho pana accayaṃ desentassa yathādhammaṃ nappaṭiggaṇhātī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: There, venerable sir, the fourth Discourse on Transgression was spoken thus: “Monks, a fool is to be known by three qualities. What three? Having seen a transgression as a transgression, he does not make amends for it in accordance with the Dhamma; and when another confesses a transgression, he does not accept it in accordance with the Dhamma.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Malasutta

The Discourse on Stains

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0195] āvuso dasamaṃ malasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: There, friend, how was the tenth Discourse on Stains spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante dasamaṃ malasuttaṃ “tīhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato tayo male appahāya yathābhataṃ nikkhitto evaṃ niraye. Katamehi tīhi, dussīlo ca hoti, dussīlyamalañcassa appahīnaṃ hoti. Issukī ca hoti, issāmalañcassa appahīnaṃ hoti. Maccharī ca hoti, maccharamalañcassa appahīnaṃ hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: There, venerable sir, the tenth Discourse on Stains was spoken thus: “Monks, endowed with three qualities, having not abandoned three stains, one is cast into hell as if carried there. What three? He is immoral, and the stain of immorality is not abandoned by him. He is envious, and the stain of envy is not abandoned by him. He is miserly, and the stain of miserliness is not abandoned by him.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Rathakāravagga, ñātasutta

The Chapter of the Chariot-Maker, the Discourse on the Well-Known

Pucchā – rathakāravagge [M 1.0197] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ ñātasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: In the Chapter of the Chariot-Maker, friend, how was the first Discourse on the Well-Known spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – paṭhamaṃ bhante ñātasuttaṃ “tīhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato ñāto bhikkhu bahujanaahitāya paṭipanno hoti bahujana dukkhāya bahuno janassa anatthāya ahitāya dukkhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katamehi tīhi, ananulomike kāyakamme samādapeti, ananulomike vacīkamme samādapeti, ananulomikesu dhammesu samādapetī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, the first Discourse on the Well-Known was spoken thus: “Monks, a well-known bhikkhu endowed with three qualities practices for the harm of many people, for the suffering of many people, for the misfortune, harm, and suffering of many beings, of devas and humans. What three? He encourages others in unconforming bodily action, he encourages others in unconforming verbal action, and he encourages others in unconforming qualities.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Āsaṃsasutta

The Discourse on Hope

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0199] āvuso tatiyaṃ āsaṃsasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: There, friend, how was the third Discourse on Hope spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante tatiyaṃ āsaṃsasuttaṃ “tayome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo nirāso āsaṃso”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: There, venerable sir, the third Discourse on Hope was spoken thus: “Monks, these three persons are found existing in the world. What three? The hopeless, the hopeful…” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Sacetanasutta

The Discourse on Sacetana

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0203] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte rathakāravagge pañcamaṃ sacetanasuttaṃ kattha kassa kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, by the Blessed One... the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One... in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Threes, in the Chapter of the Chariot-Maker, where, to whom, and how was the fifth Discourse on Sacetana spoken?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃ bhante sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ ārabbha “bhūtapubbaṃ bhikkhave rājā ahosi sacetano nāma, atha kho bhikkhave rājā sacetano rathakāraṃ āmantesi ito me samma rathakāra channaṃ māsānaṃ paccayena saṅgāmo bhavissati sakkhissasi me samma rathakāra navaṃ cakkayugaṃ kātu”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: At Bārāṇasī, venerable sir, concerning many bhikkhus: “Monks, once upon a time there was a king named Sacetana. Then, monks, King Sacetana addressed the chariot-maker: ‘My dear chariot-maker, six months from now there will be a battle. Will you be able to make a new pair of wheels for me?’” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Apaṇṇakasutta

The Apaṇṇaka Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0208] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ apaṇṇakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: How, friend, was the sixth Apaṇṇaka Sutta spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – chaṭṭhaṃ bhante apaṇṇakasuttaṃ “tīhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu apaṇṇakapaṭipadaṃ paṭipanno hoti, yoni cassa āraddhā hoti āsavānaṃ khayāya. Katamehi tīhi, idha bhikkhave bhikkhu indriyesu guttadvāro hoti, bhojane mattaññū hoti, jāgariyamanuyutto hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the sixth Apaṇṇaka Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One as follows: “Monks, a bhikkhu endowed with three qualities practices the infallible path, and his effort is aroused for the destruction of the taints. What three? Here, monks, a bhikkhu guards the doors of the senses, is moderate in eating, and is devoted to wakefulness.”

Devalokasutta

The Devaloka Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0210] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ devalokasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: How, friend, was the eighth Devaloka Sutta spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante aṭṭhamaṃ devalokasuttaṃ “sace vo bhikkhave aññatitthiyā paribbājakā evaṃ puccheyyuṃ “devalokūpapattiyā āvuso samaṇe gotame brahmacariyaṃ vussathā’ti. Nanu tumhe bhikkhave [M 1.0211] evaṃ puṭṭhā aṭṭīyeyyātha harāyeyyātha jiguccheyyāthā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the eighth Devaloka Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One as follows: “Monks, if wanderers of other sects were to ask you thus: ‘Friends, do you live the holy life under the ascetic Gotama for the sake of rebirth in a deva world?’ Would you not, monks, when asked thus, be troubled, ashamed, and disgusted?”

Paṭhama pāpaṇikasutta

The First Pāpaṇika Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0212] āvuso navamaṃ paṭhamapāpaṇikasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: How, friend, was the ninth First Pāpaṇika Sutta spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante navamaṃ paṭhamapāpaṇikasuttaṃ “tīhi bhikkhave aṅgehi samannāgato pāpaṇiko abhabbo anadhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ adhigantuṃ, adhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ phātiṃ kātuṃ. Katamehi tīhi, idha bhikkhave pāpaṇiko pubbaṇhasamayaṃ na sakkaccaṃ kammantaṃ adhiṭṭhātī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, there the ninth First Pāpaṇika Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One as follows: “Monks, a shopkeeper endowed with three qualities is incapable of acquiring wealth not yet acquired, or of increasing wealth already acquired. What three? Here, monks, a shopkeeper does not diligently apply himself to his work in the morning.”

Puggalavagga

The Chapter on Persons

Gilānasutta

The Gilāna Sutta

Pucchā – puggalavagge [M 1.0215] pana āvuso dutiyaṃ gilānasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: But, friend, how was the second Gilāna Sutta in the Chapter on Persons spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – puggalavagge bhante dutiyaṃ gilānasuttaṃ “tayome bhikkhave gilānā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo, idha bhikkhave ekacco gilāno labhanto vā sappāyāni bhojanāni alabhanto vā sappāyāni bhojanāni, labhanto vā sappāyāni bhesajjāni alabhanto vā sappāyāni bhesajjāni, labhanto vā patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ alabhanto vā patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ vuṭṭhāti tamhā ābādhā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the second Gilāna Sutta in the Chapter on Persons was spoken by the Blessed One as follows: “Monks, these three sick persons are found existing in the world. What three? Here, monks, a certain sick person, whether or not obtaining suitable food, whether or not obtaining suitable medicine, and whether or not obtaining a suitable attendant, recovers from that illness.”

Bahukārasutta

The Bahukāra Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0217] āvuso catutthaṃ bahukārasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: How, friend, was the fourth Bahukāra Sutta spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – tattheva [M 1.0218] bhante catutthaṃ bahukārasuttaṃ “tayo me bhikkhave puggalā puggalassa bahukārā. Katame tayo, yaṃ bhikkhave puggalaṃ āgamma puggalo buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti, dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti, saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gato hoti. Ayaṃ bhikkhave puggalo imassa puggalassa bahukāro”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, there the fourth Bahukāra Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One as follows: “Monks, these three persons are of great help to a person. What three? That person, monks, on account of whom another person has gone for refuge to the Buddha, has gone for refuge to the Dhamma, and has gone for refuge to the Saṅgha. This person, monks, is of great help to that person.”

Jigucchitabbasutta

The Jigucchitabba Sutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0220] bhagavatā jānatā passatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte puggalavagge sattamaṃ jigucchitabbasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Therefore, friend, how was the seventh Jigucchitabba Sutta in the Chapter on Persons of the Book of the Threes of the Numerical Discourses spoken by that Blessed One, who knows and sees, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – sattamaṃ bhante jigucchitabbasuttaṃ “tayome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo, atthi bhikkhave puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo, atthi bhikkhave puggalo ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo, atthi bhikkhave puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the seventh Jigucchitabba Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Monks, these three persons are found existing in the world. What three? There is, monks, a person to be shunned, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon. There is, monks, a person to be regarded with equanimity, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon. There is, monks, a person to be associated with, cultivated, and attended upon.’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0221] tattha bhagavatā jigucchitabbo puggalo nasevitabbo nabhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the person to be shunned, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon, declared there by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – “katamo ca bhikkhave puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo. Idha bhikkhave ekacco dussīlo hoti pāpadhammo asucisaṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo samaṇapaṭiñño abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto, evarūpo bhikkhave puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo puggalo pakāsito.

Answer: Venerable sir, the person to be shunned, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon, was declared by the Blessed One as follows: ‘And which, monks, is the person to be shunned, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon? Here, monks, a certain person is unvirtuous, of evil nature, impure and of suspect conduct, secretive in his actions, not a recluse though claiming to be one, not celibate though claiming to be so, rotten within, full of lust, a rubbish-heap. Such a person, monks, is to be shunned, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon.’

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0223] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā ajjhupekkhitabbo puggalo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the person to be regarded with equanimity, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon, declared there by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – katamo ca bhikkhave puggalo ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukarotī”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā tattha ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo puggalo pakāsito.

Answer: Venerable sir, the person to be regarded with equanimity, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon, was declared there by the Blessed One as follows: ‘And which, monks, is the person to be regarded with equanimity, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon? Here, monks, a certain person is prone to anger and frequently vexed; even when spoken to a little, he becomes offended, hostile, and stubborn, and he displays anger, hatred, and displeasure.’

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0224] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā sevitabbo puggalo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the person to be associated with, cultivated, and attended upon, declared there by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – “katamo ca bhikkhave puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo sīlavā hoti kalyāṇadhammo, evarūpo bhikkhave puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo [M 1.0225] payirupāsitabbo”ti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā sevitabbo puggalo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo pakāsito.

Answer: Venerable sir, the person to be associated with, cultivated, and attended upon, was declared there by the Blessed One as follows: ‘And which, monks, is the person to be associated with, cultivated, and attended upon? Here, monks, a certain person is virtuous, of a lovely nature. Such a person, monks, is to be associated with, cultivated, and attended upon.’

Gūthabhāṇīsutta

The Gūthabhāṇī Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0226] āvuso puggalavagge aṭṭhamaṃ gūthabhāṇīsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: How, friend, was the eighth Gūthabhāṇī Sutta in the Chapter on Persons spoken there by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante puggalavagge aṭṭhamaṃ gūthabhāṇīsuttaṃ “tayo me bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo, gūthabhāṇī pupphabhāṇī madhubhāṇī”ti evaṃ kho bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the eighth Gūthabhāṇī Sutta in the Chapter on Persons was spoken there by the Blessed One thus: ‘Monks, these three persons are found existing in the world. What three? The filth-speaker, the flower-speaker, and the honey-speaker.’

Gūthabhāṇīpuggala

The Filth-Speaking Person

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0227] tattha bhagavatā gūthabhāṇīpuggalo pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the filth-speaking person declared there by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – katamo ca bhikkhave puggalo gūthabhāṇī, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo sabhaggato vā parisaggato vā ñātimajjhagatovā pūgamajjhagatovā rājakulamajjhagatovā abhinīto sakkhipuṭṭho “ehambho purisa yaṃ jānāsi, taṃ vadehī”ti. So ajānaṃ vā āha “jānāmī”ti, jānaṃ vā āha “na jānāmī”ti, apassaṃ vā āha “passāmī”ti, passaṃ vā āha “na passāmī”ti. Iti attahetu vā parahetu vā āmisakiñcikkhahetu vā sampajānamusā bhāsitā hoti. Ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave puggalo gūthabhāṇīti, evaṃ kho bhante tattha bhagavatā gūthabhāṇīpuggalo pakāsito.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, the filth-speaking person was declared by the Blessed One thus: ‘And which, monks, is the filth-speaking person? Here, monks, a certain person, when in an assembly, a gathering, among relatives, a guild, or in the royal court, is brought forward and questioned as a witness: “Come now, good man, tell what you know.” Not knowing, he says, “I know.” Knowing, he says, “I do not know.” Not seeing, he says, “I see.” Seeing, he says, “I do not see.” Thus, for his own sake, for another’s sake, or for some trifling material gain, he knowingly speaks falsehood. This, monks, is called the filth-speaking person.’ Thus, venerable sir, the filth-speaking person was declared there by the Blessed One.”

Pupphabhāṇīpuggala

The Flower-Speaking Person

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0228] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā pupphabhāṇīpuggalo pakāsito.

The Question: “And how, friend, was the flower-speaking person declared by the Blessed One?”

Vissajjanā – katamo ca bhikkhave puggalo pupphabhāṇī, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo sabhaggato vā parisaggato vā ñātimajjhagato vā pūgamajjhagatovā rājakulamajjhagato vā abhinīto sakkhipuṭṭho “ehambho purisa yaṃ pajānāsi, taṃ vadehī”ti. So ajānaṃ vā āha “na jānāmī”ti, jānaṃ vā āha “jānāmī”ti, apassaṃ vā āha “na passāmī”ti, passaṃ vā āha “passāmī”ti, iti attahetu vā parahetu vā āmisakiñcikkhahetu vā na sampajānamusā bhāsitā hoti, ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave puggalo pupphabhāṇīti, evaṃ kho bhante tattha bhagavatā pupphabhāṇīpuggalo pakāsito.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, the flower-speaking person was declared by the Blessed One thus: ‘And which, monks, is the flower-speaking person? Here, monks, a certain person, when in an assembly, a gathering, among relatives, a guild, or in the royal court, is brought forward and questioned as a witness: “Come now, good man, tell what you know.” Not knowing, he says, “I do not know.” Knowing, he says, “I know.” Not seeing, he says, “I do not see.” Seeing, he says, “I see.” Thus, not for his own sake, not for another’s sake, nor for some trifling material gain, does he knowingly speak falsehood. This, monks, is called the flower-speaking person.’ Thus, venerable sir, the flower-speaking person was declared there by the Blessed One.”

Madhubhāṇīpuggala

The Honey-Speaking Person

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0229] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā madhubhāṇī puggalo pakāsito.

The Question: “But how, friend, was the honey-speaking person declared there by the Blessed One?”

Vissajjanā – katamo ca bhikkhave puggalo madhubhāṇī, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo pharusaṃ vācaṃ pahāya pharusāya vācāya paṭivirato

The Reply: “And which, monks, is the honey-speaking person? Here, monks, a certain person, having abandoned harsh speech, abstains from harsh speech.

Hotīti [M 1.0230] evamādinā bhante bhagavatā tattha madhubhāṇī puggalo pakāsito.

‘…he is.’ In this way, and so forth, venerable sir, the honey-speaking person was declared there by the Blessed One.”

Andhasutta

The Andhasutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0231] āvuso puggalavagge navamaṃ andhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question: “In that same section, friend, in the Chapter on Individuals, how was the ninth discourse, the Andhasutta, spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante puggalavagge navamaṃ andhasuttaṃ “tayome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo andho ekacakkhu dvicakkhū”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply: “In that same section, venerable sir, in the Chapter on Individuals, the ninth discourse, the Andhasutta, was spoken thus: ‘Monks, these three individuals exist and are found in the world. Which three? The blind, the one-eyed, and the two-eyed.’ Thus, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.”

Pucchā – kīdiso āvuso puggalo tattha bhagavatā andho akkhāto.

The Question: “What sort of individual, friend, was declared there by the Blessed One to be blind?”

Vissajjanā – yassa bhante bhogesu ceva dhammesu ca paññācakkhu natthi, ediso bhante puggalo tattha bhagavatā andho akkhāto.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, one who has no eye of wisdom regarding both wealth and the Dhamma—such an individual, venerable sir, was declared there by the Blessed One to be blind.”

Pucchā – kīdiso [M 1.0232] pana āvuso puggalo tattha bhagavatā ekacakkhu akkhāto.

The Question: “But what sort of individual, friend, was declared there by the Blessed One to be one-eyed?”

Vissajjanā – yassa bhante bhogesuyeva paññācakkhu atthi na dhammesu. Īdiso bhante puggalo tattha bhagavatā ekacakkhu akkhāto.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, one who has the eye of wisdom only regarding wealth, and not regarding the Dhamma—such an individual, venerable sir, was declared there by the Blessed One to be one-eyed.”

Pucchā – kīdiso [M 1.0233] pana āvuso puggalo tattha bhagavatā dvicakkhu akkhāto.

The Question: “But what sort of individual, friend, was declared there by the Blessed One to be two-eyed?”

Vissajjanā – yassa bhante bhogesu ceva dhammesu ca paññācakkhu atthi, īdiso bhante puggalo tattha bhagavatā dvicakkhu akkhāto.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, one who has the eye of wisdom regarding both wealth and the Dhamma—such an individual, venerable sir, was declared there by the Blessed One to be two-eyed.”

Avakujjasutta

The Avakujjasutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0234] bhagavatā jānatā passatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte puggalavagge dasamaṃ avakujjasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question: “Friend, how was the tenth discourse, the Avakujjasutta, spoken by that Blessed One—the Knower, the Seer… the Perfectly Enlightened One—in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Book of the Threes, the Chapter on Individuals?”

Vissajjanā – dasamaṃ bhante avakujjasuttaṃ “tayo me bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo, avakujjapañño puggalo ucchaṅgapañño puggalo puthupañño puggalo. Katamo ca bhikkhave avakujjapañño puggalo. Idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā hoti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavanāya, tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desentiādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ [M 1.0235] brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya nevādiṃ manasikaroti, na majjhaṃ manasikaroti, na pariyosānaṃ manasikaroti, vuṭṭhitopi tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya nevādiṃ manasikaroti, na majjhaṃ manasikaroti, na pariyosānaṃ manasikarotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply: “The tenth discourse, the Avakujjasutta, was spoken thus: ‘Monks, these three individuals exist and are found in the world. Which three? The individual with overturned wisdom, the individual with lap-wisdom, and the individual with broad wisdom. And which, monks, is the individual with overturned wisdom? Here, monks, a certain individual frequently goes to a monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the monks. The monks teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they reveal the spiritual life, utterly complete and pure. But while sitting on that seat, he does not attend to the beginning of that talk, nor to the middle, nor to the end. And even after rising from that seat, he does not attend to the beginning of that talk, nor to the middle, nor to the end.’ In this way, and so forth, it was spoken by the Blessed One.”

Devadūtavagga, sabrahmakasutta

The Chapter on the Divine Messengers, The Sabrahmakasutta

Pucchā – devadūtavagge [M 1.0239] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ sabrahmakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question: “But friend, in the Chapter on the Divine Messengers, how was the first discourse, the Sabrahmakasutta, spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vissajjanā – devadūtavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ sabrahmakasuttaṃ “sabrahmakāni bhikkhave tāni kulāni, yesaṃ puttānaṃ mātāpitaro ajjhāgāre pūjitā honti. Sapubbacariyakāni bhikkhave tāni kulāni, yesaṃ puttānaṃ mātāpitaro ajjhāgāre pūjitā honti. Āhuneyyāni bhikkhave tāni kulāni, yesaṃ puttānaṃ mātāpitaro ajjhāgāre pūjitā honti. Brahmāti bhikkhave mātāpitūnaṃ etaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Pubbācariyāti bhikkhave mātāpitūnaṃ etaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Āhuneyyāti bhikkhave etaṃ mātāpitūnaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Taṃ kissa hetu, bahukārā bhikkhave mātāpitaro puttānaṃ āpādakā posakā imassa lokassa dassetāro”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the Divine Messengers, the first discourse, the Sabrahmakasutta, was spoken thus: ‘Monks, those families are with Brahmā in which the parents are honored in the home by their children. Monks, those families are with the first teachers in which the parents are honored in the home by their children. Monks, those families are worthy of offerings in which the parents are honored in the home by their children. “Brahmā,” monks, is a designation for mother and father. “First teachers,” monks, is a designation for mother and father. “Worthy of offerings,” monks, is a designation for mother and father. For what reason? Because, monks, parents are of great help to their children: they are their producers, their nurturers, and they show them this world.’ Thus, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.”

Brahmāti [M 1.0241] mātāpitaro, pubbācariyāti vuccare;

Āhuneyyā ca puttānaṃ, pajāya anukampakā;

Tasmā hi ne namasseyya, sakkareyya ca paṇḍito;

Annena atha pānena, vatthena sayanena ca;

Ucchādanena nhāpanena, pādānaṃ dhovanena ca;

Tāya naṃ pāricariyāya, mātāpitūsu paṇḍitā.

“‘Mother and father are called “Brahmā,” and are said to be “first teachers”; worthy of offerings from their children, they are compassionate to their offspring. Therefore, a wise person should pay homage to them and honor them with food and then drink, with clothing and a resting place, with anointing, bathing, and with washing of the feet. For that service to mother and father, the wise…

Idheva naṃ pasaṃsanti, pecca sagge pamodatīti –

…praise him right here in this world, and after death, he rejoices in heaven.’”

Pucchā – tattheva āvuso pañcamaṃ hatthakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question: “Friend, how was the fifth discourse, the Hatthakasutta, spoken by the Blessed One there?”

Vissajjanā – pañcamaṃ bhante hatthakasuttaṃ āḷaviyaṃ hatthakaṃ āḷavakaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Hatthako bhante āḷavako bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “kacci bhante bhagavā sukhamasayitthā”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “evaṃ kumāra sukhamasayittha ye ca pana loke sukhaṃ senti, ahaṃ tesaṃ aññataro”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply: “Venerable sir, the fifth discourse, the Hatthakasutta, was spoken concerning Hatthaka of Āḷavī. Venerable sir, Hatthaka of Āḷavī said this to the Blessed One: ‘I hope, venerable sir, that the Blessed One slept well?’ On that occasion, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus, and so forth: ‘Yes, young man, I slept well. I am one of those who sleep well in the world.’”

“Kacci [M 1.0242] bhante bhagavā sukhamasayittha”.

“I hope, venerable sir, that the Blessed One slept well?”

“Evaṃ kumāra sukhamasayittha, ye ca pana loke sukhaṃ senti, ahaṃ tesaṃ aññataro”.

“Yes, young man, I slept well. I am one of those who sleep well in the world.”

“Evaṃ [M 1.0244] kumāra sukhamasayittha, ye ca pana loke sukhaṃ senti, ahaṃ tesaṃ aññataro”.

“Yes, young man, I slept well. I am one of those who sleep well in the world.”

“Sabbadā [M 1.0247] ve sukhaṃ seti,

Brāhmaṇo parinibbuto;

Yo na limpati kāmesu,

Sītibhūto nirūpadhi;

“Always indeed happily sleeps the brahmin, fully extinguished, who is not stained by sensual pleasures, cooled, without attachments.”

Sabbā āsattiyo chetvā,

Vineyya hadaye daraṃ;

Upasanto sukhaṃ seti;

Santiṃ pappuyya cetaso” hu –

“Having cut off all attachments, having dispelled distress in the heart, tranquil, one sleeps happily, having attained peace of mind.”

Devadūtasutta

The Discourse on the Divine Messengers

Pucchā – tenāvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte devadūtavagge chaṭṭhaṃ devadūtasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, how was the sixth Devadūta Sutta in the Devadūtavagga of the Tikanipāta in the Aṅguttara Nikāya spoken by the Blessed One… the Perfectly Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – chaṭṭhaṃ [M 1.0248] bhante devadūtasuttaṃ “tīṇimāni bhikkhave devadūtāni. Katamāni tīṇi, idha bhikkhave ekacco kāyena duccaritaṃ carati, vācāya duccaritaṃ carati, manasā duccaritaṃ carati. So kāyena duccaritaṃ caritvā vācāya duccaritaṃ caritvā manasā duccaritaṃ caritvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjati. Tamenaṃ bhikkhave nirayapālā nānābāhāsu gahetvā yamassa rañño dassenti-ayaṃ deva puriso amatteyyo apetteyyo asāmañño abrahmañño, na kule jeṭṭhapacāyī, imassa devo daṇḍaṃ paṇetū”ti. Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the sixth Devadūta Sutta was spoken thus: “Monks, there are these three divine messengers. What three? Here, monks, someone engages in misconduct by body, misconduct by speech, and misconduct by mind. Having engaged in misconduct by body, speech, and mind, with the breakup of the body, after death, he is reborn in a state of deprivation, a bad destination, a lower realm, in hell. Then, monks, the hell-wardens seize him by his various arms and present him to King Yama, saying: ‘Your Majesty, this person was not dutiful to his mother, not dutiful to his father, not dutiful to ascetics, not dutiful to brahmins, and did not honor the elders of his family. May Your Majesty inflict a punishment upon him.’ Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.”

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0251] tattha bhagavatā yamassa rañño paṭhamadevadūta samanuyuñjanā pakāsitā.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One there describe King Yama’s questioning concerning the first divine messenger?

Vissajjanā – tamenaṃ bhikkhave yamo rājā paṭhamaṃ devadūtaṃ samanuyuñjati samanugāhati samanubhāsati “ambho purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu paṭhamaṃ devadūtaṃ pātubhūta”nti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā yamassa rañño paṭhamadevadūtasamanuyuñjanā pakāsitā.

Answer: “Then, monks, King Yama questions, investigates, and cross-examines him concerning the first divine messenger: ‘Good man, did you not see the first divine messenger manifest among human beings?’ In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One there described King Yama’s questioning concerning the first divine messenger.”

“Ambho purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu paṭhamaṃ devadūtaṃ pātubhūtaṃ”.

“‘Good man, did you not see the first divine messenger manifest among human beings?’”

Dutiya devadūta

The Second Divine Messenger

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0252] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā yamassa rañño dutiyadevadūta samanuyuñjanā pakāsitā.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One there describe King Yama’s questioning concerning the second divine messenger?

Vissajjanā – tamenaṃ bhikkhave yamo rājā paṭhamaṃ devadūtaṃ samanuyuñjitvā samanugāhitvā samanubhāsitvā dutiyaṃ devadūtaṃ samanuyuñjati samanugāhati samanubhāsati “ambho purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu dutiyaṃ devadūtaṃ pātubhūta”nti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā yamassa rañño dutiyā devadūtasamanuyuñjanā pakāsitā.

Answer: “Then, monks, King Yama, having questioned, investigated, and cross-examined him concerning the first divine messenger, questions, investigates, and cross-examines him concerning the second divine messenger: ‘Good man, did you not see the second divine messenger manifest among human beings?’ In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One there described King Yama’s questioning concerning the second divine messenger.”

“Ambho [M 1.0255] purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu dutiya devadūtaṃ pātubhūtaṃ”.

“‘Good man, did you not see the second divine messenger manifest among human beings?’”

“Ambho purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu itthīṃ vā purisaṃ vā ābādhikaṃ dukkhitaṃ bāḷhagilānaṃ sake muttakarīse palipannaṃ semānaṃ aññehi vuṭṭhāpiyamānaṃ aññehi saṃvesiyamānaṃ” –

“‘Good man, did you not see among human beings a woman or a man who was sick, afflicted, gravely ill, lying fallen in their own urine and excrement, being lifted up by others and put to bed by others?’”

Tatiya devadūta

The Third Divine Messenger

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0256] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā yamassa rañño tatiyadevadūta samanuyuñjanā pakāsitā.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One there describe King Yama’s questioning concerning the third divine messenger?

Vissajjanā – tamenaṃ bhikkhave yamo rājā dutiyaṃ devadūtaṃ samanuyuñjitvā samanugāhitvā samanubhāsitvā tatiyaṃ devadūtaṃ samanuyuñjati samanugāhati samanubhāsati “ambho purisa natvaṃ manussesu tatiyaṃ devadūtaṃ pātubhūtanti” evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā yamassa rañño tatiyā devadūtasamanuyuñjanā pakāsitā.

Answer: “Then, monks, King Yama, having questioned, investigated, and cross-examined him concerning the second divine messenger, questions, investigates, and cross-examines him concerning the third divine messenger: ‘Good man, did you not see the third divine messenger manifest among human beings?’ In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One there described King Yama’s questioning concerning the third divine messenger.”

“Ambho purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu tatiyaṃ devadūtaṃ pātubhūtaṃ”.

“‘Good man, did you not see the third divine messenger manifest among human beings?’”

“Ambho [M 1.0257] purisa na tvaṃ addasa manussesu itthiṃ vā purisaṃ vā ekāhamataṃ vā dvīhamataṃ vā tīhamataṃ vā uddhumātakaṃ vinīlakaṃ vipubbakajātaṃ” –

“‘Good man, did you not see among human beings a woman or a man who had been dead for one, two, or three days, bloated, livid, and festering?’”

“Nāsakkhissaṃ bhante pamādassaṃ bhante” –

“‘I was not able, venerable sir; I was negligent, venerable sir.’”

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0258] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā nerayikassa sattassa niraya dukkhapaṭisaṃvedanā pakāsitā.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One there describe the hell-being’s experience of suffering in hell?

Vissajjanā – tamenaṃ bhikkhave “yamo rājā tatiyaṃ devadūtaṃ samanuyuñjitvā samanugāhitvā samanubhāsitvā tuṇhī hoti. Tamenaṃ bhikkhave nirayapālā pañcavidhabandhanaṃ nāma kāraṇaṃ karonti, tattaṃ ayokhilaṃ hatthe gamenti, tattaṃ ayokhilaṃ dutiyasmiṃ hatthe gamenti, tattaṃ ayokhilaṃ pāde gamenti, tattaṃ ayokhilaṃ dutiyasmiṃ pāde gamenti, tattaṃ ayokhilaṃ majjheurasmiṃ gamenti. So tattha dukkhā tibbā kharā kaṭukā vedanā vedayati, na ca tāva kālaṃ karoti, yāva na taṃ pāpakammaṃ byantīhotī”ti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā nerayikassa nirayadukkhapaṭisaṃvedanā pakāsitā.

Answer: “Then, monks, King Yama, having questioned, investigated, and cross-examined him concerning the third divine messenger, becomes silent. Then, monks, the hell-wardens inflict upon him the punishment called the fivefold binding. They drive a red-hot iron stake through one hand, they drive a red-hot iron stake through the other hand, they drive a red-hot iron stake through one foot, they drive a red-hot iron stake through the other foot, and they drive a red-hot iron stake through the middle of his chest. There he feels painful, sharp, harsh, bitter feelings, but he does not die so long as that evil kamma is not exhausted. In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One there described the hell-being’s experience of suffering in hell.”

Saṃvejanīyakathā

A Talk Inspiring a Sense of Urgency

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 1.0261] panāvuso tattha bhagavatā saṃvejanīyakathā kathitā.

Question: But how, friend, was the talk inspiring a sense of urgency spoken there by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – bhūtapubbaṃ bhikkhave yamassa rañño etadahosi “ye kira bho loke pāpakāni kammāni karonti, te evarūpā vividhā kammakaraṇā karīyanti, ahovatāhaṃ manussattaṃ labheyyaṃ, tathāgato ca loke uppajjeyya arahaṃ sammāsambuddho”ti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā saṃvejanīyakathā kathitā.

Answer: “Once in the past, monks, it occurred to King Yama: ‘Indeed, those in the world who do evil deeds are subjected to such various punishments. Oh, that I might attain the human state, and that a Tathāgata, an Arahant, a Perfectly Enlightened One, might arise in the world!’ In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One there spoke the talk inspiring a sense of urgency.”

“Ye [M 1.0262] kira bho loke pāpakāni kammāni karonti”.

“‘Indeed, those in the world who do evil deeds…’”

“Coditā [M 1.0263] devadūtehi, ye pamajjanti māṇavā;

Te dīgharattaṃ socanti, hīnakāyūpagā narā.

“Admonished by the divine messengers, those people who are heedless grieve for a long time, humans who go to a lower state.

Ye ca kho devadūtehi, santo sappurisā idha;

Coditā na pamajjanti, ariyadhamme kudācanaṃ.

But those good and true persons here, when admonished by the divine messengers, are never heedless in regard to the Noble Dhamma.

Upādāne bhayaṃ disvā, jātimaraṇasambhave;

Anupādā vimuccanti, jātimaraṇasaṅkhaye.

Seeing the danger in clinging, the source of birth and death, they are liberated through non-clinging, in the destruction of birth and death.

Te appamattā sukhino, diṭṭhadhammābhinibbutā;

Sabbaverabhayātītā, sabbadukkhaṃ upaccaguṃ” hu –

Heedful and happy, they are extinguished in this very life; having passed beyond all enmity and fear, they have overcome all suffering.”

Catumahārājasutta

The Discourse on the Four Great Kings

Pucchā – tenāvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte devadūtavagge aṭṭhamaṃ catumahārājasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: "Friend, how was the Catumahārājasutta—the eighth discourse in the Chapter on the Divine Messengers, in the Book of the Threes of the Aṅguttara Nikāya—spoken by the Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?"

Vissajjanā – aṭṭhamaṃ bhante catumahārājasuttaṃ “aṭṭhamiyaṃ bhikkhave pakkhassa catunnaṃ mahārājānaṃ amaccā pārisajjā imaṃ lokaṃ anuvicaranti kacci bahū manussā manussesu matteyyā petteyyā sāmaññā brahmaññā kule jeṭṭhapacāyino, uposathaṃ upavasanti paṭijāgaronti, puññāni karontī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: "The eighth, venerable sir, the Catumahārājasutta, was spoken by the Blessed One thus: 'On the eighth day of the fortnight, monks, the ministers and councilors of the Four Great Kings wander about this world to see whether many people among humankind are respectful to their mothers, fathers, ascetics, and brahmins, honor the elders of the family, observe the Uposatha, keep vigil, and perform meritorious deeds.'"

Appakā [M 1.0265] kho mārisā manussā manussesu matteyyā petteyyā sāmaññā brahmaññā kule jeṭṭhapacāyino, uposathaṃ upavasanti paṭijāgaronti, puññāni karonti.

"Few indeed, sirs, are the people among humankind who are respectful to their mothers, fathers, ascetics, and brahmins, who honor the elders in their family, who observe the Uposatha, who keep vigil, and who perform meritorious deeds."

“Dibbā [M 1.0266] vata bho kāyā parihāyissanti, paripūrissanti asura kāyā” –

"'Alas, divine hosts will decline, and asura hosts will become full!'"

Bahū kho mārisā manussā manussesu matteyyā petteyyā sāmaññā brahmaññā kule jeṭṭhapacāyino, uposathaṃ upavasanti paṭijāgaronti, puññāni karonti.

"But many, sirs, among human beings are those who are respectful to their mothers, fathers, ascetics, and brahmins, who honor the elders of the family, who observe the Uposatha, who keep vigil, and who perform meritorious deeds."

“Dibbā [M 1.0267] vata bho kāyā paripūrissanti, parihāyissanti asura kāyā” –

"'Indeed, the divine hosts will be fulfilled, and the asura hosts will decline!'"

Ādhipateyyāsutta

The Discourse on Dominance

Pucchā – tattheva āvuso dasamaṃ ādhipateyyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: "Friend, how was the tenth Discourse on Dominance spoken by the Blessed One there?"

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante dasamaṃ ādhipateyyasuttaṃ “tīṇimāni bhikkhave ādhipateyyāni. Katamāni tīṇi, attādhipateyyaṃ lokādhipateyyaṃ dhammādhipateyyaṃ. Katamañca bhikkhave attādhipateyyaṃ, idha bhikkhave bhikkhu araññagato vā rukkhamūlagato vā suññāgāragato vā iti paṭisañcikkhatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the tenth is the Discourse on Dominance: "Monks, there are these three lordships. Which three? Lordship over self, lordship over the world, and lordship over the Dhamma. And what, monks, is lordship over self? Here, monks, a monk gone to the forest, to the root of a tree, or to an empty dwelling reflects thus..." Thus was spoken by the Blessed One.

Brāhmaṇavagga, paribbājakasutta

The Chapter on Brahmins, The Discourse on the Wanderer

Pucchā – brāhmaṇavagge [M 1.0272] āvuso catutthaṃ paribbājakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: "Friend, concerning whom, on what subject, and in what manner was the fourth Discourse on the Wanderer spoken by the Blessed One in the Chapter on Brahmins?"

Vissajjanā – brāhmaṇavagge bhante catutthaṃ paribbājakasuttaṃ aññataraṃ brāhmaṇa paribbājakaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Aññataro bhante brāhmaṇaparibbājako bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “sanniṭṭhiko dhammo sandiṭṭhiko dhammoti bho gotama vuccati, kittāvatānu kho bho gotama sandiṭṭhiko dhammo hoti akāliko ehipassiko opaneyyiko paccattaṃ veditabbo viññūhī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “ratto kho brāhmaṇa rāgena abhibhūto pariyādinnacitto attabyābādhāyapi ceteti, parabyābādhāyapi ceteti, ubhaya byābādhāyapi ceteti, cetasikampi dukkhaṃ domanassaṃ paṭisaṃvedeti, rāge pahīne nevattabyābādhāyapi ceteti, naparabyābādhāyapi ceteti, naubhayabyābādhāyapi ceteti, nacetasikaṃ dukkhaṃ domanassaṃ paṭisaṃvedetī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: In the Chapter on Brahmins, venerable sir, the fourth is the Discourse on the Wanderer, spoken concerning a certain brahmin wanderer. A certain brahmin wanderer, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: "Master Gotama, it is said, ‘The Dhamma is immediate, the Dhamma is directly visible.’ To what extent, Master Gotama, is the Dhamma directly visible, timeless, inviting one to come and see, leading onward, to be personally experienced by the wise?" On that subject, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: "A brahmin, when overcome and obsessed by lust, with his mind consumed, intends harm for himself, intends harm for others, intends harm for both, and experiences mental pain and displeasure. When lust is abandoned, he neither intends harm for himself, nor intends harm for others, nor intends harm for both, and does not experience mental pain and displeasure."

Vacchagottasutta

The Vacchagotta Sutta

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0275] āvuso sattamaṃ vacchagottasuttaṃ bhagavatā kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: "Friend, there, concerning whom, on what subject, and in what manner was the seventh Vacchagotta Sutta spoken by the Blessed One?"

Vissajjanā – sattamaṃ bhante vacchagottasuttaṃ vacchagottaṃ paribbājakaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Vacchagotto bhante paribbājako bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “sutaṃ metaṃ bho gotama samaṇo gotamo evamāha “mayhameva dānaṃ dātabbaṃ, nāññesaṃ dānaṃ dātabba. (Peyyāla) anabbhakkhā tukāmāhi mayaṃ bhavantaṃ gotama”nti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “ye te vaccha evamāhaṃsu samaṇo gotamo evamāha mayhameva dānaṃ dātabbaṃ, nāññesaṃ dātabba”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, the seventh is the Vacchagotta Sutta, spoken concerning the wanderer Vacchagotta. The wanderer Vacchagotta, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: "Master Gotama, I have heard it said that the ascetic Gotama says, ‘Gifts should be given only to me, gifts should not be given to others.’ (Repetition) We do not wish to misrepresent the venerable Gotama." On that subject, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: "Those, Vaccha, who say, ‘The ascetic Gotama says, "Gifts should be given only to me, gifts should not be given to others,"’..."

Saṅgāravasutta

The Saṅgārava Discourse

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0278] āvuso dasamaṃ saṅgāravasuttaṃ bhagavatā kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: "Friend, concerning whom, on what topic, and in what context was the tenth Saṅgārava Discourse spoken by the Blessed One right there?"

Vissajjanā – dasamaṃ [M 1.0279] bhante saṅgāravasuttaṃ saṅgāravaṃ brāhmaṇaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Saṅgāravo bhante brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “mayamassu bho gotama brāhmaṇā nāma yaññaṃ yajāmapi yajāpemapi, tatra bho gotama yo ceva yajati yo ca yajāpeti, sabbe te anekasārīrikaṃ puññappaṭipadaṃ paṭipannā honti, yadidaṃ yaññādhikaraṇaṃ, yopanāyaṃ bho gotama yassa vā kulā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajito ekamattānaṃ dameti, ekamattānaṃ sameti, ekamattānaṃ parinibbāpeti, evamassāyaṃ ekasārīrikaṃ puññappaṭipadaṃ paṭipanno hoti, yadidaṃ pabbajjādhikaraṇa”nti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “tenahi brāhmaṇa taññevettha paṭipucchissāmi, yathā te khameyya, tathā naṃ byākareyyāsī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Tenth, venerable sir, the Saṅgārava Sutta was spoken concerning the brahmin Saṅgārava. Saṅgārava the brahmin, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: "We, Master Gotama, are brahmins who perform sacrifices and have sacrifices performed. In this regard, Master Gotama, both the one who sacrifices and the one who has sacrifices performed—all of them undertake a path of merit productive of much merit, namely, the undertaking of sacrifices. But, Master Gotama, whoever from any family has gone forth from the household life into homelessness to tame just one person, to calm just one person, to extinguish just one person, thus he undertakes a path of merit productive of little merit, namely, the undertaking of going forth." On this matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: "Well then, brahmin, I will question you on this very point. Answer as you see fit."

“Iccāyapi [M 1.0282] bho gotama evaṃ bhante anekasārīrikā puññappaṭipadā hoti” –

"Even so, Master Gotama, venerable sir, thus the path of merit is productive of much merit."

Pucchā – tattheva āvuso dutiya anusandhimhi bhagavatā kīdisī dhammadesanā desitā.

Question: "Right there, friend, in the second instance, what kind of Dhamma teaching was taught by the Blessed One?"

Vissajjanā – tattheva bhante dutiye anusandhimhi tividhā pāṭihāriyā paṭisaṃyuttā dhammadesanā bhagavatā desitā.

Answer: Right there, venerable sir, in the second instance, the Blessed One taught a Dhamma teaching connected with the threefold miracle.

“Seyyathāpi [M 1.0283] bhavaṃ gotamo bhavañcānando, ete me pujjā ete me pāsaṃsā” –

"Just as the venerable Gotama and the venerable Ananda, these are my venerated ones, these are my praised ones."

“Seyyathāpi bhavaṃ gotamo bhavañcānando, ete me pujjā ete me pāsaṃsā” –

"Just as the venerable Gotama and the venerable Ananda, these are my venerated ones, these are my praised ones."

Mahāvagga, venāgapurasutta

Mahāvagga, Venāgapura Sutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0287] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanimāte mahāvagge tatiyaṃ venāgapurasuttaṃ kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: "Friend, where, concerning whom, in what subject matter, and how was the third Venāgapura Sutta spoken by the Blessed One… by the Perfectly Enlightened One in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Tikanipāta, in the Mahāvagga?"

Vissajjanā – kosalesu bhante venāgapure nāma brāhmaṇānaṃ gāme venāgapurikaṃ vacchagottaṃ brāhmaṇaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Venāgapuriko bhante vacchagotto brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “acchariyaṃ bho gotama, abbhutaṃ bho gotama, yāvañcidaṃ bhoto gotamassa vippasannāni indriyāni, parisuddho chavivaṇṇo pariyodāto (peyyāla) evarūpānaṃ nūna bhavaṃ gotamo uccāsayana mahāsayanānaṃ nikāmalābhī akicchalābhī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “yāni kho pana tāni brāhmaṇa uccāsayana mahāsayanāni. Seyyathidaṃ, āsandi pallaṅko gonako cittako [M 1.0288] paṭikā paṭalikā tūlikā vikatikā uddalomī ekantalomī kaṭṭissaṃ koseyyaṃ kuṭṭakaṃ hatthattharaṃ assattharaṃ rathattharaṃ ajinappaveṇī kadalimigapavarapaccattharaṇaṃ sauttaracchadaṃ ubhatolohitakūpadhānaṃ. Dullabhāni tāni pabbajitānaṃ, laddhā ca pana nakappantī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, in the Kosala country, in a brahmin village named Venāgapura, concerning the brahmin Vacchagotta of Venāgapura, this was spoken. Venerable sir, Vacchagotta the brahmin of Venāgapura said this to the Blessed One: “It is amazing, Master Gotama! It is astounding, Master Gotama! How clear are Master Gotama’s senses, how pure and bright the color of his skin, so radiant! Surely Master Gotama is one who obtains at will, without trouble, such high and luxurious couches.” On that matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: “Those high and luxurious couches, brahmin, such as an armchair, a sofa, a woolen rug with long fleece, a variegated woolen rug, a white woolen rug, a woolen rug with figures of flowers, a mattress stuffed with cotton wool, a woolen rug with figures of lions and tigers, a woolen rug with hair on both sides, a woolen rug with hair on one side, a silk rug, a silk coverlet, a large woolen rug, an elephant covering, a horse covering, a chariot covering, an antelope-hide rug, a choice deer-hide rug, with a canopy, with red cushions at both ends—such things are difficult for renunciates to obtain, and when obtained, are not proper.”

“Acchariyaṃ bho gotama abbhutaṃ bho gotama”.

It is amazing, Master Gotama! It is astounding, Master Gotama!

Dibba uccāsayanamahāsayana

The Divine High and Luxurious Couch

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0290] tattha bhagavatā dibbaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ desitaṃ.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One teach about the divine high and luxurious couch there?

Vissajjanā – idhāhaṃ brāhmaṇa yaṃ gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā upanissāya viharāmi, so pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya tameva gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya pavisāmi, so pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto vanantaññeva pavisāmīti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā dibbaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ desitaṃ.

Reply: Here, brahmin, when I dwell dependent on a village or town, in the morning I dress, take my bowl and robe, and enter that very village or town for alms. After the meal, returning from the alms round, I enter the forest. In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One described the divine high and luxurious couch there.

Brahma uccāsayanamahāsayana

The Brahma High and Luxurious Couch

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso tattha bhagavatā brahmaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ desitaṃ.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One teach about the Brahma high and luxurious couch there?

Vissajjanā – idhāhaṃ brāhmaṇaṃ yaṃ gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā upanissāya viharāmi, so pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya tameva gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ [M 1.0293] vā piṇḍāya pavisāmīti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā brahmauccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ desitaṃ.

Reply: Here, brahmin, dwelling dependent on a village or town, in the morning I dress, take my bowl and robe, and enter that very village or town for alms. In this way, venerable sir, the Blessed One taught about the Brahma high and luxurious couch there.

Katamaṃ pana taṃ bho gotama brahmauccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ.

But what, Master Gotama, is that Brahma high and luxurious couch?

Ariya uccāsayanamahāsayana

The Noble High and Great Couch

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0294] tattha bhagavatā ariyaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ desitaṃ.

Question: And how, friend, did the Blessed One teach the noble high and great couch there?

Vissajjanā – idhāhaṃ [M 1.0295] brāhmaṇa yaṃ gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā upanissāya viharāmi, so pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya tameva gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya pavisāmi. So pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapāta paṭikkanto vanantaññeva pavisāmīti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā ariyaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ desitaṃ.

Reply: Here, brahmin, I dwell dependent on a certain village or town. In the morning, having dressed and taken my bowl and robe, I enter that same village or town for alms. After the meal, returning from the alms round, I enter the forest. In this way, venerable sir, the noble high and great couch was taught there by the Blessed One.

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0297] āvuso mahāvagge pañcamaṃ kesamuttisuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, in that same Mahāvagga, where, concerning whom, on what occasion, and how was the fifth Kesamutti Sutta spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – kosalesu bhante kesamuttenāma kālāmānaṃ nigame kesamuttiye kālāme ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Kesamuttiyā bhante kālāmā bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ “santi bhante eke samaṇa brāhmaṇā kesamuttaṃ āgacchanti, te sakaṃyeva vādaṃ dīpenti, jotenti, parappavādaṃ pana khuṃsenti vambhenti paribhavanti, omakkhiṃ karonti, aparepi bhante eke samaṇabrāhmaṇā kesamuttaṃ āgacchanti, tepi sakaṃyeva vādaṃ dīpenti jotenti, parappavādaṃ pana khuṃsenti vambhenti paribhavanti, omakkhiṃ karonti. Tesaṃ no bhante amhākaṃ hoteva kaṅkhā, hoti vicikicchā ko su nāma imesaṃ bhavataṃ samaṇabrāhmaṇānaṃ saccaṃ āha, ko musā”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “alañhi vo kālāmā kaṅkhituṃ alaṃ vicikicchituṃ, kaṅkhīyeva pana vo ṭhāne vicikicchā uppannā”ti eva mādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was spoken concerning the Kālāmas in the Kosala country, at the Kālāmas' town named Kesamutta. Venerable sir, the Kālāmas of Kesamutta said this to the Blessed One: "Venerable sir, some ascetics and brahmins come to Kesamutta. They expound and illuminate only their own doctrine, but they disparage, revile, despise, and treat with contempt the doctrine of others. Others too, venerable sir, some ascetics and brahmins come to Kesamutta. They also expound and illuminate only their own doctrine, but they disparage, revile, despise, and treat with contempt the doctrine of others. For us, venerable sir, there is doubt and uncertainty: which of these venerable ascetics and brahmins speaks the truth, and which speaks falsehood?" On that occasion, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke with words beginning: "It is fitting for you to doubt, Kālāmas, it is fitting for you to be uncertain. Doubt has arisen in you about a matter that invites doubt."

Uposatasutta

The Uposatha Sutta

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0303] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte mahāvagge dasamaṃ uposathasuttaṃ kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, by the Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Tika Nipāta, the Mahāvagga, the tenth Uposatha Sutta—where, concerning whom, and how was it spoken?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ ārabbha “tayo kho me visākhe uposathā. Katame tayo. Gopālakuposatho nigaṇṭhuposatho ariyuposatho”ti evaṃ kho bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, in Sāvatthī, concerning Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, it was spoken thus by the Blessed One: "Visākhā, there are these three Uposathas. What three? The cowherd’s Uposatha, the Nigaṇṭha’s Uposatha, and the noble Uposatha."

Handa kuto nu tvaṃ visākhe āgacchasi divā divassa.

Well now, Visākhā, where have you come from in the middle of the day?

“Tayo [M 1.0304] kho me visākhe uposathā. Katame tayo, gopālakuposatho nigaṇṭhuposatho ariyuposatho” –

Visākhā, there are these three Uposathas. What three? The cowherd's Uposatha, the Nigaṇṭha's Uposatha, and the noble Uposatha.

Gopālakaupuggala

The Cowherd

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso tattha bhagavatā gopālakuposatho pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the cowherd's Uposatha expounded by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – kathañca visākhe gopālakuposatho hoti, seyyathāpi visākhe gopālako sāyanhasamaye sāmikānaṃ gāvo niyyātetvā iti paṭisañcikkhati “ajja kho gāvo amukasmiñca amukasmiñca padese cariṃsu, amukasmiñca amukasmiñca padese pānīyāni piviṃsu.

Reply: And how, Visākhā, is the cowherd’s Uposatha? Just as, Visākhā, a cowherd in the evening, having returned the cows to their owners, reflects thus: "Today the cows grazed in such and such places, and drank water in such and such places.

Sve [M 1.0305] dāni gāvo amukasmiñca amukasmiñca padese carissanti, amukasmiñca amukasmiñca padese pānīyāni pivissantī”ti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā gopālakuposatho pakāsito.

Tomorrow the cows will graze in such and such places, and drink water in such and such places.' In this way, venerable sir, the cowherd’s Uposatha was expounded by the Blessed One there.

Nigaṇṭhaupuggala

The Nigaṇṭha

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0306] tattha bhagavatā nigaṇṭhuposatho pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the Nigaṇṭha’s Uposatha expounded by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – kathañca visākhe nigaṇṭhuposatho hoti, atthi visākhe nigaṇṭhānāma samaṇajātikā, te sāvakaṃ evaṃ samādapenti, “ehitvaṃ ambhopurisa ye puratthimāya disāya pāṇā paraṃ yojanasataṃ, tesu daṇḍaṃ nikkhipāhi, ye pacchimāya disāya. Ye uttarāya disāya. Ye dakkhiṇāya disāya pāṇā paraṃ yojanasataṃ, tesu daṇḍaṃ nikkhipāhī”ti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā nigaṇṭhuposatho pakāsito.

Reply: And how, Visākhā, is the Nigaṇṭha’s Uposatha? There are, Visākhā, a kind of ascetic called Nigaṇṭhas. They exhort their disciples thus: "Come, good man, lay down the rod towards beings living more than a hundred leagues to the east; lay down the rod towards beings living more than a hundred leagues to the west; lay down the rod towards beings living more than a hundred leagues to the north; lay down the rod towards beings living more than a hundred leagues to the south." In this way, venerable sir, the Nigaṇṭha’s Uposatha was expounded by the Blessed One there.

Ehi [M 1.0307] tvaṃ ambho purisa.

Come, good man.

Ehi tvaṃ ambho purisa sabbacelāni nikkhipitvā evaṃ vadehi.

Come, good man, put aside all your garments and speak thus.

Nāhaṃ [M 1.0308] kvacani kassaci kiñcanatasmiṃ, na ca mama kvacani katthaci kiñcana tatthi.

I am not anyone's possession in any way, nor is there anything of mine anywhere.

Ariyā uposatho

The Noble Uposatha

Pucchā – kathañcāvuso [M 1.0309] tattha bhagavatā ariyuposatho vitthārena vibhajitvā pakāsito.

Question: And how, friend, was the noble Uposatha expounded, analyzed, and explained in detail by the Blessed One there?

Vissajjanā – kathañca visākhe ariyuposatho hoti, upakkiliṭṭhassa visākhe cittassa upakkamena pariyodapanā hoti. Kathañca visākhe upakkiliṭṭhassa cittassa upakkamena pariyodapanā hoti. Idha visākhe ariyasāvako tathāgataṃ anussarati “itipi so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho vijjācaraṇasampanno sugato lokavidū anuttaro purisadammasārathi satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā”ti. Tassa tathāgataṃ anussarato cittaṃ pasīdati, pāmojjaṃ uppajjati, ye cittassa upakkilesā te pahīyantīti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā ariyo uposatho vitthāretvā pakāsito.

The Reply – And how, Visākhā, is the noble Uposatha? It is the cleansing of a defiled mind through effort. And how, Visākhā, is the cleansing of a defiled mind through effort? Here, Visākhā, a noble disciple recollects the Tathāgata: "Indeed, the Blessed One is an Arahant, Perfectly Self-Enlightened, accomplished in true knowledge and conduct, fortunate, knower of the world, unsurpassed trainer of persons to be tamed, teacher of devas and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One." As he recollects the Tathāgata, his mind becomes tranquil, joy arises, and whatever defilements of the mind there are, these are abandoned. Thus, venerable sir, the noble Uposatha was expounded and elucidated in detail by the Blessed One there.

Pucchā – evaṃ [M 1.0316] upavutthassa pana āvuso ariyuposathassa kathaṃ mahapphalatā mahānisaṃsatā vuttā bhagavatā.

The Question – And how, friend, has the Blessed One described the great fruit and great benefit of the noble Uposatha when it has been undertaken in this way?

Vissajjanā – kīvamahapphalā hoti, kīvamahānisaṃso, kīvamahājutiko, kīvamahāvipphāro, seyyathāpi visākhe yo imesaṃ soḷasannaṃ mahājanapadānaṃ pahūtarattajanapadānaṃ issariyādhipaccaṃ rajjaṃ kāreyya, seyyathidaṃ “aṅgānaṃ magadhānaṃ kāsīnaṃ kosalānaṃ vajjīnaṃ mallānaṃ cetīnaṃ vaṅgānaṃ kurūnaṃ pañcālānaṃ macchānaṃ sūrasenānaṃ assakānaṃ avantīnaṃ gandhārānaṃ kambojānaṃ, aṭṭhaṅgasamannāgatassa uposathassa etaṃ kalaṃ nāgghati soḷasi”nti evamādinā bhante tattha bhagavatā evaṃ upavutthassa ariyuposathassa mahapphalatā mahānisaṃsatā vuttā.

The Reply – How fruitful it is, how greatly beneficial, how radiant, how far-reaching in its effects! Just as if, Visākhā, one were to exercise sovereignty and kingship over these sixteen great countries abounding in wealth and people, namely: the Aṅgas, the Magadhans, the Kāsīs, the Kosalans, the Vajjis, the Mallas, the Cetīs, the Vaṅgas, the Kurus, the Pañcālas, the Macchas, the Sūrasenas, the Assakas, the Avantis, the Gandhārans, and the Kambojans—this is not worth a sixteenth part of the Uposatha observance endowed with eight factors. Thus, venerable sir, the Blessed One has spoken of the great fruitfulness and great benefit of the noble Uposatha observance when undertaken in this way there.

Ānandavagga

The Chapter on Ānanda

Ājīvakasutta

The Discourse on the Ājīvaka

Pucchā – aṅguttaranikāye [M 1.0319] āvuso tikanipāte ānandavagge dutiyaṃ ājīvakasuttaṃ kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kena kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of the Threes, in the Ānanda Chapter, the second Discourse on the Ājīvaka: where, concerning whom, in what context, by whom, and how was it spoken?

Vissajjanā – kosambiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ ājīvakasāvakaṃ gahapatiṃ ārabbha āyasmatā ānandattherena dhammabhaṇḍāgārikena bhāsitaṃ. Aññataro bhante ājīvakasāvako gahapati āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ etadavoca “kesaṃ no bhante ānanda dhammo svākkhāto, ke loke suppaṭipannā, ke loke sukatā”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “tena hi gahapati taññevettha paṭipucchissāmi, yathā te khameyya, tathā naṃ byākareyyāsī”ti evamādinā āyasmatā ānandattherena dhammabhaṇḍāgārikena bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, this was spoken by the Venerable Ānanda, the treasurer of the Dhamma, concerning a certain householder in Kosambī who was a disciple of the Ājīvakas. Venerable sir, a certain householder, a disciple of the Ājīvakas, said this to the Venerable Ānanda: "Venerable Ānanda, whose Dhamma is well-proclaimed? Who in the world are practicing well? Who in the world have done well?" Venerable sir, in that connection, the Venerable Ānanda, the treasurer of the Dhamma, spoke beginning with this statement: "Well then, householder, I will question you on this very matter. Answer as seems fit to you."

“Kesaṃ [M 1.0321] no bhante ānanda dhammo svākkhāto, ke loke suppaṭipannā, ke loke sukatā”.

"Venerable Ānanda, whose Dhamma is well-proclaimed? Who in the world are practicing well? Who in the world have done well?"

“Taṃ [M 1.0322] kiṃ maññasi gahapati” –

"What do you think, householder?"

“Iti [M 1.0323] kho gahapati tayāvetaṃ byākataṃ” –

"So, householder, this is how you have explained it."

“Abhikkantaṃ [M 1.0324] bhante, abhikkantaṃ bhante, seyyathāpi bhante nikkujjitaṃ vā ukkujjeyya” –

"Excellent, venerable sir! Excellent, venerable sir! Just as, venerable sir, one might set upright what had been overturned—"

Gandhajātasutta

The Discourse on Kinds of Fragrance

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0325] āvuso navamaṃ gandhajātasuttaṃ bhagavatā kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – Friend, there, in that very place, the ninth Discourse on Kinds of Fragrance: concerning whom, on what subject, and how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – āyasmantaṃ bhante ānandaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Āyasmā bhante ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “tīṇimāni bhante gandhajātāni, yesaṃ anuvātaṃyeva gandho gacchati no paṭivātaṃ…pe… atthi nu kho bhante kiñci gandhajātaṃ, yassa anuvātampi gandho gacchati paṭivātampi gandho gacchati, anuvātapaṭivātampi gandho gacchatī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “atthānanda kiñci gandhajātaṃ, yassa anuvātampi gandho gacchati, paṭivātampi gandho gacchati, anuvātapaṭivātampi gandho gacchatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – It was spoken concerning the Venerable Ānanda. Venerable sir, the Venerable Ānanda said this to the Blessed One: "Venerable sir, there are these three kinds of fragrances whose scent travels only with the wind and not against it [...]. Is there, venerable sir, any kind of fragrance whose scent travels with the wind, against the wind, and both with and against the wind?" On this matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning with this statement: "There is, Ānanda, a kind of fragrance whose scent travels with the wind, against the wind, and both with and against the wind."

Na [M 1.0327] pupphagandho paṭivātameti;

Na candanaṃ tagaramallikā vā;

Satañca gandho paṭivātameti;

Sabbā disā sappuriso pavāyati;

The scent of a flower does not travel against the wind, nor sandalwood, tagara, or jasmine; but the scent of the virtuous travels against the wind; the good person pervades all directions.

Gadrabhasutta

The Discourse on the Donkey

Pucchā – samaṇavagge [M 1.0328] āvuso dutiyaṃ gadrabhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – Friend, how was the second Discourse on the Donkey in the Chapter on Ascetics spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – samaṇavagge bhante dutiyaṃ gadrabhasuttaṃ “seyyathāpi bhikkhave gadrabho gogaṇaṃ piṭṭhito piṭṭhito anubandho hoti ‘ahampi dammo ahampi dammo’ti, tassa na tādiso vaṇṇo hoti seyyathāpi gunnaṃ, na tādiso saro hoti seyyathāpi gunnaṃ, na tādisaṃ padaṃ hoti seyyathāpi gunnaṃ, so gogaṇaṃyeva piṭṭhito piṭṭhito anubandho hoti ahampi dammo ahampi dammo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – In the Chapter on Ascetics, venerable sir, the second Discourse on the Donkey was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with this statement: "Monks, just as a donkey follows behind a herd of cows, thinking, 'I too am an ox, I too am an ox,' yet it does not have the same color as the cows, nor the same sound as the cows, nor the same gait as the cows. It simply follows behind the herd of cows, thinking, 'I too am an ox, I too am an ox.'"

Tasmātiha [M 1.0329] bhikkhave evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ.

Therefore, monks, you should train yourselves in this way.

“Tibbo no chando bhavissati adhisīlasikkhāsamādāne…pe… adhipaññāsikkhāsamādāne” –

"Our desire will be intense to undertake the training in higher virtue [...] the training in higher wisdom—"

Saṅkavāsutta

The Discourse at Saṅkavā

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0330] āvuso ekādasamaṃ saṅkavāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – Friend, there, in that very place, the eleventh Discourse at Saṅkavā: where, concerning whom, on what subject, and how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – kosalesu bhante saṅkavāyaṃ nāma kosalānaṃ nigame kassapagottaṃ nāma bhikkhuṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Kassapa gotto bhante bhikkhu bhagavati manopadūsitvā bhagavato santike accayaṃ accayato desesi. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “thero cepi kassapa bhikkhu na sikkhākāmo na sikkhāsamādānassa vaṇṇavādī. Ye caññe bhikkhū na sikkhākāmā, te ca na sikkhāya samādapeti, ye caññe bhikkhū sikkhākāmā tesañca na vaṇṇaṃ bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālenā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, in the Kosalan town named Saṅkavā, it was spoken concerning a monk named Kassapa of the Kassapa clan.
