Venerable sir, the monk Kassapa of the Kassapa clan, having mentally offended the Blessed One, confessed his transgression to the Blessed One. In this matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning with this statement: "Even if the elder monk Kassapa does not desire training and does not speak in praise of undertaking the training; and he does not encourage other monks who do not desire training to train; and of those other monks who do desire training, he does not speak praise, truthful and factual, at the right time."

Loṇakapallavagga

The Chapter on the Lump of Salt

Accāyikasutta

The Discourse on Urgency

Pucchā – tenāvuso…pe… [M 1.0334] sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte loṇakapallavagge paṭhamaṃ accāyikasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – Friend, [...] how was the first Discourse on Urgency spoken by the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of the Threes, in the Chapter on the Lump of Salt?

Vissajjanā – loṇakapallavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ accāyikasuttaṃ “tīṇimāni bhikkhave kassakassa gahapatissa accāyikāni karaṇīyāni. Katamāni tīṇi, idha bhikkhave kassako gahapati sīghaṃ sīghaṃ khettaṃ sukaṭṭhaṃ karoti sumatikataṃ. Sīghaṃ sīghaṃ khettaṃ sukaṭṭhaṃ karitvā sumatikataṃ sīghaṃ sīghaṃ bījāni patiṭṭhāpeti. Sīghaṃ sīghaṃ bījāni [M 1.0335] patiṭṭhāpetvā sīghaṃ sīghaṃ udakaṃ abhinetipi apanetipi. Imāni kho bhikkhave tīṇi kassakassa gahapatissa accāyikāni karaṇīyāni”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

The Reply – In the Chapter on the Lump of Salt, venerable sir, the first Discourse on Urgency was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with this statement: "Monks, there are these three urgent duties of a farmer householder. What are the three? Here, monks, a farmer householder swiftly and thoroughly cultivates the field, making it well-prepared. Having swiftly and thoroughly cultivated the field and made it well-prepared, he swiftly plants the seeds. Having swiftly planted the seeds, he swiftly brings water in and drains it off. These, monks, are the three urgent duties of a farmer householder."

Sambodhavagga

The Chapter on Awakening

Ruṇṇasutta

The Discourse on Weeping

Pucchā – sambodhavagge [M 1.0337] panāvuso pañcamaṃ ruṇṇasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

The Question – Friend, in the Chapter on Awakening, how was the fifth discourse, the Ruṇṇa Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sambodhavagge bhante pañcamaṃ ruṇṇasuttaṃ “ruṇṇamidaṃ bhikkhave ariyassa vinaye yadidaṃ gītaṃ, ummattakamidaṃ bhikkhave ariyassa vinaye yadidaṃ naccaṃ, komārakamidaṃ bhikkhave ariyassa vinaye yadidaṃ ativelaṃ dantavidaṃsakahasitaṃ. Tasmātiha bhikkhave setughāto gīte setughāto nacce, alaṃ vo dhammappamoditānaṃ sataṃ sitaṃ sitamattāyā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Awakening, the fifth discourse is the Ruṇṇa Sutta: “Monks, this singing is weeping in the Noble One’s Discipline. Monks, this dancing is madness in the Noble One’s Discipline. Monks, this excessive laughter, showing the teeth, is childish in the Noble One’s Discipline. Therefore, monks, put a stop to singing, put a stop to dancing. It is enough for you who delight in the Dhamma to smile a little.” Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Arakkhīkasutta

The Discourse on the Unguarded

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0338] āvuso sattamaṃ arakkhitasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, there, where, concerning whom, and how was the seventh discourse, the Arakkhita Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante anātapiṇḍikaṃ gahapatiṃ ārabbha “citte gahapati arakkhite kāyakammampi arakkhitaṃ hoti, vacīkammampi. Manokammampi arakkhitaṃ hoti. Tassa arakkhitakāyakammantassa arakkhitavacīkammantassa arakkhitamanokammantassa kāyakammampi avassutaṃ [M 1.0339] hoti, vacīkammampi avassutaṃ hoti, manokammampi avassutaṃ hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, with reference to the householder Anāthapiṇḍika, the Blessed One spoke thus: “Householder, when the mind is unguarded, bodily action is also unguarded, verbal action is also unguarded, and mental action is also unguarded. For one whose bodily action is unguarded, whose verbal action is unguarded, and whose mental action is unguarded, bodily action is defiled, verbal action is defiled, and mental action is defiled.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Āpāyikavagga

The Chapter on the States of Woe

Apaṇṇakasutta

The Discourse on the Incontrovertible

Pucchā – āpāyikavagge [M 1.0341] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ apaṇṇakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, how was the sixth discourse, the Apaṇṇaka Sutta, in the Chapter on the States of Woe, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – āpāyikavagge bhante chaṭṭhaṃ apaṇṇakasuttaṃ “tisso imā bhikkhave vipattiyo. Katamā tisso sīlavipatti, cittavipatti, diṭṭhivipatti. Katamā ca bhikkhave sīlavipatti, idha bhikkhave ekacco pāṇātipātī hoti…pe… samphappalāpī hoti. Ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave sīlavipattī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the States of Woe, the sixth discourse is the Apaṇṇaka Sutta: “Monks, there are these three kinds of downfall. What three? Downfall in moral conduct, downfall in mind, and downfall in view. And what, monks, is downfall in moral conduct? Here, monks, someone destroys living beings… indulges in idle chatter. This, monks, is called downfall in moral conduct.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Kusināravagga

The Chapter on Kusinārā

Kusinārasutta

The Discourse at Kusinārā

Pucchā – kusināravagge [M 1.0344] panāvuso paṭhamaṃ kusinārasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, in the Chapter on Kusinārā, where, concerning whom, and how was the first discourse, the Kusinārā Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – kusinārāyaṃ bhante baliharaṇe vanasaṇḍe sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha “idha bhikkhave bhikkhu aññataraṃ gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā upanissāya viharati, tamenaṃ gahapati vā gahapatiputto vā upasaṅkamitvā svātanāya bhattena nimantetī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, at Kusinārā, in the Baliharaṇa grove, concerning many monks, the Blessed One spoke thus: “Here, monks, a monk dwells depending on a certain village or town. Then a householder or a householder’s son approaches him and invites him for tomorrow’s meal.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Hatthakasutta

The Discourse concerning Hatthaka

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0347] āvuso pañcamaṃ hatthakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kena saddhiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, there, where, with whom, and how was the fifth discourse, the Hatthaka Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante hatthakena devaputtena saddhiṃ “ye te hatthaka dhammā pubbe manussabhūtassa pavattino ahesuṃ, apinu te te dhammā etarahi pavattino”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, with the devaputta Hatthaka, the Blessed One spoke thus: “Hatthaka, those qualities that were manifest in you previously when you were a human being—are those same qualities still manifest now?” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Anuruddhasutta

The Discourse concerning Anuruddha

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0350] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ anuruddhasuttaṃ kaṃ ārabbha kena kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, there, concerning whom, by whom, and how was the eighth discourse, the Anuruddha Sutta, spoken?

Vissajjanā – aṭṭhamaṃ bhante anuruddhasuttaṃ āyasmantaṃ anuruddhattheraṃ ārabbha āyasmatā sāriputtattherena dhammasenāpatinā “yaṃ kho te āvuso anuruddhaṃ evaṃ hoti ‘ahaṃ dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena sahassaṃ lokaṃ volokemī’ti, idaṃ te mānasmiṃ”ti evamādinā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, the eighth discourse, the Anuruddha Sutta, concerning the Venerable Elder Anuruddha, was spoken by the Venerable Sāriputta, the General of the Dhamma, beginning thus: “Friend Anuruddha, that it occurs to you thus: ‘With the divine eye, purified and surpassing the human, I survey a thousand worlds’—this is a matter of your conceit.”

“Sādhu [M 1.0352] vatāyasmā anuruddho ime tayo dhamme pahāya ime tayo dhamme amanasikaritvā amatāya dhātuyā cittaṃ upasaṃharatu” –

“It would be good, Venerable Anuruddha, if you were to abandon these three qualities, not attend to these three qualities, and direct your mind to the deathless element.”

Kusināravagga

The Chapter on Kusinārā

Paṭicchannasutta

The Discourse on the Concealed

Pucchā – tenāvuso [M 1.0353] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye tikanipāte kusināravagge navamaṃ paṭicchannasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, how was the ninth discourse, the Paṭicchanna Sutta, in the Chapter on Kusinārā, in the Book of Threes of the Aṅguttara Nikāya, spoken by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – navamaṃ bhante paṭicchannasuttaṃ “tīṇimāni bhikkhave paṭicchannāni āvahanti no vivaṭāni. Katamāni tīṇi, mātugāmo bhikkhave paṭicchanno āvahati no vivaṭo, brāhmaṇānaṃ bhikkhave mantā paṭicchannā āvahanti no vivaṭā, micchādiṭṭhi bhikkhave paṭicchannā āvahati no vivaṭā. Imāni kho bhikkhave tīṇi paṭicchannāni āvahanti no vivaṭānī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, the ninth discourse is the Paṭicchanna Sutta: “Monks, these three things thrive when concealed, not when revealed. What three? Womankind, monks, thrives when concealed, not when revealed. The brahmins’ sacred verses, monks, thrive when concealed, not when revealed. Wrong view, monks, thrives when concealed, not when revealed. These, monks, are the three things that thrive when concealed, not when revealed.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Lekhasutta

The Discourse on an Inscription

Pucchā – tattheva [M 1.0355] āvuso dasamaṃ lekhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, how was the tenth discourse, the Lekha Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One in that same context?

Vissajjanā – dasamaṃ bhante lekhasuttaṃ “tayome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo, pāsāṇalekhūpamo puggalo pathavilekhūpamo puggalo udakalekhūpamo puggalo. Katamo ca bhikkhave pāsāṇa lekhūpamo puggalo, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati, so ca khvassa kodho dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Seyyathāpi bhikkhave pāsāṇalekhā na khippaṃ lujjati vātena vā udakena vā, ciraṭṭhitikā hoti, evameva kho bhikkhave idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati, so ca khvassa kodho dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave pāsāṇalekhūpamo puggalo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, the tenth discourse is the Lekha Sutta: “Monks, these three kinds of persons are found existing in the world. What three? The person similar to an inscription on rock, the person similar to an inscription on earth, and the person similar to an inscription on water. And what, monks, is the person similar to an inscription on rock? Here, monks, a certain person is frequently angry, and that anger of his persists for a long time. Just as, monks, an inscription on rock is not quickly effaced by wind or water but is long-lasting, so too, monks, a certain person here is frequently angry, and that anger of his persists for a long time. This, monks, is called the person similar to an inscription on rock.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Yodhājīvavagga

The Chapter on Warriors

Kesakambalasutta

The Discourse on the Hair Blanket

Pucchā – yodhājīvavagge [M 1.0358] pana āvuso pañcamaṃ kesakambalasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question – Friend, in the Chapter on Warriors, how was the fifth discourse, the Kesakambala Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – yodhājīvavagge bhante pañcamaṃ kesakambalasuttaṃ “seyyathāpi bhikkhave yāni kānici tantāvutānaṃ vatthānaṃ, kesakambalo tesaṃ paṭikiṭṭho akkhāyati. Kesakambalo bhikkhave sīte sīto uṇhe uṇho dubbaṇṇo duggandho dukkhasamphasso. Evameva kho bhikkhave yānikānici puthusamaṇabrāhmaṇavādānaṃ, makkhalivādo tesaṃ paṭikiṭṭho akkhāyatīti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Warriors, the fifth discourse is the Kesakambala Sutta: “Monks, just as, of all woven cloths, a hair blanket is said to be the most despised—a hair blanket, monks, is cold in the cold, hot in the heat, ill-colored, foul-smelling, and unpleasant to the touch—so too, monks, of all the doctrines of the various ascetics and brahmins, the doctrine of Makkhali is said to be the most despised.” Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One, beginning with these words.

Maṅgalavagga

The Chapter on Blessings

Vandanāsutta

The Discourse on Homage

Pucchā – maṅgalavagge [M 1.0360] panāvuso navamaṃ vandanāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, in the Chapter on Blessings, how was the ninth Discourse on Homage spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – maṅgalavagge bhante navamaṃ vandanāsuttaṃ “tisso imā bhikkhave vandanā. Katamā tisso, kāyena vācāya manasā. Imā kho bhikkhave tisso vandanā”ti, evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Blessings, the ninth Discourse on Homage was spoken by the Blessed One thus: "Bhikkhus, these are three kinds of homage. What three? Homage by body, by speech, and by mind. These, bhikkhus, are the three kinds of homage." Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

“Tisso imā bhikkhave vandanā. Katamā tisso, kāyena vācāya manasā. Imā kho bhikkhave tisso vandanā”.

Bhikkhus, these are three kinds of homage. What three? Homage by body, by speech, and by mind. These, bhikkhus, are the three kinds of homage.

Pubbaṇhasutta

The Discourse on the Forenoon

Pucchā – tattho [M 1.0361] āvuso dasamaṃ pubbaṇhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, how was the tenth Discourse on the Forenoon spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – dasamaṃ bhante pubbaṇhasuttaṃ “ye bhikkhave sattā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ kāyena sucaritaṃ caranti, vācāya sucaritaṃ caranti, manasā sucaritaṃ caranti. Supubbaṇho bhikkhave tesaṃ sattānaṃ. Ye bhikkhave sattā majjhanhikasamayaṃ…pe… sāyanhasamayaṃ kāyena sucaritaṃ caranti, vācāya sucaritaṃ caranti, manasā sucaritaṃ caranti. Susāyanho bhikkhave tesaṃ sattāna”nti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, the tenth Discourse on the Forenoon was spoken by the Blessed One thus: "Bhikkhus, those beings who at forenoon practice good conduct by body, speech, and mind—for them, bhikkhus, the forenoon is auspicious. Those beings who at midday... in the evening practice good conduct by body, speech, and mind—for them, bhikkhus, the evening is auspicious." Thus, and so on, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Sunakkhattaṃ [M 1.0362] sumaṅgalaṃ, suppabhātaṃ suhuṭṭhitaṃ;

Sukhaṇo sumuhutto ca, suyiṭṭhaṃ brahmacārisu.

Auspicious is the constellation, fortunate the blessing, radiant the dawn, well-risen the morn; a good moment, a good instant, and well-offered to those who lead the holy life.

Bhaṇḍagāmavagga

The Chapter on Bhaṇḍagāma

Anubuddhasutta

The Discourse on Comprehension

Pucchā – catukkanipāte [M 2.0001] pana āvuso bhaṇḍagāmavagge paṭhamaṃ anubuddhasuttaṃ bhagavatā sattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, in the Chapter on Bhaṇḍagāma in the Book of Fours, concerning whom and how was the first Discourse on Comprehension spoken by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – vajjīsu bhante bhaṇḍagāme sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha “catunnaṃ bhikkhave dhammānaṃ ananubodhā appaṭivedhā evamidaṃ dīghamaddhānaṃ sandhāvitaṃ saṃsaritaṃ mamañceva tumhākañca. Katamesaṃ catunnaṃ, ariyassa bhikkhave sīlassa ananubodhā appaṭivedhā evamidaṃ dīghamaddhānaṃ sandhāvitaṃ saṃsaritaṃ mamañceva tumhākañca. Ariyassa bhikkhave samādhissa…. Ariyāya bhikkhave paññāya…. Ariyāya bhikkhave vimuttiyā ananubodhā appaṭivedhā evamidaṃ dīghamaddhānaṃ sandhāvitaṃ saṃsaritaṃ mamañceva tumhākañcā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, concerning a number of bhikkhus at Bhaṇḍagāma in the Vajji country, the Blessed One spoke thus: "Bhikkhus, it is because of not comprehending, not penetrating four things, that this long course of wandering and transmigration has been continued for me as well as for you. What four? Because of not comprehending, not penetrating noble virtue... noble concentration... noble wisdom... noble liberation, this long course of wandering and transmigration has been continued for me as well as for you." Thus, and so on, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Appassutasutta

The Discourse on One of Little Learning

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0003] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ appassutasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, how was the sixth Discourse on One of Little Learning spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vi – tattha bhante chaṭṭhaṃ appassutasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, appassuto sutena anupapanno, appassuto sutena upapanno, bahussuto sutena anupapanno, bahussuto sutena upapanno”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, there the sixth Discourse on One of Little Learning was spoken by the Blessed One thus: "Bhikkhus, these four persons are found existing in the world. What four? One of little learning who does not accord with what he has heard; one of little learning who accords with what he has heard; one of much learning who does not accord with what he has heard; and one of much learning who accords with what he has heard." Thus, and so on, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Caravagga

The Chapter on Wandering

Carasutta

The Discourse on Walking

Pu – caravagge [M 2.0007] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ carasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, in the Chapter on Wandering, how was the first Discourse on Walking spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – caravagge bhante paṭhamaṃ carasuttaṃ “carato cepi bhikkhave bhikkhuno uppajjati kāmavitakko vā byāpādavitakko vā vihiṃsāvibhatto vā, taṃ ce bhikkhu adhivāseti nappajahati na vinodeti na byantīkaroti na anabhāvaṃ gametī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Wandering, the first Discourse on Walking was spoken by the Blessed One thus: “Bhikkhus, if, while a bhikkhu is walking, a thought of sensuality, a thought of ill will, or a thought of violence arises, and he tolerates it, does not abandon it, does not dispel it, does not make an end of it, and does not bring it to a state of non-existence…” Thus, and so on, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Uruvelavagga

The Chapter on Uruvelā

Lokasutta

The Discourse on the World

Pu – tenāvuso…pe… [M 2.0009] sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye catukkanipāte uruvelavagge tatiyaṃ lokasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Question: Friend, …pe… how was the third Discourse on the World in the Chapter on Uruvelā of the Aṅguttara Nikāya, the Book of Fours, spoken by the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vi – uruvelavagge bhante tatiyaṃ lokasuttaṃ “loko bhikkhave tathāgatena abhisambuddho, lokasmā tathāgato visaṃyutto, lokasamudayo bhikkhave tathāgatena abhisambuddho, lokasamudayo tathāgatassa pahīno, lokanirodho bhikkhave tathāgatena abhisambuddho lokanirodho tathāgatassa sacchikato, lokanirodhagāminī paṭipadā bhikkhave tathāgatena abhisambuddhā, lokanirodhagāmini paṭipadā tathāgatassa bhāvitā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Response: Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Uruvelā, the third Discourse on the World was spoken by the Blessed One thus: “Bhikkhus, the world has been fully comprehended by the Tathāgata; the Tathāgata is disjoined from the world. The origin of the world, bhikkhus, has been fully comprehended by the Tathāgata; the origin of the world has been abandoned by the Tathāgata. The cessation of the world, bhikkhus, has been fully comprehended by the Tathāgata; the cessation of the world has been realized by the Tathāgata. The path leading to the cessation of the world, bhikkhus, has been fully comprehended by the Tathāgata; the path leading to the cessation of the world has been developed by the Tathāgata.” Thus, and so on, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Danto [M 2.0012] damayataṃ seṭṭho,

Santo samayataṃ isi;

Mutto mocayataṃ aggo,

Tiṇṇo tārayataṃ varo–

The tamed is the best among those who tame, the peaceful one is the sage among those who pacify; the liberated is the foremost among those who liberate, the one who has crossed over is the best among those who lead across.

Sabbaṃ lokaṃ abhiññāya,

Sabbaṃ loke yathātathaṃ;

Sabbaṃ lokaṃ visaṃyutto,

Sabbaloke anūpayo.

Having fully understood the entire world, and everything in the world as it really is; he is disjoined from the entire world, without clinging to anything in the world.

Sa ve sabbābhibhū dhīro,

Sabbaganthappamocano;

Puṭṭha’ssa paramā santi,

Nibbānaṃ akuto bhayaṃ.

He is indeed the wise one, the conqueror of all, the one released from all ties; for him, the supreme peace is attained: Nibbāna, from which there is no fear.

Esa khīṇāsavo buddho,

Anīgho chinnasaṃsayo;

Sabbakammakkhayaṃ [M 2.0013] patto,

Vimutto upadhisaṅkhaye;

This is the Buddha, whose taints are destroyed, trouble-free, with doubts cut off; he has attained the destruction of all kamma, liberated through the exhaustion of the substrata of existence.

Esa so bhagavā buddho,

Esa sīho anuttaro;

Sadevakassa lokassa,

Brahmacakkaṃ pavattayī.

This is the Blessed One, the Buddha, this is the unsurpassed lion. For the world with its devas, he set in motion the Wheel of Brahma.

Iti devā manussā ca,

Ye buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gatā;

Saṅgamma taṃ namassanti,

Mahantaṃ vītasāradaṃ.

Thus, devas and humans, who have gone to the Buddha for refuge, having come together, they pay homage to him, the great one, free from diffidence.

Danto damayataṃ seṭṭho,

Santo samayataṃ isi;

Mutto mocayataṃ aggo,

Tiṇṇo tārayataṃ varo.

The tamed one is the best among those who tame, The peaceful one is the sage among those who pacify; The liberated one is the foremost among those who liberate, The one who has crossed over is the best among those who lead across.

Iti hetaṃ namassanti,

Mahantaṃ vītasāradaṃ;

Sadevakasmiṃ lokasmiṃ,

Natthi me paṭipuggalo–

Thus, they pay homage to him, the great one, free from diffidence; in the world with its devas, there is no equal to me.

Brahmacariyasutta

The Discourse on the Holy Life

Pu – tattheva āvuso pañcamaṃ brahmacariyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the fifth Discourse on the Holy Life spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – pañcamaṃ bhante brahmacariyasuttaṃ “nayidaṃ bhikkhave brahmacariyaṃ vussati janakuhanatthaṃ, na janalapanatthaṃ, na lābhasakkārasilokānisaṃsuttaṃ[M 2.0014], na itivādappamokkhānisaṃsatthaṃ, na ‘iti maṃ jano jānātū’ti. Atha kho idaṃ bhikkhave brahmacariyaṃ vussati saṃvaratthaṃ pahānatthaṃ virāgatthaṃ nirodhattha”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the fifth Discourse on the Holy Life was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, this holy life is not lived for the sake of deceiving people, nor for the sake of flattering people, nor for the sake of gain, honor, and renown, nor for the sake of the advantages of disputation, nor for thinking, ‘Let people know me thus.’ Rather, monks, this holy life is lived for the sake of restraint, for the sake of abandoning, for the sake of dispassion, for the sake of cessation.”

Ariyavaṃsasutta

The Discourse on the Noble Lineages

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0015] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ ariyavaṃsasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the eighth Discourse on the Noble Lineages spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – tattheva bhante aṭṭhamaṃ ariyavaṃsasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave ariyavaṃsā aggaññā rattaññā vaṃsaññā porāṇā, asaṃkiṇṇā asaṃkiṇṇapubbāna saṃkīyissanti, appaṭikuṭṭhā samaṇehi brāhmaṇehi viññūhi. Katame cattāro, idha bhikkhave bhikkhu santuṭṭho hoti itarītarena cīvarena, itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā ca vaṇṇavādī, na ca cīvarahetu anesanaṃ appatirūpaṃ āpajjati, aladdhā ca cīvaraṃ na paritassati, laddhā ca cīvaraṃ agadhito amucchito anajjhopanno ādīnavadassāvī nissaraṇapañño paribhuñjati, tāya ca pana itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā nevattānukkaṃseti no paraṃ vambheti. Yo hi tattha dakkho analaso sampajāno paṭissabho, ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhave bhikkhu porāṇe aggaññe ariyavaṃse ṭhito”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the eighth Discourse on the Noble Lineages was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, these four noble lineages are foremost, long-standing, traditional, and ancient; they are unmixed, have been unmixed in the past, and will not be mixed; they are not censured by recluses, brahmins, and the wise. What four? Here, monks, a bhikkhu is content with any kind of robe. He speaks in praise of contentment with any kind of robe, and does not resort to an unseemly and inappropriate search for robes. If he does not obtain a robe, he does not fret, and if he obtains one, he uses it without being greedy, without being infatuated, and without being attached, seeing the danger and understanding the escape. And by this contentment with any kind of robe, he neither exalts himself nor disparages others. Whoever is skilled, not lazy, clearly comprehending, and mindful in this is called, monks, a bhikkhu who stands in the ancient, foremost noble lineage.”

Cakkavagga

The Chapter on the Wheel

Cakkasutta

The Discourse on the Wheel

Pu – cakkavagge [M 2.0019] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ cakkasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the first Discourse on the Wheel in the Chapter on the Wheel spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – cakkavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ cakkasuttaṃ “cattārimāni bhikkhave cakkāni, yehi samannāgatānaṃ devamanussānaṃ catucakkaṃ vattati, yehi samannāgatā devamanussā nacirasseva mahantattaṃ vepullattaṃ pāpuṇanti bhogesu. Katamāni cattāri, patirūpadesavāso sappurisāvassayo attasammāpaṇidhi pubbe ca katapuññatā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the first Discourse on the Wheel in the Chapter on the Wheel was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, there are these four wheels. When devas and humans are endowed with these, their fourfold wheel turns. Endowed with these, devas and humans soon attain greatness and abundance in wealth. What four? Living in a suitable region, reliance on good persons, right self-direction, and having made merit in the past.”

Saṅgahasutta

The Discourse on the Bases of Sympathy

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0021] āvuso dutiyaṃ saṅgahasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the second Discourse on the Bases of Sympathy spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – tattheva bhante dutiyaṃ saṅgahasuttaṃ “cattārimāni bhikkhave saṅgahavatthūni. Katamāni cattāri, dānaṃ peyyavajjaṃ atthacariyā samānattatā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, right there, the second Discourse on the Bases of Sympathy was spoken by the Blessed One, beginning: “Monks, there are these four bases of sympathy. What four? Generosity, kind speech, beneficial conduct, and impartiality.”

Sīhasutta

The Discourse on the Lion

Pu – bhattheva [M 2.0023] āvuso tatiyaṃ sīhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the third Discourse on the Lion spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – tattheva bhante tatiyaṃ sīhasuttaṃ “sīho bhikkhave migarājā sāyanusamayaṃ āsayā nikkhamati, āsayā nikkhamitvā vijambhati, vijambhitvā samantā catuddisā anuviloketi, samantā catuddisā anuviloketvā tikkhattuṃ sīhanādaṃ nadati, tikkhattuṃ sīhanādaṃ naditvā gocarāya pakkamatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, right there, the third Discourse on the Lion was spoken by the Blessed One, beginning: “Monks, the lion, the king of beasts, emerges from his lair in the evening. Having emerged from his lair, he yawns; having yawned, he surveys the four directions all around; having surveyed the four directions all around, he roars a lion’s roar three times; having roared a lion’s roar three times, he then departs for his feeding ground.”

Doṇasutta

The Discourse to Doṇa

Pu – tenāvuso [M 2.0025] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye catukkanipāte cakkavagge chaṭṭhaṃ doṇasuttaṃ kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, where, concerning whom, regarding what subject, and how was the sixth Discourse to Doṇa spoken by that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, in the Chapter on the Wheel of the Book of the Fours in the Aṅguttara Nikāya?

Vi – antarā [M 2.0026] ca bhante ukkaṭṭhaṃ antarā ca setabyaṃ doṇaṃ brāhmaṇaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Doṇo bhante brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “devo no bhavaṃ bhavissatī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “na kho ahaṃ brāhmaṇa devo bhavissāmī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, it was spoken concerning the brahmin Doṇa between Ukkaṭṭha and Setabya. Venerable sir, the brahmin Doṇa said this to the Blessed One: “Will the revered one be a god?” Regarding that matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke, beginning: “I will not be a god, brahmin.”

“Acchariyaṃ [M 2.0027] vata bho, abbhutaṃ vata bho, na vatimāni manussabhūtassa padāni bhavissanti”.

“It is wonderful, sirs! It is amazing, sirs! These could not possibly be the footprints of a human being.”

“Devo no bhavaṃ bhavissati”.

“Will the revered one be a god?”

“Na kho ahaṃ brāhmaṇa devo bhavissami”.

“I will not be a god, brahmin.”

“Gandhabbo no bhavaṃ bhavissati”.

“Will the revered one be a gandhabba?”

“Na kho ahaṃ brāhmaṇa gandhabbo bhavissāmi”.

“I will not be a gandhabba, brahmin.”

Ujjayasutta

The Discourse to Ujjaya

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0030] āvuso navamaṃ ujjayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the ninth Discourse to Ujjaya spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – navamaṃ bhante ujjayasuttaṃ “na kho ahaṃ brāhmaṇa sabbaṃ yaññaṃ vaṇṇemi, na panāhaṃ brāhmaṇa sabbaṃ yaññaṃ na vaṇṇemi. Yathārūpe kho brāhmaṇa yaññe gāvo haññanti, ajeḷakā haññanti, kukkuṭasūkarā haññanti, vividhā pāṇā saṅghātaṃ āpajjantī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the ninth Discourse to Ujjaya was spoken by the Blessed One, beginning: “Brahmin, I do not praise every sacrifice, nor do I not praise every sacrifice. Brahmin, in a sacrifice where cows are slaughtered, goats and sheep are slaughtered, chickens and pigs are slaughtered, and various living beings meet their destruction…”

“Bhavampi [M 2.0031] no gotamo yaññaṃ vaṇṇeti”.

“Does Master Gotama praise any sacrifice?”

Rohitassavagga

The Chapter on Rohitassa

Samādhibhāvanāsutta

The Discourse on the Development of Concentration

Pu – rohitassavagge [M 2.0033] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ samādhibhāvanāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the first Discourse on the Development of Concentration spoken by the Blessed One in the Chapter on Rohitassa?

Vi – rohitassavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ samādhibhāvanāsuttaṃ “catasso imā bhikkhave samādhibhāvanā, katamā catasso, atthi bhikkhave samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāya saṃvattati, atthi bhikkhave samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā ñāṇadassanappaṭilābhāya saṃvattati, atthi bhikkhave samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā satisampajaññāya saṃvattati, atthi bhikkhave samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā āsavānaṃ khayāya saṃvattatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the first Discourse on the Development of Concentration in the Chapter on Rohitassa was spoken by the Blessed One, beginning: “Monks, there are these four developments of concentration. What four? There is, monks, a development of concentration that, when developed and cultivated, leads to pleasant abiding in this very life. There is, monks, a development of concentration that, when developed and cultivated, leads to the acquisition of knowledge and vision. There is, monks, a development of concentration that, when developed and cultivated, leads to mindfulness and clear comprehension. There is, monks, a development of concentration that, when developed and cultivated, leads to the destruction of the taints.”

Pañhabyākaraṇasutta

The Discourse on Answering Questions

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0037] āvuso dutiyaṃ pañhabyākaraṇasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the second Discourse on Answering Questions spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – tattheva bhante dutiyaṃ pañhabyākaraṇasuttaṃ “cattārimāni bhikkhave pañhabyākaraṇāni. Katamāni cattāri, atthi bhikkhave pañho ekaṃsabyākaraṇīyo, atthi bhikkhave pañho vibhajjabyākaraṇīyo, atthi bhikkhave pañho paṭipucchābyākaraṇīyo, atthi bhikkhave pañho ṭhapanīyo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, right there, the second Discourse on Answering Questions was spoken by the Blessed One, beginning: “Monks, there are these four ways of answering questions. What four? There is, monks, a question to be answered categorically. There is, monks, a question to be answered analytically. There is, monks, a question to be answered with a counter-question. There is, monks, a question to be set aside.”

Puññābhisandavagga

The Chapter on Streams of Merit

Paṭhamasaṃvāsasutta

The First Discourse on Cohabitation

Pu – puññābhisandavagge [M 2.0039] pana āvuso tatiyaṃ saṃvāsasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, where, concerning whom, and how was the third Discourse on Cohabitation spoken by the Blessed One in the Chapter on Streams of Merit?

Vi – puññābhisandavagge bhante tatiyaṃ saṃvāsasuttaṃ antarā ca madhuraṃ antarā ca verañjaṃ sambahule gahapatayo ca gahapatāniyo ca ārabbha “cattārome gahapatayo saṃvāsā. Katame cattāro, chavo chavāya saddhiṃ saṃvasati, chavo deviyā saddhiṃ saṃvasati, devo chavāya saddhiṃ saṃvasati, devo deviyā saddhiṃ saṃvasatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Streams of Merit, the third Discourse on Cohabitation was spoken by the Blessed One concerning numerous householders and their wives, between Madhurā and Verañjā, beginning: “Householders, there are these four kinds of cohabitation. What four? A corpse cohabits with a corpse; a corpse cohabits with a goddess; a god cohabits with a corpse; a god cohabits with a goddess.”

Paṭhamasamajīvīsutta

The First Samajīvī Sutta

Pu – tenāvuso [M 2.0043] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye catukkanipāte puññābhisandavagge pañcamaṃ samajīvisuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the fifth Samajīvī Sutta spoken by the Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fours, in the Puññābhisanda Chapter?

Vi – pañcamaṃ bhante samajīvisuttaṃ “ākaṅkheyyuṃ ce gahapatayo ubho jānipatayo diṭṭhe ceva dhamme aññamaññaṃ passituṃ, abhisamparāyañca aññamaññaṃ passituṃ, ubhova assu samasaddhā samasīlā samacāgā samapaññā, te diṭṭhe ceva dhamme aññamaññaṃ passanti, abhisamparāyañca aññamaññaṃ passantī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the fifth Samajīvī Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning: “If, householders, both husband and wife should wish to see one another in this very life and in the life to come, and both are of equal faith, virtue, generosity, and wisdom, then they see one another in this very life and in the life to come.”

Pattakammavagga

The Chapter on Meritorious Deeds

Ānaṇyasukhasutta

The Discourse on the Happiness of Debtlessness

Pu – pattakammavagge [M 2.0046] pana āvuso dutiyaṃ ānaṇyasukhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Chapter on Meritorious Deeds, where, regarding whom, and how was the second Discourse on the Happiness of Debtlessness spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – pattakammavagge [M 2.0047] bhante dutiyaṃ ānaṇyasukhasuttaṃ sāvatthiyaṃ anāthapiṇḍikaṃ gahapatiṃ ārabbha “cattārimāni gahapati sukhāni adhigamanīyāni gihinā kāmabhoginā kālena kālaṃ samayena samayaṃ upādāya. Katamāni cattāri, atthisukhaṃ bhogasukhaṃ anaṇyasukhaṃ anavajjasukha”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Meritorious Deeds, the second Discourse on the Happiness of Debtlessness was spoken by the Blessed One at Sāvatthī, concerning the householder Anāthapiṇḍika, beginning: “Householder, these four kinds of happiness are attainable by a householder who enjoys sensual pleasures, from time to time, on occasion. Which four? The happiness of possession, the happiness of enjoyment, the happiness of debtlessness, and the happiness of blamelessness.”

Ānaṇyasukhaṃ [M 2.0050] ñatvāna, atho atthisukhaṃ paraṃ;

Bhuñjaṃ bhogasukhaṃ macco, tato paññā vipassati;

Vipassamāno jānāti, ubho bhāge sumedhaso;

Anavajjasukhassetaṃ, kalaṃ nāgghati soḷasiṃ–

Having known the happiness of debtlessness, and then the happiness of possession, a mortal enjoying the happiness of enjoyment then sees with wisdom. Seeing wisely, the wise one knows both aspects. This does not equal a sixteenth part of the happiness of blamelessness.

Appaṇṇakavagga

The Appaṇṇaka Chapter

Sappurisasutta

The Discourse on a Good Person

Pu – apaṇṇakavagge pana āvuso tatiyaṃ sappurisasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Appaṇṇaka Chapter, how was the third Discourse on a Good Person spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – apaṇṇakavagge [M 2.0051] bhante tatiyaṃ sappurisasuttaṃ “catūhi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato asappuriso veditabbo. Katamehi catūhi, idha bhikkhave asappuriso yo hoti parassa avaṇṇo taṃ apuṭṭhopi pātu karoti ko pana vādo puṭṭhassā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Appaṇṇaka Chapter, the third Discourse on a Good Person was spoken by the Blessed One beginning: “Monks, an ignoble person is to be understood by four qualities. Which four? Here, monks, an ignoble person, even when unasked, reveals another’s dispraise; what need to mention it when asked?”

Adhunāgatavadhukāsamena [M 2.0055] cetasā viharissāma” –

“We will dwell with minds like a newly arrived bride.”

Acinteyyasutta

The Discourse on the Unthinkable

Pu – tattheva āvuso sattamaṃ acinteyyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the seventh Discourse on the Unthinkable spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vi – sattamaṃ bhante acinteyyasuttaṃ “cattārimāni bhikkhave acinteyyāni na cintetabbāni, yāni cintento ummādassa vighātassa bhāgī assa. Katamāni cattāri, buddhānaṃ bhikkhave buddhavisayo acinteyyo na cintetabbo, yaṃ cintento ummādassa vighātassa bhāgī assā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the seventh Discourse on the Unthinkable was spoken by the Blessed One beginning: “Monks, there are these four unthinkables that should not be pondered, for whoever ponders them would partake of madness and vexation. What four? The range of the Buddhas, monks, is an unthinkable that should not be pondered, for whoever ponders it would partake of madness and vexation…”

Macalavagga

The Macala Chapter

Tamotamasutta

The Discourse on Darkness to Darkness

Pu – macalavagge [M 2.0057] pana āvuso pañcamaṃ tamotamasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Macala Chapter, how was the fifth Discourse on Darkness to Darkness spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – macalavagge bhante pañcamaṃ tamotamasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, tamotamaparāyaṇo, tamojotiparāyaṇo, jotitamaparāyaṇo, jotijotiparāyaṇo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Amacala Chapter, the fifth Discourse on Darkness was spoken by the Blessed One beginning: “Monks, these four types of individuals are found existing in the world. What four? One headed for darkness from darkness, one headed for light from darkness, one headed for darkness from light, and one headed for light from light…”

Asuravagga

The Asura Chapter

Asurasutta

The Discourse on Asuras

Pu – tenāvuso [M 2.0061] bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena aṅguttaranikāye catukkanipāte asuravagge paṭhamaṃ asurasuttaṃ kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the first Discourse on Asuras spoken by the Blessed One, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fours, in the Asura Chapter?

Vi – asuravagge bhante paṭhamaṃ asurasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, asuro asuraparivāro, asuro devaparivāro, devo asuraparivāro, devo devaparivāro”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Asura Chapter, the first Discourse on Asuras was spoken by the Blessed One beginning: “Monks, these four types of individuals are found existing in the world. What four? An asura with an asura retinue, an asura with a deva retinue, a deva with an asura retinue, and a deva with a deva retinue.”

Samādhisutta

The Discourse on Concentration

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0064] āvuso catutthaṃ samādhisuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the fourth Discourse on Concentration spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vi – catutthaṃ bhante samādhisuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, idha bhikkhave ekacco puggalo lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya. Idha pana bhikkhave puggalo lābhī hoti adhipaññādhammavipassanāya, na lābhī ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa. Idha pana bhikkhave ekacco puggalo na ceva lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na ca lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya. Idha pana bhikkhave ekacco puggalo lābhī ceva hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, lābhī ca adhipaññādhammavipassanāyā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the fourth Discourse on Concentration was spoken by the Blessed One beginning: “Monks, these four types of individuals are found existing in the world. What four? Here, monks, a certain person attains internal serenity of mind but does not attain higher wisdom through insight into phenomena. Here, another person attains higher wisdom through insight into phenomena but does not attain internal serenity of mind. Here, another person attains neither internal serenity of mind nor higher wisdom through insight into phenomena. And here, another person attains both internal serenity of mind and higher wisdom through insight into phenomena…”

Rāgavinayasutta

The Discourse on the Subduing of Lust

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0068] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ rāgavinayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the sixth Discourse on the Subduing of Lust spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vi – chaṭṭhaṃ bhante rāgavinayasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, attahitāya paṭipanno no parahitāya, parahitāya paṭipanno no attahitāya, nevattahitāya paṭipanno no parahitāya, attahitāya ceva paṭipanno parahitāya cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the sixth Discourse on the Subduing of Lust was spoken by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, there are these four persons found existing in the world. Which four? One who practices for his own welfare but not for the welfare of others; one who practices for the welfare of others but not for his own welfare; one who practices neither for his own welfare nor for the welfare of others; and one who practices both for his own welfare and for the welfare of others.'

Valāhakavagga

The Cloud Chapter

Paṭhamavalāhakasutta

The First Discourse on Clouds

Pu – valāhakavagge [M 2.0071] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ valāhakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Cloud Chapter, where, concerning whom, and how was the first Discourse on Clouds spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha “cattārome bhikkhave valāhakā. Katame cattāro, gajjitā no vassitā, vassitā no gajjitā, neva gajjitā no vassitā, gajjitā ca vassitā ca. Ime kho bhikkhave cattāro valāhakā. Evameva kho bhikkhave cattāro valāhakūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjāmānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, gajjitā no vassitā, vassitā no gajjitā, neva gajjitā no vassitā, gajjitā ca vassitā cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, it was at Sāvatthī, concerning several monks. 'Monks, there are these four clouds. Which four? One that thunders but does not rain; one that rains but does not thunder; one that neither thunders nor rains; and one that both thunders and rains. These, monks, are the four clouds. Just so, monks, there are these four persons comparable to clouds found existing in the world. Which four? One who thunders but does not rain; one who rains but does not thunder; one who neither thunders nor rains; and one who both thunders and rains.' Thus was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Udakarahadasutta

The Discourse on the Pool of Water

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0073] āvuso catutthaṃ udakarahadasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the fourth Discourse on the Pool of Water spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – catuttha bhante udakarahadasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave udakarahadā. Katame cattāro, uttāno gambhīrobhāso, gambhīro uttānobhāso, uttāno uttānobhāso, gambhīro gambhīrobhāso, ime kho bhikkhave cattāro udakarahadā. Evameva kho bhikkhave cattāro udakarahadūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, uttāno gambhīrobhāso, gambhīro uttānobhāso, uttāno uttānobhāso, gambhīro gambhīrobhāso”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the fourth Discourse on the Pool of Water was spoken by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, there are these four pools of water. Which four? A shallow one that appears deep; a deep one that appears shallow; a shallow one that appears shallow; and a deep one that appears deep. These, monks, are the four pools of water. In the same way, monks, there are these four persons comparable to pools of water found existing in the world. Which four? One who is shallow but appears deep; one who is deep but appears shallow; one who is shallow and appears shallow; and one who is deep and appears deep.'

Mūsikasutta

The Discourse on the Mouse

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0076] āvuso sattamaṃ mūsikasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the seventh Discourse on the Mouse spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – sattamaṃ bhante mūsikasuttaṃ “catasso imā bhikkhave mūsikā. Katamā catasso, gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, vasitā no gādhaṃ kattā, neva gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, gādhaṃ kattā ca vasitā ca. Imā kho bhikkhave catasso mūsikā. Evameva kho bhikkhave cattāro mūsikūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro, gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, vasitā no gādhaṃ kattā, neva gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, gādhaṃ kattā ca vasitā cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the seventh Discourse on the Mouse was spoken by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, there are these four mice. Which four? One that digs a burrow but does not dwell in it; one that dwells in a burrow but does not dig it; one that neither digs a burrow nor dwells in it; and one that both digs a burrow and dwells in it. These, monks, are the four mice. In the same way, monks, there are these four persons comparable to mice found existing in the world. Which four? One who digs a burrow but does not dwell in it; one who dwells in a burrow but does not dig it; one who neither digs a burrow nor dwells in it; and one who both digs a burrow and dwells in it.'

Balībaddhasutta

The Discourse on the Ox

Pu – saṃgītāpi [M 2.0079] āvuso aṅguttaranikāyato kānici suttāni uddharitvā paṭipucchissāmi bahujanassa sutavuḍḍhiyā. Saṃgīte āvuso aṅguttaranikāye catukkanipāte valāhakavagge aṭṭhamaṃ balībaddhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, I will extract some discourses from the Aṅguttara Nikāya and ask about them to increase the learning of many people. In the collected Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of the Fours, in the Cloud Chapter, how was the eighth Discourse on the Ox spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – valāhakavagge bhante aṭṭhamaṃ balībaddhasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave balībaddhā. Katame cattāro, sagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo, paragavacaṇḍo no sagavacaṇḍo, sagavacaṇḍo ca paragavacaṇḍo ca, neva sagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo. Ime kho bhikkhave cattāro balībaddhā. Evameva kho bhikkhave cattāro balībaddhūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmi”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Cloud Chapter, the eighth Discourse on the Ox was spoken by the Blessed One thus: 'Monks, there are these four oxen. Which four? One fierce towards its own herd but not fierce towards other herds; one fierce towards other herds but not fierce towards its own herd; one fierce towards its own herd and fierce towards other herds; and one neither fierce towards its own herd nor fierce towards other herds. These, monks, are the four oxen. In the same way, monks, there are these four persons comparable to oxen found existing in the world.'

Kesisutta

The Discourse with Kesi

Pu – kesivagge [M 2.0082] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ kesisuttaṃ bhagavatā kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Kesi Chapter, regarding whom, on what subject, and in what manner was the first Discourse with Kesi spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – kesiṃ bhante assadammasārathiṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Kesi bhante assadammasārathi bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “bhagavā pana bhante anuttaro purisadammasārathi, kathaṃ pana bhante bhagavā purisadammaṃ vinetī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “ahaṃ kho kesi purisadammaṃ saṇhenapi vinemi, pharusenapi vinemi, saṇhapharusenapi vinemi. Tatridaṃ kesi saṇhasmiṃ – iti kāyasucaritaṃ iti kāyasucaritassa vipāko, iti vacīsucaritaṃ iti vacīsucaritassa [M 2.0083] vipāko, iti manosucaritaṃ iti manosucaritassa vipāko, iti devā iti manussā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, this was spoken concerning Kesi, the horse trainer. Venerable sir, Kesi the horse trainer said this to the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, the Blessed One is the unsurpassed trainer of persons to be tamed. How, venerable sir, does the Blessed One train a person to be tamed?' On that matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke thus: 'I, Kesi, train a person to be tamed sometimes gently, sometimes harshly, and sometimes both gently and harshly. Here, Kesi, is the gentle way: thus is good bodily conduct, thus the result of good bodily conduct; thus is good verbal conduct, thus the result of good verbal conduct; thus is good mental conduct, thus the result of good mental conduct; thus are gods, thus are humans.'

“Tvaṃ khosi kesi paññāto assadammasārathīti, kathaṃ pana tvaṃ kesi assadammasārathi” –

'You, Kesi, are a renowned horse trainer. But how do you, Kesi, train a horse to be tamed?'

“Ahaṃ kho bhante assadammaṃ saṇhenapi vinemi, pharusenapi vinemi, saṇhapharusenapi vinemi” –

'Venerable sir, I train a horse to be tamed sometimes with gentleness, sometimes with harshness, and sometimes with both gentleness and harshness.'

“Sace [M 2.0084] te kesi assadammo saṇhena vinayaṃ na upeti, pharusena vinayaṃ na upeti, saṇhapharusena vinayaṃ na upeti, kinti naṃ karosi” –

'If, Kesi, a horse to be tamed does not submit to training by gentleness, nor by harshness, nor by both gentleness and harshness, what do you do with it?'

“Bhagavā pana bhante anuttaro purisadammasārathi, kathaṃ pana bhante bhagavā purisadammaṃ vineti” –

“But, venerable sir, the Blessed One is the unsurpassed trainer of persons to be tamed. How, venerable sir, does the Blessed One train a person to be tamed?”

Na 0.0087 kho bhante bhagavato pāṇātipāto kappati, atha ca pana bhagavā evamāha “hanāmi naṃ kesī”ti.

“Venerable sir, the taking of life is not approved of by the Blessed One, yet the Blessed One says, ‘I will subdue him, Kesi.’”

Attānuvādasutta

The Discourse on Self-Recrimination

Pu – bhayavagge [M 2.0089] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ attānuvādasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

“But, friend, how was the first Discourse on Self-Recrimination in the Chapter on Fears spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – bhayavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ attānuvādasuttaṃ “cattārimāni bhikkhave bhayāni. Katamāni cattāri, attānuvādabhayaṃ parānuvādabhayaṃ daṇḍabhayaṃ duggatibhaya”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, the first Discourse on Self-Recrimination in the Chapter on Fears was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, there are these four fears. Which four? The fear of self-recrimination, the fear of others’ recrimination, the fear of punishment, and the fear of a bad destination.’”

Dhammakathikasutta

The Discourse on the Dhamma Speaker

Pu – idāni [M 2.0092] āvuso bahujanassa sutavuḍḍhiyā saṃgītā aṅguttaranikāyatopi kānici suttāni uddharitvā paṭipucchissāmi, saṃgīte āvuso aṅguttaranikāye catukkanipāte puggalavagge navamaṃ dhammakathikasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, now for the increase of many people’s learning, I will extract some suttas from the compiled Aṅguttara Nikāya and question you about them. Friend, in the compiled Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fours, in the Chapter on Persons, how was the ninth Discourse on the Dhamma Speaker spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – puggalavagge bhante navamaṃ dhammakathikasuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave dhammakathikā. Katame cattāro, idha bhikkhave ekacco dhammakathiko appañca bhāsati asahitañca, parisā cassa na kusalā hoti sahitāsahitassa. Evarūpo bhikkhave dhammakathiko evarūpāya parisāya dhammakathikotveva saṅkhaṃ gacchatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, the ninth Discourse on the Dhamma Speaker in the Chapter on Persons was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, there are these four kinds of Dhamma speakers. Which four? Here, monks, a certain Dhamma speaker speaks little and incoherently, and his audience is not skilled in what is coherent or incoherent. Such a Dhamma speaker, monks, is reckoned simply as a Dhamma speaker for such an audience.’”

Rogasutta

The Discourse on Illness

Pu – indriyavagge [M 2.0095] pana āvuso sattamaṃ rogasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, how was the seventh Discourse on Illness in the Chapter on Faculties spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – sattamaṃ bhante rogasuttaṃ “dveme bhikkhave rogā. Katame dve, kāyiko ca rogo, cetasiko ca rogo. Dissanti bhikkhave sattā kāyikena rogena ekampi vassaṃ ārogyaṃ paṭijānamānā dvepi vassāni tīṇipi vassāni cattāripi vassāni pañcapi vassāni dasapi vassāni vīsampi vassāni tiṃsampi vassāni cattārīsampi vassāni paññāsampi vassāni ārogyaṃ paṭijānamānā vassasatampi bhiyyopi ārogyaṃ paṭijānamānā. Te bhikkhave sattā sudullabhā lokasmiṃ, ye cetasikena rogena muhuttampi ārogyaṃ paṭijānanti aññatra khīṇāsavehī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, the seventh Discourse on Illness was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, there are these two kinds of illness. Which two? Bodily illness and mental illness. Monks, beings are seen who claim to be healthy, free from bodily illness for one year, two years, three years, four years, five years, ten years, twenty years, thirty years, forty years, fifty years, even a hundred years or more. But, monks, those beings are very rare in the world who claim to be healthy, free from mental illness even for a moment, except for those whose taints are destroyed.’”

Cattārome 0.0096 bhikkhave pabbajitassa rogā.

“Monks, these are the four illnesses of a renunciate.”

Yuganaddhasutta

The Discourse on Yoking

Pu – paṭipadāvagge [M 2.0098] āvuso dasamaṃ yuganaddhasuttaṃ kattha kaṃ ārabbha kena kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, in the Chapter on Practice, the tenth, the Discourse on Yoking—where, concerning whom, by whom, and how was it spoken?”

Vi – kosambiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha “yo hi koci āvuso bhikkhu vā bhikkhunī vā mama santike arahattappattiṃ byākaroti, sabbo so catūhi maggehi etesaṃ vā aññatarenā”ti evamādinā bhante āyasmatā ānandattherena dhammabhaṇḍāgārikena bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, it was spoken at Kosambī concerning several bhikkhus, by the Venerable Ānanda, the Treasurer of the Dhamma, beginning thus: ‘Friend, any bhikkhu or bhikkhunī who declares the attainment of arahantship in my presence, all of them do so by means of one or another of these four paths.’”

Pāṭibhogasutta

The Discourse on a Guarantor

Pu – brāhmaṇavagge [M 2.0101] pana āvuso dutiyaṃ pāṭibhogasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, how was the second Discourse on a Guarantor in the Chapter on Brahmins spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – brāhmaṇavagge bhante dutiyaṃ pāṭibhogasuttaṃ “catunnaṃ bhikkhave dhammānaṃ natthi koci pāṭibhogo samaṇo vā brāhmaṇo vā devo vā māro vā brahmā vā koci vā lokasmi”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, the second Discourse on a Guarantor in the Chapter on Brahmins was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, for these four things there is no guarantor—no ascetic, brahmin, god, Māra, Brahmā, or anyone in the world.’”

Sutasutta

The Discourse on What Is Heard

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0103] āvuso tatiyaṃ sutasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, in that same place, where, concerning whom, on what basis, and how was the third Discourse on What Is Heard spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – rājagahe bhante vassakāraṃ brāhmaṇaṃ magadhamahāmattaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Vassakāro bhante brāhmaṇo magadhamahāmatto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “ahañhi bho gotama evaṃvādī evaṃdiṭṭhi yo koci diṭṭhaṃ bhāsati ‘evaṃ me diṭṭha’nti, natthi tato doso. Yo koci sutaṃ bhāsati ‘evaṃ me suta’nti, natthi tato doso”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “nāhaṃ brāhmaṇa sabbaṃ diṭṭhaṃ ‘bhāsitabba’nti vadāmi, na panāhaṃ brāhmaṇa sabbaṃ diṭṭhaṃ ‘na bhāsitabba’nti vadāmi, nāhaṃ brāhmaṇa sabbaṃ sutaṃ ‘bhāsitabba’nti vadāmi, na panāhaṃ brāhmaṇa sabbaṃ sutaṃ ‘na bhāsitabba’nti vadāmī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, it was spoken at Rājagaha concerning the brahmin Vassakāra, the chief minister of Magadha. The brahmin Vassakāra, the chief minister of Magadha, said this to the Blessed One: ‘Master Gotama, I am one who speaks and holds this view: “Whatever one speaks of as seen, saying, ‘Thus I have seen,’ there is no fault in that. Whatever one speaks of as heard, saying, ‘Thus I have heard,’ there is no fault in that.”’ On that matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: ‘Brahmin, I do not say that everything seen should be spoken of, nor, brahmin, do I say that everything seen should not be spoken of. I do not say, brahmin, that everything heard should be spoken of, nor, brahmin, do I say that everything heard should not be spoken of.’”

Abhayasutta

The Discourse on Fearlessness

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0106] āvuso catukkanipāte brāhmaṇavagge catutthaṃ abhayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fours, in the Chapter on Brahmins, concerning whom, on what matter, and how was the fourth Discourse on Fearlessness spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – brāhmaṇavagge bhante catutthaṃ abhayasuttaṃ jāṇussoṇiṃ brāhmaṇaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Jāṇussoṇi bhante brāhmaṇo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “natthi yo maraṇadhammo samāno na bhāyati, na santāsaṃ āpajjati maraṇassā”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “atthi brāhmaṇa maraṇadhammo samāno bhāyati, santāsaṃ āpajjati maraṇassa. Atthi pana brāhmaṇa maraṇadhammo samāno na bhāyati, na santāsaṃ āpajjati maraṇassā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, the fourth Discourse on Fearlessness in the Chapter on Brahmins was spoken concerning the brahmin Jāṇussoṇi. The brahmin Jāṇussoṇi said this to the Blessed One: ‘There is no one subject to death who does not fear or become terrified of death.’ On that matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: ‘Brahmin, there is one subject to death who fears and becomes terrified of death. But, brahmin, there is also one subject to death who does not fear or become terrified of death.’”

Sotānugatasutta

The Discourse on What is Learned by Hearing

Pu – mahāvagge [M 2.0113] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ sotānugatasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, in the Great Chapter, how was the first Discourse on What is Learned by Hearing spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – mahāvagge bhante paṭhamaṃ sotānugatasuttaṃ “sotānugatānaṃ bhikkhave dhammānaṃ vacasā paricitānaṃ manasānupekkhitānaṃ diṭṭhiyā suppaṭividdhānaṃ cattāro ānisaṃsā pāṭikaṅkhā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

“Venerable sir, in the Great Chapter, the first Discourse on What is Learned by Hearing was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, four benefits are to be expected for teachings that have been learned by hearing, verbally recited, mentally examined, and thoroughly penetrated by view.’”

Ayaṃ [M 2.0115] vā so dhammavinayo, yatthāhaṃ pubbe brahmacariyaṃ acariṃ.

This is that Dhamma and Discipline in which I formerly led the holy life.

Bhaddiyasutta

The Bhaddiya Sutta

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0118] āvuso catukkanipāte mahāvagge tatiyaṃ bhaddiyasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

“Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fours, in the Great Chapter, the third Bhaddiya Sutta: where, concerning whom, on what subject, and how was it spoken by the Blessed One?”

Vi – vesāliyaṃ bhante bhaddiyaṃ licchavi ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Bhaddiyo bhante licchavi bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “sutaṃ metaṃ bhante ‘māyāvī samaṇo gotamo āvaṭṭaniṃ māyaṃ jānāti, yāya aññatitthiyānaṃ sāvake āvaṭṭetī’ti. Ye te bhante evamāhaṃsu ‘māyāvī samaṇo gotamo āvaṭṭaniṃ māyaṃ jānāti, yāya aññatitthiyānaṃ sāvake āvaṭṭetī’ti…pe… anabbhakkhātukāmā [M 2.0119] hi mayaṃ bhante bhagavanta”nti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “etha tumhe bhaddiya mā anussavena mā paramparāya mā itikirāya mā piṭakasampadānena mā takkahetu mā nayahetu mā ākāravitakkena mā diṭṭhinijjhānakkhantiyā mā bhabbarūpatāya mā samaṇo no garūti. Yadā tumhe bhaddiya attanāva jāneyyātha ‘ime dhammā akusalā, ime dhammā sāvajjā, ime dhammā viññū garahitā, ime dhammā samattā samādinnā ahitāya dukkhāya saṃvattantī’ti. Atha tumhe bhaddiya pajaheyyāthā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, it was spoken in Vesālī concerning Bhaddiya the Licchavi. Bhaddiya the Licchavi, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, I have heard this: ‘The ascetic Gotama is a conjurer; he knows a conjuring trick by which he entices the disciples of other sects.’ Those, venerable sir, who say this—‘The ascetic Gotama is a conjurer; he knows a conjuring trick by which he entices the disciples of other sects’… we do not wish to misrepresent the Blessed One.” On that occasion, venerable sir, in regard to this matter, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: “Come, Bhaddiya, do not go by oral tradition, by lineage of teaching, by hearsay, by what is in a scripture collection, by logical reasoning, by inferential thought, by considering appearances, by agreement with a considered opinion, by seeming possibility, or because you think, ‘The ascetic is our teacher.’ When you know for yourselves, Bhaddiya, ‘These things are unwholesome, these things are blameworthy, these things are censured by the wise, these things, when undertaken and practiced, lead to harm and suffering,’ then you should abandon them.”

Pu – imissā [M 2.0126] ca pana āvuso desanāya desitāya bhaddiyassa licchavissa kīdiso dhammasavanānisaṃso adhigato. Kathañca naṃ bhagavā anuyuñjitvā taṃ vacanaṃ viniveṭhesi.

Pu – And what benefit, friend, did Bhaddiya the Licchavi gain from hearing this teaching? And how, by questioning him, did the Blessed One unravel that statement?

Vi – imissā bhante dhammadesanāya desitāya bhaddiyassa licchavissa sotāpattiphalasaṅkhāto dhammasavanānisaṃso adhigato. Api nu tāhaṃ bhaddiya evaṃ avacaṃ “ehi me tvaṃ bhaddiya sāvako hohi, ahaṃ satthā bhavissāmī”ti evamādinā ca naṃ bhante bhagavā paṭipucchitvā anuyuñjitvā taṃ vacanaṃ viniveṭhesi.

Vi – Venerable sir, by this teaching that was delivered, Bhaddiya the Licchavi attained the benefit of hearing the Dhamma, namely, the fruit of stream-entry. And did I say to you, Bhaddiya, “Come, Bhaddiya, be my disciple, I will be your teacher”? Thus, venerable sir, the Blessed One questioned him back, interrogated him, and clarified that statement.

Mallikādevīsutta

The Discourse to Queen Mallikā

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0129] āvuso sattamaṃ mallikādevīsuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, right there, the seventh discourse, the Discourse to Queen Mallikā: where, concerning whom, on what subject, and how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante mallikādeviṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Mallikā bhante devī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “ko nu kho bhante hetu ko paccayo, yena midhekacco mātugāmo dubbaṇṇā ca hoti durūpā supāpikā dassanāya, daliddā ca hoti appassakā appabhogā appesakkhā ca…pe… ko pana bhante hetu ko paccayo, yena midhekacco mātugāmo abhirūpā ca hoti dassanīyā pāsādikā paramāya vaṇṇapokkharatāya samannāgatā, aḍḍhā ca hoti mahaddhanā mahābhogā mahesakkhā cā”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “idha mallike mātugāmo kodhanā hoti upāyāsabahulā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, it was spoken in Sāvatthī concerning Queen Mallikā. Queen Mallikā, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, what is the cause, what is the reason, whereby a certain woman here is ugly, ill-favored, unpleasant to look at, poor, with little wealth, few possessions, and of little influence?... But what, venerable sir, is the cause, what is the reason, whereby a certain woman here is beautiful, lovely, pleasing, endowed with surpassing beauty, wealthy, with great riches, abundant possessions, and of great influence?” On that occasion, venerable sir, in regard to this matter, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: “Here, Mallikā, a woman is prone to anger and frequent irritation.”

Dasakammasutta

The Discourse on Ten Actions

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0134] āvuso catukkanipāte sappurisavagge catutthaṃ dasakammasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fours, in the Chapter on the Good Person, the fourth discourse, the Discourse on Ten Actions: how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – sappurisavagge [M 2.0135] bhante catutthaṃ dasakammasuttaṃ “asappurisañca vo bhikkhave desessāmi asappurisena asappurisatarañca sappurisañca sappurisena sappurisatarañca, taṃ suṇātha sādhukaṃ manasi karothā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the Good Person, the fourth discourse, the Discourse on Ten Actions, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, I will teach you about the ignoble person and one more ignoble than the ignoble person, and about the noble person and one more noble than the noble person. Listen to that and attend carefully.”

Parisāsutta

The Discourse on the Assembly

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0137] āvuso parisāvagge paṭhamaṃ parisāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, right there, in the Chapter on Assemblies, the first discourse, the Discourse on the Assembly: how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – parisāvagge bhante paṭhamaṃ parisāsuttaṃ “cattārome bhikkhave parisadūsanā. Katame cattāro, bhikkhu bhikkhave dussīlo pāpadhammo parisadūsano, bhikkhunī bhikkhave dussīlā pāpadhammā parisadūsanā, upāsako bhikkhave dussīlo pāpadhammo parisadūsano, upāsikā bhikkhave dussīlā pāpadhammā parisadūsanā. Ime kho bhikkhave cattāro parisadūsanā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Assemblies, the first discourse, the Discourse on the Assembly, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, there are these four defilers of an assembly. Which four? A monk, monks, who is immoral and of bad character is a defiler of an assembly; a nun, monks, who is immoral and of bad character is a defiler of an assembly; a male lay follower, monks, who is immoral and of bad character is a defiler of an assembly; a female lay follower, monks, who is immoral and of bad character is a defiler of an assembly. These, monks, are the four defilers of an assembly.”

Sekhabalavagga

The Chapter on the Powers of a Trainee

Saṃkhittasutta

The Brief Discourse

Pu – pañcakanipāte [M 2.0139] pana āvuso paṭhame sekhabalavagge paṭhamaṃ saṃkhittasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in the Book of Fives, in the first chapter, on the Powers of a Trainee, the first discourse, the Brief Discourse: where, concerning whom, and how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha “pañcimāni bhikkhave sekhabalāni. Katamāni pañca, saddhābalaṃ hirībalaṃ ottappabalaṃ vīriyabalaṃ paññābalaṃ. Imāni kho bhikkhave pañca sekhabalānī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, concerning a number of monks, it was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, there are these five powers of a trainee. Which five? The power of faith, the power of conscience, the power of prudence, the power of energy, and the power of wisdom. These, monks, are the five powers of a trainee.’

Dukkhasutta

The Discourse on Suffering

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0140] āvuso tatiyaṃ dukkhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, how was the third discourse, the Discourse on Suffering, spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vi – tatiyaṃ bhante dukkhasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato bhikkhu diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhaṃ viharati savighātaṃ saupāyāsaṃ sapariḷāhaṃ kāyassa ca bhedā paraṃ maraṇā duggati pāṭikaṅkhā. Katamehi pañcahi, idha bhikkhave bhikkhu asaddho hoti ahiriko hoti, anottappī hoti, kusīto hoti, duppañño hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, the third discourse, the Discourse on Suffering, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, a monk endowed with five qualities dwells in suffering in this very life—with distress, with despair, and with burning—and on the breakup of the body, after death, a bad destination is to be expected. With which five? Here, monks, a monk is faithless, shameless, without fear of wrongdoing, lazy, and unwise.’

Samāpattisutta

The Discourse on Attainments

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0142] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ samāpattisuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, how was the sixth discourse, the Discourse on Attainments, spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vi – chaṭṭhaṃ bhante samāpattisuttaṃ “na tāva bhikkhave akusalassa samāpatti hoti, yāva saddhā paccupaṭṭhitā hoti kusalesu dhammesu. Yato ca kho bhikkhave saddhā antarahitā hoti asaddhiyaṃ pariyuṭṭhāya tiṭṭhati, atha akusalassa samāpatti hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, the sixth discourse, the Discourse on Attainments, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, there is no attainment of the unwholesome so long as faith is present in wholesome qualities. But when, monks, faith has disappeared and faithlessness has arisen and beset the mind, then the attainment of the unwholesome occurs.’

Kāmasutta

The Discourse on Sensual Pleasures

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0143] āvuso sattamaṃ kāmasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, how was the seventh discourse, the Discourse on Sensual Pleasures, spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vi – sattamaṃ bhante kāmasuttaṃ “yebhuyyena bhikkhave sattā kāmesu laḷitā. Asitabyābhaṅgiṃ bhikkhave kulaputto ohāya agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajito hoti, saddhāpabbajito kulaputtoti alaṃ vacanāyā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, the seventh discourse, the Discourse on Sensual Pleasures, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘For the most part, monks, beings are fond of sensual pleasures. When a clansman, having given up the sickle and carrying pole, has gone forth from the household life into homelessness—it is enough to call him a clansman gone forth out of faith.’

Vimuttāyatanasutta

The Discourse on the Grounds for Deliverance

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0146] pañcakanipāte pañcaṅgikavagge chaṭṭhaṃ vimuttāyatanasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – In the Aṅguttara Nikāya, Book of the Fives, in the Chapter on Five Factors, how was the sixth discourse, the Discourse on the Grounds for Deliverance, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – pañcaṅgikavagge bhante chaṭṭhaṃ vimuttāyatanasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave vimuttāyatanāni, yattha bhikkhuno appamattassa ātāpino pahitattassa viharato avimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ vimuccati, aparikkhīṇā vā āsavā parikkhayaṃ gacchanti, ananuppattaṃ vā anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ anupāpuṇātī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Five Factors, the sixth discourse, the Discourse on the Grounds for Deliverance, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Monks, there are these five grounds for deliverance where, for a monk dwelling heedful, ardent, and resolute, the unliberated mind becomes liberated, the unexhausted taints come to exhaustion, and the unattained supreme security from bondage is attained.’

Caṅkamasutta

The Discourse on Walking Meditation

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0150] āvuso navamaṃ caṅkamasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, how was the ninth discourse, the Discourse on Walking Meditation, spoken by the Blessed One in that very place?

Vi – navamaṃ bhante caṅkamasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave caṅkame ānisaṃsā. Katame pañca, addhānakkhamo hoti, padhānakkhamo hoti, appābādho [M 2.0151] hoti, asitaṃ pītaṃ khāyitaṃ sāyitaṃ sammā pariṇāmaṃ gacchati, caṅkamādhigato samādhi ciraṭṭhitiko hoti. Ime kho bhikkhave pañca caṅkame ānisaṃsā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the ninth, the Discourse on Walking Meditation: 'Monks, there are these five benefits of walking meditation. What five? One becomes fit for long journeys, fit for striving, and has little illness; what is eaten, drunk, chewed, and tasted is properly digested; and the concentration attained through walking meditation is long-lasting. These, monks, are the five benefits of walking meditation.' Thus, venerable sir, was it spoken by the Blessed One.

Sumanasutta

The Discourse on Sumanā

Pu – sumanavagge [M 2.0152] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ sumanasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

But regarding the first Sumanā Sutta in the Sumanāvagga, friend, where, concerning whom, on what occasion, and how was it spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sumanaṃ rājakumāriṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Sumanā bhante rājakumārī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “idhassu bhante bhagavato dve sāvakā samasaddhā samasīlā samapaññā eko dāyako eko adāyako. Te kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjeyyuṃ. Devabhūtānaṃ pana nesaṃ bhante siyā viseso siyā nānākaraṇa”nti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “yo so sumane dāyako, so amhaṃ adāyakaṃ devabhūto samāno pañcahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhāti, dibbena āyunā dibbena vaṇṇena dibbena sukhena dibbena yasena dibbena ādhipateyyena. Yo so sumane dāyako, so amhaṃ adāyakaṃ devabhūto samāno imehi pañcahi ṭhānehi adhigaṇhātī”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, this was spoken at Sāvatthī concerning Princess Sumanā. Princess Sumanā, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: ‘Here, venerable sir, are two disciples of the Blessed One, equal in faith, equal in virtue, and equal in wisdom—one a giver, one a non-giver. Both, on the breakup of the body, after death, are reborn in a good destination, in a heavenly world. But, venerable sir, would there be any distinction or difference between them once they have become devas?’ On this matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: ‘Sumanā, that giver, having become a deva, surpasses the non-giver in five respects: with divine lifespan, divine beauty, divine happiness, divine fame, and divine sovereignty. Sumanā, that giver, having become a deva, surpasses the non-giver in these five respects.’ In this way, and so on, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

“Yathāpi [M 2.0156] cando vimalo, gacchaṃ ākāsadhātuyā;

Sabbe tārāgaṇe loke, ābhāya atirocati;

Tatheva sīlasampanno, saddho purisapuggalo;

Sabbe maccharino loke, cāgena atirocati”–

“Just as the spotless moon,

travelling through the sphere of space,

outshines all the hosts of stars

with its radiance in the world,

so too a person consummate in virtue,

a faithful individual,

outshines all the stingy

with generosity.”

Uggahasutta

The Discourse to Uggaha

Pu – tattheva āvuso tatiyaṃ uggahasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, where, concerning whom, in reference to what matter, and how was the third Discourse to Uggaha spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – tatiyaṃ bhante uggahasuttaṃ bhaddiye uggahaṃ meṇḍakanattāraṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Uggaho bhante meṇḍakanattā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “imā me bhante kumāriyo patikulāni gamissanti, ovadatu tāsaṃ bhante bhagavā, anusāsatu tāsaṃ bhante bhagavā, yaṃ tāsaṃ assa dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “tasmātiha kumāriyo evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ” yassa vo mātāpitaro bhattuno dassanti atthakāmā hitesino anukampakā anukampaṃ upādāya, tassa bhavissāma pubbuṭṭhāyino [M 2.0157] pacchānipātiniyo kiṃ kārapaṭissāviniyo manāpacāriniyo piyavādiniyo’ti. Evañhi vo kumāriyo sikkhitabba”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the third Discourse to Uggaha was spoken in Bhaddiya concerning Uggaha, Meṇḍaka’s grandson. Uggaha, Meṇḍaka’s grandson, venerable sir, said this to the Blessed One: ‘Venerable sir, these young women of mine will go to their husbands’ families. May the Blessed One advise them, may the Blessed One instruct them as to what will be for their long-term welfare and happiness.’ On that occasion, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: ‘Therefore, young women, you should train yourselves thus: “To whichever husband our parents give us—desiring our welfare, seeking our good, and compassionate, acting out of compassion—for him we will be ones who rise up early and go to bed late, be willing workers, conducting ourselves agreeably and speaking endearingly.” Thus, young women, you should train yourselves.’ In this way, and so on, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Sīhasenāpatisutta

The Discourse to Sīha the General

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0160] āvuso pañcakanipāte sumanavagge catutthaṃ sīhasenāpatisuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of the Fives, in the Sumanā Chapter, regarding the fourth discourse, the Sīhasenāpati Sutta: where was it spoken by the Blessed One, concerning whom, in reference to what matter, and how was it spoken?

Vi – vesāliyaṃ bhante sīhaṃ senāpatiṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Sīho bhante senāpati bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “sakkā nu kho bhante sandiṭṭhikaṃ dānaphalaṃ paññāpetu”nti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “sakkā sīha, dāyako sīha dānapati bahuno janassa piyo hoti manāpo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, this was spoken at Vesālī concerning General Sīha. Venerable sir, General Sīha said this to the Blessed One: ‘Is it possible, venerable sir, to point out a visible fruit of giving?’ On that occasion, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning thus: ‘It is possible, Sīha. A giver, Sīha, a benefactor, is dear and agreeable to many people.’ In this way, and so on, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Kāladānasutta

The Discourse on Seasonable Giving

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0164] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ kāladānasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the sixth Discourse on Seasonable Giving spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – chaṭṭhaṃ [M 2.0165] bhante kāladānasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave kāladānāni. Katamāni pañca, āgantukassa dānaṃ deti, gamikassa dānaṃ deti, gilānassa dānaṃ deti, dubbhikkhe dānaṃ deti, yāni tāni navasassāni navaphalāni, tāni paṭhamaṃ sīlavantesu patiṭṭhāpeti. Imāni kho bhikkhave pañca kāladānānī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the sixth Discourse on Seasonable Giving was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five seasonable gifts. What five? One gives a gift to a newcomer; one gives a gift to one who is departing; one gives a gift to one who is sick; one gives a gift in a time of famine; and whatever new grain and new fruits there are, one first establishes them for the virtuous. These, bhikkhus, are the five seasonable gifts.’ In this way, and so on, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Bhojanasutta

The Discourse on a Meal

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0166] āvuso sattamaṃ bhojanasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the seventh Discourse on a Meal spoken by the Blessed One right there?
