Vi – sattamaṃ bhante bhojanasuttaṃ “bhojanaṃ bhikkhave dadamāno dāyako paṭiggāhakānaṃ pañca ṭhānāni deti. Katamāni pañca, āyuṃ deti, vaṇṇaṃ deti, sukhaṃ deti, balaṃ deti, paṭibhānaṃ deti. Āyuṃ kho pana datvā āyussa bhāgī hoti dibbassa vā mānusassa vā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the seventh Discourse on a Meal was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, by giving a meal, a donor gives five things to the recipients. What five? He gives life, beauty, happiness, strength, and eloquence. Having given life, he becomes a partaker of life, whether divine or human.’ In this way, and so on, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Puttasutta

The Discourse on a Son

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0167] āvuso navamaṃ puttasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the ninth Discourse on a Son spoken by the Blessed One right there?

Vi – navamaṃ bhante puttasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave ṭhānāni sampassanto mābhāpitaro puttaṃ icchanti kule jāyamānaṃ. Katamāni pañca,

Venerable sir, the ninth Discourse on a Son was spoken thus: ‘Bhikkhus, considering five things, parents wish for a son to be born in their family. What five?

Bhato [M 2.0168] vā no bharissati, kiccaṃ vā no karissati, kulavaṃso ciraṃ ṭhassati, dāyajjaṃ paṭipajjissatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

‘“Having been supported by us, he will support us in turn; he will do our work for us; our family lineage will long endure; he will manage the inheritance.”’ In this way, and so on, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Nāradasutta

The Nārada Sutta

Pu – muṇḍarājavagge [M 2.0169] panāvuso dasamaṃ nāradasuttaṃ kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kena kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the King Muṇḍa Chapter, the tenth discourse, the Nārada Sutta: where was it spoken, concerning whom, in reference to what matter, by whom, and how was it spoken?

Vi – pāṭaliputte bhante muṇḍaṃ rājānaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Muṇḍassa bhante rañño bhaddā devī kālaṅkatā hoti piyā manāpā. So bhaddāya deviyā kālaṅkatāya piyāya manāpāya neva nhāyati na vilimpati na bhattaṃ bhuñjati, na kammantaṃ payojeti, rattindivaṃ bhaddāya deviyā sarīre ajjhomucchito. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “pañcimāni mahārāja alabbhanīyāni ṭhānāni samaṇena vā brāhmaṇena vā devena vā mārena vā brahmunā vā kenaci vā lokasmi”nti evamādinā bhante āyasmatā nāradattherena rañño pasenadissa kosalassa bhagavatā desitaniyāmena desitaṃ.

Venerable sir, this was spoken at Pāṭaliputta concerning King Muṇḍa. Venerable sir, King Muṇḍa’s dear and beloved queen, Bhaddā, had passed away. Since his dear and beloved Queen Bhaddā had passed away, he did not bathe, anoint himself, eat a meal, or attend to his duties; day and night, he remained infatuated with Queen Bhaddā’s corpse. On that occasion, venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Nārada taught King Muṇḍa in accordance with the principle that the Blessed One had proclaimed to King Pasenadi of Kosala, beginning: ‘Great king, these five things are unobtainable by any ascetic or brahmin, by a deva, Māra, or Brahmā, or by anyone in the world.’

Ko [M 2.0175] nāmo ayaṃ bhante dhammapariyāyo.

What, venerable sir, is the name of this Dhamma exposition?

Taggha bhante sokasallaharaṇo.

Indeed, venerable sir, it is the ‘Removal of the Arrow of Sorrow’.

Samayasutta

The Samaya Sutta

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0176] āvuso pañcakanipāte nīvaraṇavagge catutthaṃ samayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fives, in the Nīvaraṇavagga, how was the fourth Samaya Sutta spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – nīvaraṇavagge bhante catutthaṃ samayasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave asamayā padhānāya. Katame pañca, idha bhikkhave bhikkhu jiṇṇo hoti jarāyābhibhūto. Ayaṃ bhikkhave paṭhamo asamayo padhānāyā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Nīvaraṇavagga, the fourth Samaya Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, these five are unsuitable times for striving. What five? Here, monks, a monk is old, overcome by old age. This, monks, is the first unsuitable time for striving.” In this way, and so on, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Ṭhānasutta

The Ṭhāna Sutta

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0180] āvuso sattamaṃ ṭhānasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the seventh Ṭhāna Sutta spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vi – sattamaṃ bhante ṭhānasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave ṭhānāni abhiṇhaṃ paccavekkhitabbāni itthiyā vā purisena vā gahaṭṭhena vā pabbajitena vā. Katamāni pañca, jarādhammomhi jaraṃ anatītoti abhiṇhaṃ paccavekkhitabbaṃ itthiyā vā purisena vā gahaṭṭhena vā pabbajitena vā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the seventh Ṭhāna Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, these five things should be often reflected upon by a woman or a man, a householder or one gone forth. What are the five? ‘I am of a nature to age, I have not gone beyond aging’—this should be often reflected upon by a woman or a man, a householder or one gone forth.”

Dhammavihārīsutta

The Dhammavihārī Sutta

Pu – yodhājīvavagge [M 2.0185] panāvuso tatiyaṃ dhammavihārisuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the third Dhammavihārī Sutta in the Yodhājīvavagga spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – yodhājīvavagge bhante tatiyaṃ dhammavihārisuttaṃ “idha bhikkhu bhikkhu dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇāti suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ dutivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ, so tāya dhammapariyattiyā divasaṃ atināmeti, riñcati paṭisallānaṃ, nānuyuñjati ajjhattaṃ cetosamathaṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati bhikkhu bhikkhu pariyattibahulo no dhammavihārī”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Yodhājīvavagga, the third Dhammavihārī Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Here, a monk learns the Dhamma—discourses, mixed prose and verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, ‘as it was said’ discourses, birth stories, amazing phenomena, and questions and answers. He spends the day with that learning of the Dhamma, neglects seclusion, and does not apply himself to internal serenity of mind. This monk is called one who is much occupied with learning, not a dweller in the Dhamma.”

Dhammavihārī dhammavihārīti bhante vuccati, kittāvatā nu kho bhante bhikkhu dhammavihārī hoti.

“‘A dweller in the Dhamma, a dweller in the Dhamma,’ venerable sir, it is said. To what extent, venerable sir, is a monk a dweller in the Dhamma?”

Dutiyaanāgatabhayasutta

The Second Discourse on Future Dangers

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0188] āvuso pañcakanipāte yodhājīvavagge aṭṭhamaṃ dutiyaanāgatabhayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fives, in the Yodhājīva Vagga, how was the eighth, the Second Discourse on Future Dangers, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – yodhājīvavagge bhante aṭṭhamaṃ dutiyaanāgatabhayasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave anāgatabhayāni sampassamānena alameva bhikkhunā appamattena ātāpinā pahitattena viharituṃ appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāyā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Yodhājīva Vagga, the eighth, the Second Discourse on Future Dangers, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, seeing these five future dangers, it is quite enough for a monk to dwell heedful, ardent, and resolute for the attainment of the unattained, the achievement of the unachieved, the realization of the unrealized.”

Tatiyaanāgatabhayasutta

The Third Discourse on Future Dangers

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0193] āvuso navamaṃ tatiyaanāgatabhayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the ninth, the Third Discourse on Future Dangers, spoken by the Blessed One in that same place?

Vi – tattheva bhante navamaṃ katiyaanāgatabhayasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave anāgatabhayāni etarahi asamuppannāni āyatiṃ samuppajjissanti, tāni vo paṭibujjhitabbāni, paṭibujjhitvā ca tesaṃ pahānāya vāyamitabbaṃ. Katamāni pañca, bhavissanti bhikkhave bhikkhū anāgatamaddhānaṃ abhāvitakāyā abhāvitasīlā abhāvitacittā abhāvitapaññā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in that same place, the ninth, the Third Discourse on Future Dangers, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, these five future dangers, not arisen at present, will arise in the future. You should awaken to them and, having awakened to them, strive for their abandonment. What are the five? There will be monks in the future who are uncultivated in body, uncultivated in virtue, uncultivated in mind, uncultivated in wisdom.”

Catutthaanāgatabhayasutta

The Fourth Discourse on Future Dangers

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0199] āvuso pañcakanipāte yodhājīvavagge dasamaṃ catutthaanāgatabhayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fives, in the Yodhājīva Vagga, how was the tenth, the Fourth Discourse on Future Dangers, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – yodhājīvavagge bhante dasamaṃ catutthaanāgatabhayasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave anāgatabhayāni etarahi asamuppannāni āyatiṃ samuppajjissanti, tāni vo paṭibujjhitabbāni, paṭibujjhitvā ca tesaṃ pahānāya vāyamitabba”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Yodhājīva Vagga, the tenth, the Fourth Discourse on Future Dangers, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, these five future dangers, not arisen at present, will arise in the future. You should awaken to them and, having awakened to them, strive for their abandonment.”

Kakudhavagga

The Kakudha Vagga

Sīhasutta

The Lion Sutta

Pu – kakudhavagge [M 2.0202] āvuso navamaṃ sīhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the ninth Lion Sutta in the Kakudha Vagga spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – kakudhavagge bhante navamaṃ sīhasuttaṃ “sīho bhikkhave migarājā sāyanhasamayaṃ āsayā nikkhamati, āsayā nikkhamitvā vijambhati, vijambhitvā samantā catuddisaṃ anuviloketi, samantā catuddisaṃ anuviloketvā tikkhattuṃ sīhanādaṃ nadatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Kakudha Vagga, the ninth, the Lion Sutta, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, the lion, the king of beasts, emerges from his lair in the evening. Having emerged from his lair, he yawns, surveys the four directions all around, and then roars his lion’s roar three times.”

Andhakavindavagga

The Andhakavinda Vagga

Kulūpakasutta

The Kulūpaka Sutta

Pu – andhakavindavagge [M 2.0204] pana āvuso “paṭhamaṃ kulūpakasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Andhakavinda Vagga, how was the first Kulūpaka Sutta spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – andhakavindavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ kulūpakasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato kulūpako bhikkhu kulesu appiyo ca hoti amanāpo ca agaru ca abhāvanīyo cā”ti evamādinā bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Andhakavinda Vagga, the first Kulūpaka Sutta was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: “Monks, a monk who is a frequenter of families, endowed with five qualities, becomes unloved, disagreeable, unrespected, and unvenerated among those families.”

Andhakavindasutta

At Andhakavinda

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0206] āvuso catutthaṃ andhakavindasuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in that same chapter, where, concerning whom, and how was the fourth sutta, At Andhakavinda, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – magadhesu bhante andhakavinde āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ ārabbha “ye te ānanda bhikkhū navā acirapabbajitā adhunāgatā imaṃ dhammavinayaṃ, te vo ānanda bhikkhū pañcasu dhammesu samādapetabbā nivesetabbā patiṭṭhāpetabbā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, at Andhakavinda among the Magadhans, concerning the Venerable Ānanda, the Blessed One spoke beginning with these words: “Ānanda, those bhikkhus who are new, recently gone forth, newly come to this Dhamma and Discipline—you should encourage them, settle them, and establish them in five things.”

Gilānavagga

The Chapter on the Sick

Satisūpaṭṭhitasutta

Well-Established Mindfulness

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0208] āvuso pañcakanipāte gilānavagge dutiyaṃ satisūpaṭṭhitasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the Sutta on Well-Established Mindfulness, the second discourse in the Chapter on the Sick in the Book of the Fives of the Aṅguttara Nikāya, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – gilānavagge bhante dutiyaṃ satisūpaṭṭhitasuttaṃ “yo hi koci bhikkhave bhikkhu vā bhikkhunī vā pañca dhamme bhāveti, pañca dhamme bahulīkaroti, tassa dvinnaṃ phalānaṃ aññataraṃ phalaṃ pāṭikaṅkhaṃ ‘diṭṭheva dhamme aññā, sati vā upādisese anāgāmitā’. Katame pañca, idha bhikkhave bhikkhuno ajjhattaññeva satisūpaṭṭhitā hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the second Sutta on Well-Established Mindfulness in the Chapter on the Sick was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: “Bhikkhus, for any bhikkhu or bhikkhunī who develops and cultivates five things, one of two fruits is to be expected: either final knowledge in this very life or, if there is a remnant of clinging, non-returning. What five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu has mindfulness well established internally.”

Upaṭṭhākasutta

An Attendant

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0209] āvuso tatiyaṃ upaṭṭhākasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the third sutta, on an Attendant, spoken by the Blessed One in that same chapter?

Vi – tatiyaṃ [M 2.0210] bhante upaṭṭhākasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato gilāno dūpaṭṭhāko hoti. Katamehi pañcahi, asappāyakārī hoti, sappāye mattaṃ na jānāti, bhesajjaṃ nappaṭisevitā hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the third sutta, on an Attendant, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, a sick person endowed with five qualities is difficult to attend to. What five? He does what is unsuitable; he does not know the measure in what is suitable; and he does not take his medicine.’

Anāyussāsutta

Not Conducive to Long Life

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0212] āvuso pañcamaṃ anāyussāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the fifth sutta, on what is Not Conducive to Long Life, spoken by the Blessed One in that same chapter?

Vi – pañcamaṃ bhante anāyussāsuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave dhammā anāyussā. Katame pañca, asappāyakārī hoti, sappāye mattaṃ na jānāti, apariṇatabhojī ca hoti, akālacārī ca hoti, abrahmacārī ca. Ime kho bhikkhave pañca dhammā anāyussā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the fifth sutta, on what is Not Conducive to Long Life, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, these five things are not conducive to long life. What five? One does what is unsuitable; one does not know the measure in what is suitable; one eats undigested food; one wanders about at the wrong time; and one is unchaste. These, bhikkhus, are the five things that are not conducive to long life.’

Samaṇasukhasutta

A Recluse’s Happiness

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0213] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ samaṇasukhasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the eighth sutta, on a Recluse’s Happiness, spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vi – aṭṭhamaṃ bhante samaṇasukhasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave samaṇadukkhāni. Katamāni pañca, idha bhikkhave bhikkhu asantuṭṭho hoti itarītarena cīvarena, asantuṭṭho hoti itarītarena piṇḍapātena, asantuṭṭho hoti itarītarena senāsanena, asantuṭṭho hoti itarītarena gilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārena, anabhirato ca brahmacariyaṃ carati. Imāni kho bhikkhave pañca samaṇadukkhānī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the eighth sutta, on a Recluse’s Happiness, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five kinds of suffering for a recluse. What five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is discontented with any kind of robe, discontented with any kind of almsfood, discontented with any kind of lodging, discontented with any kind of medicinal requisites for the sick, and he leads the holy life without delight. These, bhikkhus, are the five kinds of suffering for a recluse.’

Byasanasutta

Misfortunes

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0215] āvuso dasamaṃ byasanasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, how was the tenth sutta, on Misfortunes, spoken by the Blessed One there?

Vi – dasamaṃ bhante byasanasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave byasanāni. Katamāni pañca, ñātibyasanaṃ bhogabyasanaṃ rogabyasanaṃ sīlabyasanaṃ diṭṭhibyasana”nti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, the tenth sutta, on Misfortunes, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five misfortunes. What five? The misfortune of relatives, the misfortune of wealth, the misfortune of illness, the misfortune of virtue, and the misfortune of view.’

Rājavagga

Kings

Patthanāsutta

Aspiration

Pu – rājavagge [M 2.0217] pana āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ patthanāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Now, friend, in the Chapter on Kings, how was the sixth sutta, on Aspiration, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – rājavagge bhante chaṭṭhaṃ patthanāsuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave aṅgehi samannāgato rañño khattiyassa muddhāvasittassa jeṭṭho putto oparajjaṃ patthetī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Kings, the sixth sutta, on Aspiration, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, endowed with five factors, the eldest son of an anointed khattiya king aspires to the viceroyalty.’

Sappurisadānasutta

A Good Person’s Gifts

Pu – tikaṇḍakīvagge [M 2.0221] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ sappurisadānasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Chapter at Tikaṇḍakī, how was the eighth sutta, on a Good Person’s Gifts, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tikaṇḍakīvagge bhante aṭṭhamaṃ sappurisadānasuttaṃ “pañcimāni bhikkhave sappurisadānāni. Katamāni pañca, saddhāya dānaṃ deti, sakkaccaṃ dānaṃ deti, kālena dānaṃ deti, anuggahitacitto dānaṃ deti, attānañca parañca anupahacca dānaṃ detī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter at Tikaṇḍakī, the eighth sutta, on a Good Person’s Gifts, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five gifts of a good person. What five? One gives a gift with faith; one gives a gift respectfully; one gives a gift at the proper time; one gives a gift with a compassionate heart; and one gives a gift without harming oneself or others.’

Saddhammavagga

The True Dhamma

Paṭhamasammattaniyāmasutta

The Fixed Course of Rightness (1)

Pu – saddhammavagge [M 2.0224] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ sammattaniyāmasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Now, friend, in the Chapter on the True Dhamma, how was the first sutta, The Fixed Course of Rightness, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – saddhammavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ sammattaniyāmasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato sunakkhopi saddhammaṃ abhabbo niyāmaṃ okkamituṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ. Katamehi pañcahi, kathaṃ paribhoti, kathikaṃ paribhoti, attānaṃ paribhotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the True Dhamma, the first sutta, The Fixed Course of Rightness, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, endowed with five qualities, even Sunakkhatta is incapable of entering the fixed course of rightness in wholesome states. What five? One disparages the teaching, one disparages the speaker, and one disparages oneself.’

Paṭhamasaddhammasammosasutta

The First Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0226] āvuso catutthaṃ paṭhamasaddhammasammosasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, right there, how was the fourth discourse, the First Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante catutthaṃ paṭhamasaddhammasammosasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave dhammā saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattanti. Katame pañca, idha bhikkhave bhikkhū na sakkaccaṃ dhammaṃ suṇanti, na sakkaccaṃ dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇanti, na sakkaccaṃ dhammaṃ dhārenti, na sakkaccaṃ dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhanti, na sakkaccaṃ atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammaṃ paṭipajjanti. Ime kho bhikkhave pañca dhammā saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattantī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, right there the fourth discourse, the First Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, these five things lead to the confusion and disappearance of the true Dhamma. What five? Here, bhikkhus, they do not listen to the Dhamma attentively; they do not learn the Dhamma attentively; they do not retain the Dhamma attentively; they do not attentively examine the meaning of the teachings they have retained; and having understood the meaning and the teaching, they do not practice attentively in accordance with the Dhamma. These, bhikkhus, are the five things that lead to the confusion and disappearance of the true Dhamma.’

Dutiyasaddhammasammosasutta

The Second Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0228] āvuso pañcamaṃ dutiyasaddhammasammosasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, right there, how was the fifth discourse, the Second Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante pañcamaṃ dutiyasaddhammasammosasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave dhammā saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattanti. Katame pañca, idha bhikkhave bhikkhū dhammaṃ na pariyāpuṇanti suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. Ayaṃ bhikkhave paṭhamo dhammo saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, right there the fifth discourse, the Second Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with these words: ‘Bhikkhus, these five things lead to the confusion and disappearance of the true Dhamma. What five? Here, bhikkhus, they do not learn the Dhamma: discourses, songs in prose and verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, quotations, birth stories, amazing accounts, and question-and-answer texts. This, bhikkhus, is the first thing that leads to the confusion and disappearance of the true Dhamma.’

Tatiyasaddhammasammosasutta

The Third Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0231] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ tatiyasaddhammasammosasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, right there, how was the sixth discourse, the Third Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante chaṭṭhaṃ tatiyasaddhammasammosasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave dhammā saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattanti. Katame pañca, idha bhikkhave bhikkhū duggahitaṃ suttantaṃ pariyāpuṇanti dunnikkhittahi padabyañjanehi. Dunnikkhittassa bhikkhave padabyañjanassa atthopi dunnayo hoti. Ayaṃ bhikkhave paṭhamo dhammo saddhammassa sammosāya antaradhānāya saṃvattatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, right there the sixth discourse, the Third Discourse on the Confusion of the True Dhamma, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with the words: ‘Bhikkhus, these five things lead to the confusion and disappearance of the true Dhamma. What five? Here, bhikkhus, they learn discourses that have been wrongly grasped, with poorly arranged words and phrases. When the words and phrases are poorly arranged, bhikkhus, the meaning is also wrongly inferred. This, bhikkhus, is the first thing that leads to the confusion and disappearance of the true Dhamma.’

Dukkatāsutta

The Discourse on Unpleasant Talk

Pu – aṅguttaranikāye [M 2.0237] āvuso pañcakanipāte saddhammavagge sattamaṃ dukkathāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in the Aṅguttara Nikāya, in the Book of Fives, in the Chapter on the True Dhamma, how was the seventh discourse, the Discourse on Unpleasant Talk, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – saddhammavagge bhante sattamaṃ dukkathāsuttaṃ “pañcannaṃ bhikkhave puggalānaṃ kathā dukkathā puggale puggalaṃ upanidhāya. Katamesaṃ pañcannaṃ, assaddhassa bhikkhave saddhākathā dukkathā, dussīlassa sīlakathā dukkathā, appassutassa bāhusaccakathā dukkathā, maccharissa cāgakathā dukkathā, duppaññassa paññākathā dukkathā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on the True Dhamma, the seventh discourse, the Discourse on Unpleasant Talk, was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with the words: ‘Bhikkhus, for five kinds of persons, talk is unpleasant. What five? For a person without faith, bhikkhus, talk about faith is unpleasant; for an immoral person, talk about virtue is unpleasant; for a person with little learning, talk about great learning is unpleasant; for a stingy person, talk about generosity is unpleasant; for a person with no wisdom, talk about wisdom is unpleasant.’

Udāyīsutta

The Udāyī Sutta

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0240] āvuso navamaṃ udāyisuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in that same section, where, concerning whom, on what occasion, and how was the ninth discourse, the Udāyī Sutta, spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – kosambiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ udāyiṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Āyasmā bhante udāyī mahatiyā gīhiparisāya parivuto [M 2.0241] dhammaṃ desento nisinno hoti. Addasā kho āyasmā ānando āyasmantaṃ udāyiṃ mahatiyā gihiparisāya parivutaṃ dhammaṃ desentaṃ nisinnaṃ. Disvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami, upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “āyasmā bhante udāyī mahatiyā gihiparisāya parivuto dhammaṃ desetī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “na kho ānanda sukaraṃ paresaṃ dhammaṃ desetuṃ, paresaṃ ānanda dhammaṃ desentena pañca dhamme ajjhattaṃ upaṭṭhāpetvā paresaṃ dhammo desetabbo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, it was spoken at Kosambī concerning the Venerable Udāyī. The Venerable Udāyī was sitting, teaching the Dhamma, surrounded by a large assembly of householders. The Venerable Ānanda saw the Venerable Udāyī sitting and teaching the Dhamma, surrounded by a large assembly of householders. Having seen him, he went to the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the Venerable Ānanda said to the Blessed One: ‘Venerable sir, the Venerable Udāyī is teaching the Dhamma surrounded by a large assembly of householders.’ On that occasion, Venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning with the words: ‘It is not easy, Ānanda, to teach the Dhamma to others. Ānanda, one who would teach the Dhamma to others should do so after having established five things in himself.’

Āghātavagga

The Chapter on Ill Will

Paṭhamaāghātapaṭivinayasutta

The First Discourse on Removing Resentment

Pu – āghātavagge [M 2.0243] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ āghātapaṭivinayasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Now, friend, in the Chapter on Ill Will, how was the first discourse on removing resentment spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – āghātavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ āghātapaṭivinayasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave āghātapaṭivinayā, yattha bhikkhuno uppanno āghāto sabbaso paṭivinetabbo. Katame pañca, yasmiṃ bhikkhave puggale āghāto jāyetha, mettā tasmiṃ puggale bhāvetabbā, evaṃ tasmiṃ puggale āghāto paṭivinetabbo”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Ill Will, the first discourse on removing resentment was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with the words: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five ways of removing resentment, by which a bhikkhu should completely remove any resentment that has arisen. What five? Bhikkhus, toward whatever person resentment has arisen, one should develop loving-kindness for that person. In this way, resentment for that person is to be removed.’

Upāsakavagga

The Chapter on Lay Followers

Caṇḍālasutta

The Sutta on the Outcaste

Pu – upāsakavagge [M 2.0245] pana āvuso pañcamaṃ caṇḍālasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Now, friend, in the Chapter on Lay Followers, how was the fifth Sutta on the Outcaste spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – upāsakavagge bhante pañcamaṃ caṇḍālasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato upāsako upāsakacaṇḍālo ca [M 2.0246] hoti upāsakamalañca upāsakapatikuṭṭho cā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Lay Followers, the fifth Sutta on the Outcaste was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with the words: ‘Bhikkhus, a lay follower who possesses five qualities is an outcaste among lay followers, a stain among lay followers, and a pariah among lay followers.’

Pītisutta

The Sutta on Rapture

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0247] āvuso chaṭṭhaṃ pītisuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in that same chapter, where, in reference to whom, and how was the sixth Sutta on Rapture spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – chaṭṭhaṃ bhante pītisuttaṃ sāvatthiyaṃ anāthapiṇḍikaṃ gahapatiṃ ārabbha “tumhe kho gahapati bhikkhusaṅghaṃ paccupaṭṭhitā cīvara piṇḍapāta senāsana gilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārena[M 2.0248]. Na kho gahapati tāvatakeneva tuṭṭhi karaṇīyā “mayaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ paccupaṭṭhitā cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilāna paccaya bhesajjaparikkhārenā”ti. Tasmātiha gahapati evaṃ sikkhitabbaṃ “kindi mayaṃ kālena kālaṃ pavivekaṃ pītiṃ upasampajja vihareyyāmā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, the sixth Sutta on Rapture was spoken by the Blessed One at Sāvatthī in reference to the householder Anāthapiṇḍika, beginning with the words: ‘Householder, you have been attending to the Saṅgha of bhikkhus with robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. But, householder, you should not be satisfied with just that, thinking: “We have been attending to the Saṅgha of bhikkhus with robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick.” Therefore, householder, you should train thus: “How might we, from time to time, enter and dwell in the rapture of seclusion?”’

Vaṇijjāsutta

The Sutta on Trades

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0251] āvuso sattamaṃ vaṇijjāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in that same chapter, how was the seventh Sutta on Trades spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante sattamaṃ vaṇijjāsuttaṃ “pañcimā bhikkhave vaṇijjā upāsakena akaraṇīyā. Katamā pañca, satthavaṇijjā sattavaṇijjā maṃsavaṇijjā majjavaṇijjā visavaṇijjā. Imā kho bhikkhave pañca vaṇijjā upāsakena akaraṇīyā”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in that same chapter, the seventh Sutta on Trades was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five kinds of trade that a lay follower should not engage in. What five? Trade in weapons, trade in living beings, trade in meat, trade in intoxicants, and trade in poison. These, bhikkhus, are the five kinds of trade that a lay follower should not engage in.’ Thus, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Gavesīsutta

The Sutta on Gavesī

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0252] āvuso dasamaṃ gavesīsuttaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ kathañca bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in that same chapter, where, in reference to whom, on what occasion, and how was the tenth Sutta on Gavesī spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – dasamaṃ bhante gavesīsuttaṃ kosalesu āyasmantaṃ ānandattheraṃ dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ ārabbha bhāsitaṃ. Āyasmā bhante ānandatthero dhammabhaṇḍāgāriko bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “ko nu kho bhante hetu ko paccayo bhagavato sitassa pātukammāya, na akāraṇena tathāgatā sitaṃ pātukarontī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “bhūtapubbaṃ ānanda imasmiṃ padese nagaraṃ ahosi [M 2.0253] iddhañceva phītañca bahujanaṃ ākiṇṇamanussaṃ, taṃ kho panānanda nagaraṃ kassapo bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho upanissāya vihāsī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, the tenth Sutta on Gavesī was spoken in the Kosalan country in reference to the Venerable Elder Ānanda, the treasurer of the Dhamma. Venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Ānanda, the treasurer of the Dhamma, said this to the Blessed One: ‘What, venerable sir, is the cause, what is the reason for the Blessed One to smile? The Tathāgatas do not smile without a reason.’ On that occasion, venerable sir, the Blessed One spoke beginning with the words: ‘Formerly, Ānanda, in this very place there was a city that was successful, prosperous, populous, and crowded with people. The Blessed One Kassapa, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, dwelt depending on that city, Ānanda.’

Vācāsutta

The Sutta on Speech

Pu – brāhmaṇavagge [M 2.0260] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ vācāsuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in the Chapter on Brahmins, how was the eighth Sutta on Speech spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – brāhmaṇavagge bhante aṭṭhamaṃ vācāsuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgatā vācā subhāsitā hoti no dubbhāsitā, anavajjā ca ananuvajjā ca viññūnaṃ. Katamehi pañcahi, kālena bhāsitā hoti, saccā ca bhāsitā hoti, saṇhā ca bhāsitā hoti, atthasaṃhitā ca bhāsitā hoti, mettācittena ca bhāsitā hoti, imehi kho bhikkhave pañcahi aṅgehi samannāgatā vācā subhāsitā hoti no dubbhāsitā, anavajjā ca ananuvajjā ca viññūna”nti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Vi – Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Brahmins, the eighth Sutta on Speech was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Bhikkhus, speech endowed with five factors is well-spoken, not ill-spoken; it is blameless and not censured by the wise. What five? It is spoken at the right time; it is spoken in truth; it is spoken gently; it is spoken beneficially; and it is spoken with a mind of loving-kindness. Bhikkhus, speech endowed with these five factors is well-spoken, not ill-spoken; it is blameless and not censured by the wise.’ Thus, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Kulasutta

The Sutta on Families

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0261] āvuso navamaṃ kulasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Pu – Friend, in that same chapter, how was the ninth Sutta on Families spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante navamaṃ kulasuttaṃ “yaṃ bhikkhave sīlavanto pabbajitā kulaṃ upasaṅkamanti, tattha manussā pañcahi ṭhānehi bahuṃ puññaṃ pasavanti. Katamehi pañcahi, yasmiṃ bhikkhave samaye sīlavante pabbajite kulaṃ upasaṅkamante manussā disvā cittāni pasādenti, saggasaṃvattanikaṃ bhikkhave taṃ kulaṃ tasmiṃ samaye paṭipadaṃ paṭipannaṃ hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in that same chapter, the ninth Sutta on Families was spoken by the Blessed One beginning with the words: ‘Bhikkhus, when virtuous ascetics approach a family, the people there generate much merit on five occasions. What five? Bhikkhus, on the occasion when people see virtuous ascetics approaching their family, their minds become confident. Bhikkhus, at that time that family is practicing the way that leads to heaven.’

Kimilavagga

The Chapter on Kimila

Dhammassavanasutta

The Sutta on Hearing the Dhamma

Pu – kimilavagge [M 2.0263] pana āvuso dutiyaṃ dhammassavanasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Chapter on Kimila, how was the second Sutta on Hearing the Dhamma spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – kimilavagge bhante dutiyaṃ dhammassavanasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave ānisaṃsā dhammassavane. Katame pañca, assutaṃ suṇāti, sutaṃ pariyodāpeti, kaṅkhaṃ vitarati, diṭṭhiṃ ujuṃ karoti, cittamassa pasīdati, ime kho bhikkhave pañca ānisaṃsā dhammassavane”ti evaṃ kho bhante bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Kimila, the second Sutta on Hearing the Dhamma was spoken by the Blessed One thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five benefits in hearing the Dhamma. What five? One hears what one has not heard, one clarifies what one has heard, one overcomes doubt, one straightens one’s view, and one’s mind becomes confident. These, bhikkhus, are the five benefits in hearing the Dhamma.’ Thus, venerable sir, it was spoken by the Blessed One.

Akkosakavagga

The Chapter on Abuse

Akkhantisutta

The Sutta on Impatience

Pu – akkosakavagge [M 2.0264] pana āvuso pañcamaṃ akkhantisuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Chapter on Abuse, how was the fifth Sutta on Impatience spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – akkosakavagge bhante pañcamaṃ akkhantisuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave ādīnavā akkhantiyā. Katame pañca, bahuno janassa [M 2.0265] appiyo hoti amanāpo, verabahulo ca hoti, vajjabahulo ca, sammūḷho kālaṃ karoti, kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjatī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Abuse, the fifth Sutta on Impatience was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five dangers in impatience. What five? One is unloved and disagreeable to many people; one is full of enmity; one is full of blame; one dies bewildered; and with the breakup of the body, after death, one is reborn in a state of deprivation, a bad destination, the lower world, in hell.’

Apāsādikasutta

The Sutta on the Uninspiring

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0266] āvuso aṭṭhamaṃ apāsādikasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in that same chapter, how was the eighth Sutta on the Uninspiring spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante aṭṭhamaṃ apāsādikasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave ādīnavā apāsādike. Katame pañca, appasannā nappasīdanti, pasannānañca ekaccānaṃ aññathattaṃ hoti, satthusāsanaṃ akataṃ hoti, pacchimā janatā diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjati, cittamassa nappasīdati. Ime kho bhikkhave pañca ādīnavā apāsādike”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in that same chapter, the eighth Sutta on the Uninspiring was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five dangers in one who is uninspiring. What five? The unconvinced do not become convinced, and some who are convinced are changed; the Teacher’s instruction is not followed; the next generation follows that example; and one's own mind does not become serene. These, bhikkhus, are the five dangers in one who is uninspiring.’

Āvāsikavagga

The Chapter on Residents

Āvāsikasutta

The Sutta on the Resident

Pu – āvāsikavagge [M 2.0268] panāvuso paṭhamaṃ āvāsikasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Chapter on Residents, how was the first Sutta on the Resident spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – āvāsikavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ āvāsikasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato āvāsiko bhikkhu abhāvanīyo hoti. Katamehi pañcahi, na ākappasampanno hoti na vattasampanno, na bahussuto hoti na sutadharo, na paṭisallekhitā hoti na paṭisallānārāmo, na kalyāṇavāco hoti na kalyāṇavākkaraṇo, duppañño hoti jaḷo eḷamūgo. Imehi kho bhikkhave pañcahi dhammehi samannāgato āvāsiko bhikkhu abhāvanīyo hotī”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Residents, the first Sutta on the Resident was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, a resident bhikkhu endowed with five qualities is not to be esteemed. What five? He is not accomplished in deportment and duties; he is not learned and does not retain what he has learned; he is not given to reflection and does not delight in seclusion; he is not of good speech and fine elocution; he is unwise, dull, and slow-witted. A resident bhikkhu endowed with these five qualities is not to be esteemed.’

Avaṇṇārahasutta

The Sutta on One Worthy of Dispraise

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0270] āvuso sattamaṃ avaṇṇārahasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in that same chapter, how was the seventh Sutta on One Worthy of Dispraise spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva bhante sattamaṃ avaṇṇārahasuttaṃ “pañcahi bhikkhave dhammehi samannāgato āvāsiko bhikkhu yathābhataṃ nikkhitto evaṃ niraye. Katamehi pañcahi, ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, āvāsamaccharī hoti āvāsapaligedhī, kulamaccharī hoti kulapaligedhi, saddhādeyyaṃ vinipāteti. Imehi kho bhikkhave pañcahi dhammehi samannāgato āvāsiko [M 2.0271] bhikkhu yathābhataṃ nikkhitto evaṃ niraye”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in that same chapter, the seventh Sutta on One Worthy of Dispraise was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, a resident bhikkhu endowed with five qualities is deposited in hell as if carried there. What five? Without investigating and scrutinizing, he praises one who deserves dispraise; without investigating and scrutinizing, he dispraises one who deserves praise; he is stingy and greedy regarding lodgings; he is stingy and greedy regarding families; and he squanders the offerings of the faithful. A resident bhikkhu endowed with these five qualities is deposited in hell as if carried there.’

Duccaritavagga

The Chapter on Misconduct

Paṭhamaduccaritasutta

The First Sutta on Misconduct

Pu – duccaritavagge [M 2.0273] pana āvuso paṭhamaṃ duccaritasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in the Chapter on Misconduct, how was the first Sutta on Misconduct spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – duccaritavagge bhante paṭhamaṃ duccaritasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave ādīnavā duccarite. Katame pañca, attāpi attānaṃ upavadati, anuvicca viññū garahanti, pāpako kittisaddo abbhuggacchati, sammūḷho kālaṃ karoti, kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjati. Ime kho bhikkhave pañca ādīnavā duccarite”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in the Chapter on Misconduct, the first Sutta on Misconduct was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five dangers in misconduct. What five? One’s own self reproaches one; having investigated, the wise censure one; an evil report about one spreads; one dies bewildered; and with the breakup of the body, after death, one is reborn in a state of deprivation, a bad destination, the lower world, in hell. These, bhikkhus, are the five dangers in misconduct.’

Sivathikasutta

The Sutta on the Charnel Ground

Pu – tattheva [M 2.0274] āvuso navamaṃ sivathikasuttaṃ bhagavatā kathaṃ bhāsitaṃ.

Friend, in that same chapter, how was the ninth Sutta on the Charnel Ground spoken by the Blessed One?

Vi – tattheva [M 2.0275] bhante navamaṃ sivathikasuttaṃ “pañcime bhikkhave ādīnavā sivathikāya. Katame pañca, asuci, duggandhā, sappaṭibhayā, vāḷānaṃ amanussānaṃ āvāso, bahuno janassa ārodanā. Ime kho bhikkhave pañca ādīnavā sivathikāyā”ti evamādinā bhagavatā bhāsitaṃ.

Venerable sir, in that same chapter, the ninth Sutta on the Charnel Ground was spoken by the Blessed One beginning thus: ‘Bhikkhus, there are these five dangers of a charnel ground. What five? It is unclean, foul-smelling, and frightening; it is a habitat for beasts of prey and non-human beings; and it is a place of weeping for many people. These, bhikkhus, are the five dangers of a charnel ground.’
